### 1. The Billing Architecture & Workspace Topology

To avoid confusing users with multiple dashboards while ensuring Cloudflare usage variance doesn't erode margins, AVOS uses a **Hybrid Subscription + Metered Gas Tank** billing model.

```
                                  USER BILLING & PHONE TOPOLOGY
                                                │
                                                ▼
                         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                         │      AVOS SHARED WHATSAPP BUSINESS NUMBER    │
                         │      (+1-800-AVOS / Global Edge Routing)     │
                         └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────┘
                                                │ (Identified by User Phone Number)
                                                ▼
                         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                         │            PERSONAL MAIN SPACE (Root)        │
                         │     👤 EXECUTIVE PERSONAL CHIEF OF STAFF     │
                         │     • Base Account • Global Identity         │
                         └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────┘
                                                │
                         ┌──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┐
                         ▼ (Delegates internal tasks)                  ▼ (Purchased business blueprints)
          ┌─────────────────────────────┐               ┌─────────────────────────────┐
          │   SUBSPACE A: Legal Studio  │               │ SUBSPACE B: Logistics Fleet │
          │   👤 Legal Specialist Bot   │               │ 👤 Logistics Dispatcher Bot │
          │   (Internal private work)   │               │ (Internal private work)     │
          └─────────────────────────────┘               └─────────────────────────────┘
                                                                       │
                                                                       ▼ (Optional Paid Add-On)
                                                        ┌─────────────────────────────┐
                                                        │ PUBLIC CUSTOMER-FACING BOT  │
                                                        │ Dedicated Twilio/Meta Number│
                                                        │ (Separate public project)   │
                                                        └─────────────────────────────┘
```

#### A. The Phone Topology (Personal vs. Subspaces vs. Public Customer Bots)
* **The Single-Number Personal Hub:** When a user registers via WhatsApp, their phone number generates their **Personal Main Space** with an **Executive Personal Assistant**.
* **Subspace Delegation (Internal):** When the user buys a business template (e.g., "Miami Real Estate Wholesaler"), it installs as an **attached business project/space**. The user chats with their single Personal Assistant, which coordinates behind the scenes:
  * User texts: *"Audit the lease agreement from yesterday and send an update to the driver."*
  * Main Assistant routes contract tasks to the Legal Subspace Bot, dispatches cargo updates to the Logistics Subspace Bot, and summaries results back to the user's single chat thread.
* **Customer-Facing Bots (Strict Separation):** Public customer auto-reply bots **never share the owner's private conversation thread**. 
  * Customers text a **Dedicated Virtual Number** (provisioned via WhatsApp Cloud API / Twilio / OpenClaw).
  * This runs in an isolated public project container. Customer chats are aggregated into an in-app CRM inbox, keeping private managerial discussions separate from public client messaging.

#### B. The Simplified Billing Model: "The Base Seat + Edge Gas Tank"
To handle users who use very little compute versus users who run heavy workflows:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE 3-TIER SIMPLE BILLING TIERS                           │
├──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Tier                 │ Base Price           │ What is Included                         │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Consumer**         │ **$6.99 / month**    │ 1 Personal Main Space, 1 Executive Bot,  │
│                      │                      │ Shared WhatsApp number, 500 Gas Credits. │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Pro / Solo SMB**   │ **$29 / month**      │ 1 Main Space + 3 Subspace Projects,      │
│                      │                      │ 2,500 Gas Credits, Canvas Desktop UI.    │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Enterprise**       │ **$199 / month**     │ Unlimited Subspaces, Dedicated Cloudflare│
│                      │                      │ DLS EU/US Region, 20,000 Gas Credits.    │
└──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┘
```
* **The "Gas Tank" Mechanism:** Every plan includes pre-loaded platform credits.
  * Routine Cloudflare Worker requests cost **$0.0001** (virtually free).
  * Fast models (Gemini Flash / Cloudflare Llama 8B) cost **0.1 credits**.
  * Deep reasoning or heavy tools draw directly from the user's balance.
  * If a user does nothing all month, AVOS earns the base subscription at near-100% margin. If they use heavy compute, the gas tank auto-refills, insulating AVOS from compute cost overruns.
* **Dedicated Number Add-on:** **$15/month per dedicated WhatsApp number** (covers carrier provisioning, Meta WhatsApp message fees, and routing costs).

---

### 2. Marketplace Pricing & Unit Economics Protection

A major risk in vertical marketplaces is mispricing by third-party builders: an FDE might sell a template for $50 that incurs heavy cloud infrastructure costs, or sell an overpriced template that harms consumer trust.

#### The Rule: Bundle Month 1 into the Upfront Price
To protect cash flow and simplify billing, the upfront template price **automatically bundles the first month of the base platform subscription**:

```
                              UPFRONT PURCHASE CHECKOUT BREAKDOWN
                                               │
               Customer Purchases FDE Template: $55.00 Upfront Total
                                               │
                        ┌──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┐
                        ▼                                             ▼
           [AVOS Platform Base Cut]                       [FDE Creator Payout]
           $20.00 (Covers Month 1 SaaS)                  $35.00 (Template Margin)
                                                                      +
                                                         100% of Upfront Custom Setup
                                                         (e.g., $2,500 consulting fee)
```

#### How AVOS Enforces Infrastructure Minimums (`resource_class` Quotas):
To prevent an FDE from pricing a compute-heavy template at a baseline that drains platform margins, the `blueprint.json` schema mandates a **Resource Class Rating**:

```json
// blueprint.json
{
  "name": "Maritime Automated EDI Customs Terminal",
  "resource_class": "heavy_compute", 
  "minimum_monthly_subscription": 150.00,
  "one_time_template_price": 500.00,
  "requires_dedicated_number": true
}
```
* **The Compiler Gate:** If an FDE submits a template containing heavy OCR models, continuous dynamic workflows, and vector indexes, the AVOS compiler automatically enforces a **minimum monthly subscription floor** (e.g., $150/mo minimum). 
* The FDE cannot lower the recurring subscription below that floor, ensuring AVOS's Cloudflare infrastructure and model margins are mathematically secured.

---

### 3. Deep Dive into Non-Legal Verticals

Enterprise AI extends far beyond legal redlining. Examining other domains highlights where specialized platforms succeed and what they charge:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              NON-LEGAL VERTICAL AI LANDSCAPE & ECONOMICS                               │
├─────────────────────┬───────────────────┬───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┤
│ Vertical Domain     │ Leading Platforms │ System Foundation         │ Pricing & Business Model         │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
│ **1. Film & Video** │ **InterPositive** │ Proprietary models trained│ **$50,000 – $250,000 / movie**   │
│ (VFX, Dailies,      │ (Ben Affleck,     │ on a production's own raw │ or enterprise studio license     │
│ Relighting, Color)  │ acquired by       │ camera dailies; fine-tuned│ (Acquired by Netflix             │
│                     │ Netflix for $587M)│ for continuity & relighting│ for $587M cash)                 │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
│ **2. Healthcare**   │ **Abridge**,      │ Custom ambient clinical   │ **$150 – $350 / clinician / mo** │
│ (Ambient Clinical   │ **Ambience**,     │ speech-to-text models     │ (Annual contracts bundled        │
│ Scribing, EHR Notes)│ **Nabla**         │ mapped to Epic/Cerner EHR │ with hospital health systems)    │

│ (Architecture & MEP │ **TestFit**,      │ geometric & electrical    │ or tiered project usage fee      │

├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤

│ (Logistics & Load   │ **Kargo**,        │ coupled to EDI gateways & │ or $1,000 – $3,000/mo            │
│ Dispatch)           │ **Transfix AI**   │ WhatsApp driver tracking  │ platform subscription            │
└─────────────────────┴───────────────────┴───────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### What These Verticals Teach Us:
1. **Film/Media (InterPositive):** Generative text is useless on a movie set. Filmmakers do not want text-to-video prompt tools like Sora; they need **deeply specialized models trained on that day's camera footage** to fix continuity, adjust lighting, and match color grading.
2. **Healthcare (Abridge):** Generalist models generate chatty responses that fail compliance. Clinical tools use specialized models trained to convert audio into structured medical codes (ICD-10, CPT) that integrate into electronic health records.
3. **Construction/AEC (TestFit):** Text models cannot calculate structural loads or floor plan geometry. The AI layer functions as a parametric solver paired with a visualization engine.

---

### 4. Deeply Specialized Models (The "Ben Affleck / InterPositive" Challenge)

Ben Affleck’s startup **InterPositive** sold to Netflix for **$587 million** because it avoided a basic trap: **it did not try to prompt generalist models to make movies.** Instead, it trained specialized computer vision and neural rendering models on a specific production’s raw 8K camera dailies to handle lighting adjustments and continuity fixes in post-production.

What happens when an FDE or company on AVOS needs a **deeply specialized model** (e.g., custom visual VFX models, seismic geological models, proprietary medical image classifiers) rather than standard LLMs?

```
                    SPECIALIZED MODEL PIPELINE ON AVOS
                                    │
                                    ▼
       ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
       │ 1. CLIENT COMMISSION / TRAINING PHASE (Billed Upfront)      │
       │ • Client pays FDE: $5,000–$25,000 Custom Training Retainer. │
       │ • Client pays Compute: $500–$2,000 GPU Training Run (Rented)│
       └────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┘
                                    │
                                    ▼
       ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
       │ 2. MODEL ARTIFACT PACKAGING (The Model Container)           │
       │ • Model weights (.safetensors, ONNX, or GGUF) stored in R2. │
       │ • Registered in `blueprint.json` under `specialized_models`.│
       └────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┘
                                    │
                                    ▼
       ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
       │ 3. PRODUCTION INFERENCE EXECUTION                           │
       │ • Lightweight Models (<3B): Run serverless on CF Workers AI.│
       │ • Heavy Specialized Models (Diffusion/VFX):                 │
       │   Routed to On-Prem Client GPU via `LocalTunnelDO`          │
       │   OR Serverless GPU Clusters (RunPod, Baseten, Modal).      │
       └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### How the FDE Builds and Prices This:
1. **Who Pays for the Model Training?**
   * **The Client Pays the Training Run:** An FDE does not train custom models out of their own pocket. The client pays an **Upfront Custom Model Setup Fee** ($5,000–$25,000) that includes rented GPU training compute on Lambda Labs or RunPod.
2. **What if the FDE Wants to Sell a Pre-Trained Specialized Vertical on the Marketplace?**
   * If an FDE invests their own capital to train a specialized model (e.g., an automated "Film Dailies Relighting Model" or a "Dental Orthodontic Alignment Model"), they package it into AVOS as a **Proprietary Model Extension**.
   * **How Inference is Priced:** Instead of standard token billing, the FDE configures **Per-Unit Consumption Billing**:
     * **Film Model:** **$0.05 per processed video frame**.
     * **Dental Model:** **$2.50 per 3D orthodontic mesh alignment**.
     * **Customs OCR:** **$0.50 per parsed Bill of Lading**.
3. **The Revenue Split on Specialized Models:**
   * **60% to FDE:** Compensates the developer for training the specialized model.
   * **40% to AVOS:** Covers the edge routing, Cloudflare storage, and platform margin.

---

### Summary of the Operational Model

1. **Simple User Billing:** Users pay a flat **$6.99 (Personal) or $29 (Business)** subscription with an included gas tank. Subspaces organize multiple projects inside a single account.
2. **Separation of Concerns:** Personal assistants handle internal work across linked subspaces; public customer-facing bots operate on isolated, dedicated numbers.
3. **Protection Against Underpricing:** Upfront template sales bundle Month 1 SaaS, and the `resource_class` engine enforces a minimum subscription price on compute-heavy verticals.
4. **Deep Specialized Model Support:** For production domains like film (InterPositive) or medicine, FDEs deploy custom-trained models with **per-unit/per-frame pricing**, allowing AVOS to handle specialized non-LLM pipelines without bearing the training costs.





UI:


This is a breakthrough in simplifying your platform architecture: **Option 2 (Chat + Right Canvas + GenUI) is so versatile that it can serve as the universal workhorse for 80% of all verticals.** 

If you treat **`ProjectKind`** as the foundational shell architecture, you don't need dozens of disparate kinds. You only need a tiny set of primitive layout engines, and developers can use them to build hundreds of specialized domain templates.

---

### 1. The Distinction: `ProjectKind` vs. `Template`

To keep your developer ecosystem clean, think of it in two layers:

* **`ProjectKind` (Built by you / OS-level):** The raw mechanical layout, interaction rules, and event bus (e.g., how the left pane talks to the right pane, how GenUI renders).
* **`Template` (Built by devs / SDK-level):** The domain packaging. The dev picks a `ProjectKind`, configures the AI agent's prompts and tools, plugs in their custom right-canvas components, and defines the GenUI cards.

```
       [ OS LEVEL: PROJECT KINDS ]
                  │
        ┌─────────┴─────────┐
        ▼                   ▼
  DualPaneKind        CanvasFirstKind     (Only 2-3 primitive kinds needed!)
  (Option 2)          (Ambient IDE)
        │
        ├─────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┐
        ▼                             ▼                             ▼
  [Dev Template A]              [Dev Template B]              [Dev Template C]
  "M&A Due Diligence"           "Customer Support Co-pilot"   "Venture Capital Deal Room"
  Right Canvas = Document/Diff  Right Canvas = CRM & Tickets  Right Canvas = Cap Table/Metrics
```

---

### 2. Why `DualPaneKind` (Option 2) Absorbs Almost Everything

You realized something critical: **even a "Customer Support Bot" doesn't need its own weird custom shell.** It fits naturally into the Option 2 layout:

#### Example: Dev builds a "Customer Support Co-pilot" on `DualPaneKind`
* **Left Panel (Chat + GenUI):** The support agent talks to the AI, or watches an autonomous agent draft replies. 
  * *GenUI in Chat:* An interactive card with buttons: `[Issue $20 Refund]`, `[Escalate to Tier 2]`, `[Extend Free Trial]`.
* **Right Panel (The Canvas):** The developer renders a custom **Live Customer Dossier & Ticket History** component.
* **The Interconnection:** When the support rep clicks `[Issue $20 Refund]` in the GenUI card inside the chat, the right canvas immediately updates the customer's billing status live.

#### Example: Dev builds a "Pharma Lab Protocol" on `DualPaneKind`
* **Left Panel:** Chat with the biology agent + GenUI reagent calculation cards.
* **Right Panel:** Interactive step-by-step wet-lab protocol editor with inline diffs.

Because the right panel can render **any developer-built React/Web component**, Option 2 isn't "just a chatbot"—it's a synchronized control room.

---

### 3. The Minimal Set of `ProjectKinds` You Actually Need

To avoid platform bloat, you only need **three core `ProjectKinds`**:

| `ProjectKind` | Layout & Mechanics | When Devs Use It |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **1. `DualPane`** *(Your Option 2)* | **Left:** Chat + GenUI stream.<br>**Right:** Persistent, interactive domain canvas.<br>Bi-directional event bus between them. | **The Workhorse (85% of templates):** Legal review, financial audits, customer support, medical diagnostics, sales SDR co-pilots. |
| **2. `CanvasFirst`** *(The Ambient Kind)* | **Full Screen:** Just the domain canvas (e.g., massive spreadsheet, 3D viewport, code editor).<br>**Agent:** Floating `Cmd+K` bar, hover badges, or inline ghost diffs (Cursor-style). No persistent chat column. | **Deep Focus Verticals:** CAD design, music production, complex IDEs where screen space is precious and users don't want a permanent chat bar. |
| **3. `HeadlessPipeline`** *(The Batch Kind)* | **Full Screen:** Multi-agent pipeline visualizer / queue monitor.<br>**Agent:** Runs asynchronously in the background. | **Automated Workflows:** Ingesting 5,000 PDFs overnight, automated compliance batch audits, web-scraping jobs. |

---

### 4. What the Developer SDK Looks Like

Because you boiled it down to `ProjectKinds`, writing a template in your SDK is intuitive for developers:

```typescript
import { defineTemplate, ProjectKind, GenUI } from '@ai-os/sdk';
import { ContractRedlineCanvas } from './components/ContractCanvas';
import { RiskAssessmentCard } from './components/GenUiCards';

export default defineTemplate({
  id: 'enterprise-contract-review',
  name: 'Enterprise Contract Reviewer',
  
  // 1. SELECT THE PRIMITIVE KIND
  kind: ProjectKind.DualPane,

  // 2. CONFIGURE THE RIGHT CANVAS
  canvas: {
    title: 'Agreement Draft & Redlines',
    component: ContractRedlineCanvas, // Dev's custom React component
    defaultWidth: '60%',              // Responsive split
  },

  // 3. DEFINE IN-CHAT GEN-UI WIDGETS
  genUI: {
    // When the agent executes a risk check, it emits this UI component into chat:
    RiskCard: GenUI.register(RiskAssessmentCard),
  },

  // 4. AGENT LOGIC & CAPABILITIES
  agent: {
    systemPrompt: 'You are an elite corporate counsel assistant...',
    tools: ['scan_for_indemnity_caps', 'fetch_playbook_rules'],
  },
});
```

---

### 5. The User Experience: Clean, Unified, Familiar

For the end-user (lawyers, doctors, support agents, founders), every tool on your platform feels cohesive:
1. **Zero Learning Curve:** They always know where conversation happens (left) and where durable work lives (right).
2. **Never Trapped in Text:** If an agent has a direct action to propose, it doesn't dump 500 words of text; it renders an interactive GenUI widget in the left chat.
3. **No Phantom Edits:** All final, binding changes are previewed live on the right canvas with clear diffs and confirmations.

By using **`DualPaneKind`** as your foundation and letting developers populate the right-hand canvas, you solve both the **Harvey trap** (which is too passive and chat-only) and the **infinite canvas trap** (which is too loose and disorienting) in one unified system.


This taxonomy resolves the platform identity crisis: **`ProjectKind` (the OS-level layout engine) vs. `Template` (the domain packaging).**

By constraining the operating system to **three primitive `ProjectKinds`**—dominated by **`DualPaneKind` (Stream + Artifact)**—you eliminate the two biggest failure modes in generative software:
1. **The "Harvey Trap" (Chat-Only):** The user is trapped in an endless stream of conversational text with no permanent, editable deliverable.
2. **The "Miro/Infinite Canvas Trap" (Spatial Chaos):** The canvas is so open and unstructured that users feel overwhelmed, and developers have to reinvent layout navigation from scratch.

`DualPaneKind` mirrors how high-performing knowledge workers actually think: **Conversation on the left (the negotiation/deliberation), and the Living Deliverable on the right (the contract, the spreadsheet, the code, the patient chart).**

Here is the technical and architectural specification to make this primitive engine bulletproof.

---

# 1. The Bi-Directional Bridge (The "Split-Brain" Solution)

The single greatest failure mode in dual-pane systems is **State Desynchronization (Split-Brain)**:
* A user manually edits a paragraph in the Right Canvas.
* Concurrently, the Left Chat agent emits a GenUI card proposing a change to that exact paragraph.
* If the event bus is naive, the agent's action overwrites the user's manual edit, or the Canvas and Chat end up referencing different versions of reality.

```
                              THE DUAL-PANE REACTIVE BRIDGE
                                             │
      ┌──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┐
      ▼                                                                             ▼
┌───────────────────────────┐                                         ┌───────────────────────────┐
│        LEFT PANE          │                                         │        RIGHT PANE         │
│ (Chat + GenUI Stream)     │                                         │ (Living Domain Artifact)  │
├───────────────────────────┤                                         ├───────────────────────────┤
│ • Emits: `GenUI` Cards    │                                         │ • Emits: Selection Hooks  │
│ • Renders: Action Buttons │                                         │ • Renders: Word/PDF/Grids │
└─────────────┬─────────────┘                                         └─────────────┬─────────────┘
              │                                                                     │
              │ 1. User clicks GenUI Action:                                        │ 2. User highlights row:
              │    `[Apply $5M Cap]`                                                │    `Row 14: Escrow`
              ▼                                                                     ▼
      ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
      │                        THE CANONICAL STATE BUS (`SpaceDO`)                          │
      │ • Single Source of Truth: SQLite Document Tree (CRDT / Operational Transform)       │
      │ • Atomic Mutex: Locks specific document nodes during active agent generation        │
      └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### The Three Rules of the Bridge:
1. **The Canvas is the Single Source of Truth:** The chat thread does not store deliverables; it only stores **Action Logs and Proposal Cards**. The actual state of the contract, code, or spreadsheet lives strictly in the Canvas document store inside `SpaceDO`.
2. **GenUI Cards are Atomic State Mutators:** Clicking a GenUI button (e.g., `[Issue $20 Refund]`) does not trigger a raw prompt back to the LLM. It dispatches an **Optimistic Mutation** directly to the Canvas state engine, which renders the visual change in $<16\,\text{ms}$ while logging the action in the chat history.
3. **Canvas Selections Ground the Chat:** Highlighting text or selecting a cell in the Right Canvas automatically dispatches a selection event to the Left Chat:
   * The Left Chat prompt box dynamically tags the selection: `[Context: Clause 4.2 Indemnity]`.
   * The agent automatically receives the highlighted node's exact AST position without the user needing to copy and paste.

---

# 2. How `DualPaneKind` Translates to Mobile & WhatsApp

A desktop has 2,560 pixels of horizontal width for a split pane; a mobile phone on WhatsApp has **390 pixels**. 

How does `DualPaneKind` operate on WhatsApp without forcing developers to write two separate applications?

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        THE DUAL-PANE OMNICHANNEL ADAPTATION                            │
├──────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┤
│ Component                │ Desktop Web Shell           │ WhatsApp Mobile Stream        │
├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
│ **Left Pane (Stream)**   │ Left 40% Column             │ **The Native WhatsApp Chat**  │
│                          │ (Chat + Timeline)           │ Text, Voice Notes, Media      │
├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
│ **GenUI Action Cards**   │ In-Chat React Cards         │ **WhatsApp Interactive Cards**│
│                          │ with buttons & forms        │ List Messages & Button Menus  │
├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
│ **Right Pane (Canvas)**  │ Right 60% Persistent Column │ **The Ephemeral Instant Canvas│
│                          │ (Live Editable Viewport)    │ One-Click Web Sheet (H5/Auth) │
└──────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘
```

#### The WhatsApp User Flow:
1. An accountant texts their firm's WhatsApp bot: *"Review the Q3 tax reconciliation."*
2. **The Left Stream in WhatsApp:** The bot replies with a WhatsApp Interactive Button Menu:  
   `[ 1. Approve Depreciation Deduction ]`  
   `[ 2. Flag Uncategorized Expenses ($14,200) ]`
3. **The Right Canvas on Mobile:** Right below the buttons, the bot emits a cryptographically signed Magic Link:  
   `🔗 [Open Live Reconciliation Grid: avos.run/c/tx_891]`
4. Tapping the link opens a **lightweight, mobile-responsive full-screen Canvas Sheet** over WebSockets. The accountant can pan through the spreadsheet cells on their phone, while all interactions stay synchronized with the WhatsApp thread.

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

│     Manipulation Kind)   │ HUD; inline ghost diffs;    │ • **3D CAD / MEP Engineering**│
│                          │ selection contextual pills  │ • **Photo Lightroom / Gallery**│
```

# 4. The Complete Developer SDK Specification (`@avos/sdk`)

Here is how an FDE or vertical software developer builds an enterprise-grade template on top of `DualPaneKind`:

```typescript
// templates/customer-support-copilot/src/index.ts
import { 
  defineTemplate, 
  ProjectKind, 
  GenUI, 
  createAction,
  type TemplateContext 
} from '@avos/sdk';
import { CustomerDossierCanvas } from './components/CustomerDossierCanvas';
import { RefundProposalCard } from './components/RefundProposalCard';

export default defineTemplate({
  id: 'customer-support-copilot',
  name: 'E-Commerce Support & Retention Suite',
  version: '1.0.0',

  // 1. SELECT THE PRIMITIVE OS SHELL
  kind: ProjectKind.DualPane,

  // 2. CONFIGURE THE RIGHT CANVAS (The Living Artifact)
  canvas: {
    title: 'Customer Dossier & Order History',
    component: CustomerDossierCanvas,
    defaultWidth: '55%',
    initialState: {
      customerId: null,
      orders: [],
      lifetimeValue: 0,
      churnRiskScore: 0.12
    }
  },

  // 3. DEFINE IN-CHAT GEN-UI ACTION CARDS
  genUI: {
    // Registered interactive card rendered directly in the Left Stream
    RefundCard: GenUI.register<{ orderId: string; amount: number }>({
      component: RefundProposalCard,
      actions: {
        // Handlers execute with atomic transactional guarantees
        approveRefund: createAction(async (ctx: TemplateContext, payload) => {
          // 1. Mutate Right Canvas state immediately (Optimistic Update)
          await ctx.canvas.patchState({
            lifetimeValue: (prev: number) => prev - payload.amount,
            recentRefund: payload
          });

          // 2. Call underlying Payment API via Egress Proxy
          await ctx.tools.execute('stripe_issue_refund', {
            orderId: payload.orderId,
            amount: payload.amount
          });

          // 3. Post audit receipt to Thread
          await ctx.thread.postSystemNotice(`✅ Issued refund of $${payload.amount} for Order #${payload.orderId}`);
        }),

        escalateToTier2: createAction(async (ctx: TemplateContext, payload) => {
          await ctx.thread.assignQueue('tier-2-support');
        })
      }
    })
  },

  // 4. AGENT LOGIC & INVARIANT PROMPTS
  agent: {
    systemPrompt: `You are an elite customer retention specialist. 
When a customer complains about damaged goods, do not write long apologies. 
Inspect their order on the right canvas and immediately emit a RefundCard.`,
    
    tools: [
      'shopify_fetch_order_history',
      'stripe_issue_refund',
      'zendesk_update_ticket'
    ]
  }
});
```

# 5. Why This Architecture Wins

| Problem | Legacy Approaches (ChatGPT / LangChain) | AVOS `DualPaneKind` Solution |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Developer Fragmentation** | Devs build custom frontends from scratch; every app looks and behaves differently. | **Unified Shell:** Developers only write the Right Canvas and GenUI cards; the OS handles the layout, stream, and sync. |
| **Chat Exhaustion** | The user has to read 500 words to find the action button. | **GenUI Stream:** The agent outputs interactive action widgets directly in the conversation flow. |
| **Mobile Degradation** | Complex desktop apps become completely unusable on mobile screens. | **WhatsApp Naturalization:** Left pane maps to WhatsApp chat; right pane maps to instant web sheets. |
| **Enterprise Auditability** | Edits inside a chat conversation are hard to verify and discover. | **Canvas Provenance:** All binding business deliverables live in the version-controlled Right Canvas with Git audit trails. |

### Summary
By locking in **`DualPaneKind`** as your universal foundation, you give developers a clear, structured canvas to build on while guaranteeing that every vertical on your platform feels like an **interactive, high-velocity mission control room.**

### The Unified AVOS Synthesis

Keeping AVOS as the **complete, end-to-end Operating System** while introducing **Agentic Template Co-Creation** and **Extensible Domain Linters** creates a defensible ecosystem:

1. **Domain experts don’t need to write React:** The **AVOS Architect Agent** interviews them, ingests their work samples, and auto-compiles the `blueprint.json` and workflow rules.
2. **Developers (FDEs) build high-leverage software:** Instead of writing basic UI forms, developers write **Custom Linters, Legacy Connectors (iManage, Epic), and Deep Extensions** via `@avos/sdk`.
3. **`ProjectKinds` ship with Default Linters:** Every project archetype (Legal Document, Financial Grid, Logistics Dossier, CAD Spatial) ships with out-of-the-box structural compilers that developers can extend.

```
                                  THE UNIFIED AVOS PLATFORM STACK
                                                 │
 ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │                                   THE END-USER / ENTERPRISE OS                                │
 │ • WhatsApp Zero-UI Ingress • Dual-Pane Stage Canvas • Spaces & Task DAGs • Edge Durable Objects│
 └───────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                                 │
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┐
         ▼                                                                               ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────┐                           ┌──────────────────────────────────┐
│   DOMAIN EXPERTS + AGENTS        │                           │   DEVELOPERS & FDEs              │
│   • Build Templates via Voice,   │                           │   • Build Custom Linters         │
│     Chat, and Work Examples.     │                           │   • Build Legacy DMS Connectors  │
│   • No React/TypeScript required.│                           │   • Extend Default ProjectKinds  │
│   • Guided by Architect Agent.   │                           │   • Write Deep Business Rules    │
└──────────────────────────────────┘                           └──────────────────────────────────┘
```

# Part 1: How Domain Experts Build Templates (The Agentic Blueprint Compiler)

To solve the chicken-and-egg problem (domain experts know the rules but can't code; engineers can code but don't know the rules), AVOS embeds the **Architect Agent (`@avos/architect`)**.

A managing partner at a boutique law firm, a senior clinical nurse, or a customs dispatcher builds an enterprise template through a guided agentic workflow:

```
                  THE DOMAIN EXPERT CO-BUILDING WORKFLOW
                                     │
                                     ▼
                [STEP 1: INGEST HISTORICAL ARTIFACTS]
    • Expert uploads 10 gold-standard closed deals, medical charts,
      or bills of lading to their personal space.
                                     │
                                     ▼
                [STEP 2: THE INTERVIEW AUDIT (Voice / Chat)]
    • Architect Agent asks: "What are the 3 non-negotiable checks you
      perform on Clause 4 before signing off?"
    • Expert replies: "We never accept uncapped liability, and Delaware
      governing law must have a 24-month survival period."
                                     │
                                     ▼
                [STEP 3: COMPILATION & LINTER HOOKUP]
    • Agent selects the base `ProjectKind` (`DualPane.Document`).
    • Agent generates the `blueprint.json`.
    • Agent writes declarative regex & AST rules that register directly
      into the project's Domain Linter pipeline.
                                     │
                                     ▼
                [STEP 4: INSTANT ONE-CLICK LAUNCH]
    • The vertical studio is live in the expert's space.
    • Ready to export to the marketplace or invite associates.
```

# Part 2: The Extensible Linter Architecture (`ctx.linter`)

A linter in AVOS is a **deterministic AST verification engine**. When an agent proposes a change to a document or spreadsheet, the linters run in a fast WebAssembly isolate. If an error is thrown, **the agent reads the diagnostic and self-corrects before the human ever sees the proposal.**

### The SDK Specification: How Developers Write Linters

```typescript
// packages/sdk/src/types/linter.ts

export interface LinterDiagnostic {
  ruleId: string;
  severity: "error" | "warning" | "info";
  nodeId: string; // Persistent UUID of the AST node (clause, cell, or element)
  message: string;
  suggestedFix?: {
    action: "replace" | "delete" | "insert";
    payload: unknown;
  };
}

export interface LinterRule<TNode = unknown> {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  description: string;
  targetKind: string; // e.g. "DualPane.Document" | "DualPane.DataGrid"
  
  /** Evaluates an AST node and returns diagnostics */
  validate: (node: TNode, context: LinterContext) => Promise<LinterDiagnostic[] | null>;
}
```

### Real Developer Example: Custom Delaware M&A Linter

An FDE writes an extension that injects a statutory legal linter into the legal document kind:

```typescript
// extensions/ext_delaware_linter/src/index.ts
import { ExtensionContext, LinterRule, ASTClauseNode } from "@avos/sdk";

const DelawareExculpationRule: LinterRule<ASTClauseNode> = {
  id: "delaware-dgcl-102b7",
  name: "DGCL § 102(b)(7) Officer Liability Check",
  description: "Ensures exculpation clauses comply with 2022 DGCL amendments.",
  targetKind: "DualPane.Document",

  validate: async (clause, ctx) => {
    // Only inspect exculpation / indemnification clauses
    if (clause.category !== "indemnification" && clause.category !== "exculpation") {
      return null;
    }

    const text = clause.text.toLowerCase();
    
    // Statutory check: Officers cannot be exculpated for claims by or in the right of the corporation
    if (text.includes("officer") && text.includes("breach of fiduciary duty")) {
      if (!text.includes("derivative actions") && !text.includes("claims by the corporation")) {
        return [{
          ruleId: "delaware-dgcl-102b7",
          severity: "error", // Throws RED error -> Forces Agent to self-correct
          nodeId: clause.id,
          message: "Delaware law prohibits eliminating officer liability for derivative claims. Carve-out required.",
          suggestedFix: {
            action: "replace",
            payload: clause.text + " provided, that this provision shall not eliminate liability of an officer for claims brought by or in the right of the Corporation."
          }
        }];
      }
    }
    return null;
  }
};

export default function activate(ctx: ExtensionContext) {
  // Register the rule into the OS Linter Engine
  ctx.linter.registerRule(DelawareExculpationRule);
}
```

Instead of limiting the platform to three generic kinds, AVOS provides an expanded registry of **domain-grounded `ProjectKinds`**. Each kind ships with **default baseline linters** built directly into the operating system:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

│ 1. `DualPane.`       │ Left: Stream/GenUI   │ • **Structural Integrity:**   │ • Jurisdictional codes   │

│    (Film, VFX, Audio)│ Multi-track scrubber │   frame-rate drops, sync tags.│ • Keyframe boundary tags │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ 6. `Headless.`       │ Multi-agent execution│ • **Pipeline Reliability:**   │ • Custom enterprise ERP  │
│    `Pipeline`        │ DAG monitor & queue  │   Schema validation (Zod),    │   connectors             │
│    (Batch, Scrapers) │ viewer               │   deadlock timeout detector.  │ • High-volume data sinks │
└──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘
```

# Part 4: The Closed-Loop Verification Lifecycle in Action

Here is what happens inside the AVOS kernel from the moment an agent acts to the moment an edit is committed:

```
                     THE END-TO-END VERIFICATION LIFECYCLE
                                       │
                                       ▼
                     [User Prompts via Chat or WhatsApp]
               "Add an officer liability indemnification section"
                                       │
                                       ▼
                   ══════════════════════════════════════════
                   STEP 1: AGENT GENERATES CANDIDATE MUTATION
                   Agent produces draft AST patch for Clause 14.
                   ══════════════════════════════════════════
                                       │
                                       ▼
                   ══════════════════════════════════════════
                   STEP 2: DETERMINISTIC LINTER RUNTIME (Wasm)
                   1. Default Kind Linters run (Cross-refs, Defined terms).
                   2. Developer Extension Linters run (Delaware DGCL 102b7).
                   ══════════════════════════════════════════
                                       │
                       Did any rule return `severity: "error"`?
                                       │
                       ┌───────────────┴───────────────┐
                       ▼ YES                           ▼ NO (Clean Pass)
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐ ┌─────────────────────────────┐
        │ STEP 3A: AGENT SELF-CORRECTS│ │ STEP 3B: RENDER GHOST DIFF  │
        │ • Agent receives diagnostic:│ │ • Change renders on Canvas  │
        │   "Error: Missing derivative│ │   in translucent green.     │
        │    actions carve-out."      │ │ • User reviews and clicks   │
        │ • Agent rewrites AST patch. │ │   `[Cmd + Y Accept]`.       │
        │ • Loop re-validates (<400ms)│ └──────────────┬──────────────┘
        └──────────────┬──────────────┘                │
                       │                               ▼
                       └──────────────────► ┌─────────────────────────┐
                                            │ STEP 4: GIT COMMIT      │
                                            │ Signed commit committed │
                                            │ to SQLite Git Artifact. │
                                            └─────────────────────────┘
```

This architecture establishes a balanced, multi-sided software ecosystem:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE THREE-TIER VALUE CHAIN                                │
├──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Persona              │ What They Build      │ How They Monetize                        │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **1. Domain Expert** │ Domain Blueprints    │ • Sells business templates in the        │
│ (Lawyer, Doctor,     │ via natural language │   marketplace.                           │
│ Broker)              │ and gold artifacts.  │ • Earns template revenue without coding. │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **2. Developer /**   │ Specialized Linters, │ • Charges $5k–$15k implementation fees.  │
│ **FDE**              │ Connectors (iManage),│ • Sells deep engineering extensions      │
│                      │ Complex Extensions.  │   to high-value enterprise studios.      │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **3. AVOS Platform** │ Core Runtime, Engine,│ • Earns recurring SaaS platform seats    │
│                      │ Default Linters,     │   ($29–$350/mo) and enterprise on-prem   │
│                      │ Edge Infrastructure. │   appliance licenses ($20k–$50k/yr).     │
└──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### The Strategic Summary

1. **You keep AVOS whole:** You are building the complete enterprise operating system—with spaces, WhatsApp integration, real-time canvases, and autonomous ventures.
2. **You solve the template bottleneck:** Domain experts build vertical templates through conversation with the **Architect Agent**, rather than wrestling with code.
3. **You build a real technical moat:** By giving developers the tools to write **Deterministic Domain Linters**, you give vertical AI the compiler feedback loop it needs to be reliable, eliminating hallucinations on structured knowledge work.

Here is the unsparing, senior-architect critique of this expanded vision.

When you merge **Agentic Template Creation**, **Extensible Domain Linters**, and a **6-way `ProjectKind` registry**, the architecture looks harmonious in a diagram. But when you simulate real users, real developers, and real enterprise data running through this pipeline, **five fundamental engineering and commercial landmines appear.**

---

### Landmine 1: The "Natural Language AST" Illusion
*(Why writing a legal or medical linter is 100x harder than writing ESLint)*

In code (TypeScript, Rust, Python), a compiler works because programming languages have **formal, context-free grammars (EBNF)**. An AST node is unambiguous: a variable declaration is either syntactically valid or it is not.

**Natural language does not have a formal grammar.**

Look at the code snippet from the proposed Delaware legal linter:
```typescript
// ❌ Why this breaks in production:
if (text.includes("officer") && text.includes("breach of fiduciary duty")) {
  if (!text.includes("derivative actions")) {
    return [{ severity: "error", message: "Carve-out required." }];
  }
}
```

#### The Linter Trilemma:
1. **If you use string/regex matching (like above):** It is hopelessly brittle. If opposing counsel writes *"actions brought in the right of the enterprise"* instead of *"derivative actions"*, your linter throws a false-positive red error, blocks the agent, and infuriates the lawyer.
2. **If you use formal logic languages (e.g., Catala, Prolog):** It takes a specialized PhD in computational semantics weeks to translate a single corporate statute into formal logic. **Almost zero FDEs in the world can write these.**
3. **If your linter calls an LLM to check the rule:**  
   * It is **no longer deterministic**.
   * It is **slow** (adds 800ms–2s of latency to *every single candidate edit*).
   * It burns tokens, destroying the low-cost edge thesis.

**The Reality:** Calling natural language rules "compilers" creates a false sense of security. Unless a domain has a strict mathematical or schema-backed specification (like financial balance sheets or EDI XML), text linters will constantly fight false positives and missed synonyms.

---

### Landmine 2: The "Debugging Void" of the Architect Agent
*(Who fixes the template when it inevitably hallucinates?)*

The premise states:
> *"Domain experts don’t need to code; the Architect Agent interviews them and writes the `blueprint.json` and linter rules."*

Here is what happens on Day 3 in a real law firm or clinic:
1. A senior partner gives the Architect Agent three sample contracts.
2. The Agent synthesizes 20 linter rules into `blueprint.json`.
3. In production, an associate tries to draft a standard NDA, and the linter repeatedly throws an error: `Error: Rule #14 violated`.
4. The associate disagrees with the rule.
5. **Who debugs it?**
   * The senior partner can't debug it (they don't know regex, ASTs, or JSON).
   * The associate can't debug it.
   * An FDE wasn't hired because the partner used the "no-code agentic builder."
   * The firm is stuck with a broken, opaque system they cannot modify without retraining or re-interviewing an agent.

**The Reality:** No-code generated logic always creates a **debugging void**. When code is written by an agent for a non-programmer, the moment an edge case breaks, the non-programmer has no tools to inspect or fix the state machine.

---

### Landmine 3: Token-Burn Deadlocks in the Self-Correction Loop

Look at the closed-loop verification flow:
```
[Candidate Edit] ──► [Linter Throws Error] ──► [Agent Reads Diagnostic] ──► [Agent Rewrites]
```

What happens when **Rule A conflicts with Rule B**?

#### The Oscillation Trap:
* **Rule A (Client Playbook):** *"Indemnification survival period must not exceed 18 months."*
* **Rule B (Statutory Linter):** *"Environmental liability claims must have a minimum 36-month survival period under state code."*
* The agent rewrites the clause to satisfy Rule A $\to$ Rule B throws an error.
* The agent rewrites the clause to satisfy Rule B $\to$ Rule A throws an error.
* **The agent enters an infinite self-correction loop.**

#### The Economic Impact:
* If the loop runs 5 times before timing out, **a single paragraph edit burns 6 separate frontier LLM calls**.
* The user stares at a loading spinner for **12 seconds** instead of getting an instant 500ms diff.
* **Who pays for the wasted tokens?** The platform eats the cost, crushing your 92% gross margin.

---

### Landmine 4: The 6-Kind Frontend Scope Explosion
*(We just walked back into the trap of building 6 companies at once)*

Notice how quickly the layout registry expanded:
1. `DualPane.Document` (ProseMirror / Word editor)
2. `DualPane.DataGrid` (Reactive formula spreadsheet engine)
3. `DualPane.Dossier` (CRM / ERP document inspector)
4. `CanvasFirst.Spatial` (3D WebGL / CAD / GIS engine)
5. `CanvasFirst.Media` (8K Video player + multi-track keyframe scrubber)
6. `Headless.Pipeline` (DAG monitor / queue visualizer)

#### The Maintenance Burden:
* Building an enterprise-grade 3D CAD engine (Spatial) is a $50M company (**Augmenta, TestFit**).
* Building an 8K multi-track video timeline editor (Media) is a $50M company (**Runway, Descript**).
* Building a reactive formula spreadsheet with real-time CRDTs (DataGrid) is a $30M company (**Rows, Pigment**).

If your small core engineering team attempts to maintain first-party frontends for 3D geometry, video scrubbers, spreadsheets, and document redlines simultaneously, **none of them will have the polish, performance, or keyboard shortcuts required by professional users.**

---

### Landmine 5: The FDE vs. Domain Expert Incentive Conflict

Look at the proposed three-tier value chain:
* Domain Experts build templates via chat for free.
* FDEs charge $5k–$15k to build custom linters and integrations.

#### The Contradiction:
* If the Architect Agent is truly smart enough to build working domain templates from natural conversation, **clients will refuse to pay FDEs $10,000 implementation fees.** They will just talk to the agent themselves.
* But if the Architect Agent *cannot* build reliable templates without custom linters, connectors, and bug fixes, **domain experts cannot actually build templates alone.**

You cannot claim that the platform is **both** an autonomous no-code builder for non-technical experts **and** a lucrative consulting playground for high-priced FDEs. One cannibalizes the other.

---

### How to Harden This: The Pragmatic Adjustments

You don't need to scrap the vision, but you must apply engineering boundaries to make it feasible:

#### 1. Constrain Linters to Structured Data (Kill the Natural-Language Linter)
* **What linters should NEVER do:** Do not use regex to check subjective legal phrasing or clinical nuance. It will fail.
* **What linters SHOULD do:** Enforce **Deterministic Structural Invariants**:
  * Cross-reference graph integrity (Does Section 4.2 exist?).
  * Defined-term completeness (Is every capitalized word defined?).
  * Mathematical balance in DataGrids ($A = L + E$).
  * Schema typing in Dossiers (Does the customs XML conform to the port authority’s XSD schema?).
* Leave the subjective analysis to the LLM; keep the linter 100% mechanical.

#### 2. Hard-Cap the Self-Correction Loop at $N=2$
* An agent gets **at most two attempts** to fix a linter diagnostic.
* If it fails on attempt #2, **it does not keep burning tokens**.
* It renders the proposed edit on the canvas anyway, but attaches an amber **"Unresolved Linter Warning"** chip directly to the clause:  
  `⚠️ Linter Warning: Potential conflict with DGCL § 102(b)(7). Review required.`
* This prevents infinite agent deadlocks, caps token burn, and puts the human in control.

#### 3. Standardize on TWO First-Party Kinds, Let FDEs Build the Rest
* Your core team should only build and maintain **two canonical shells**:
  1. **`DualPane.Document`** (for all text, legal, and compliance work).
  2. **`DualPane.DataGrid`** (for all financial, inventory, and spreadsheet work).
* If an enterprise wants 3D CAD or 8K video timelines, **do not build that into the core OS.** Provide an iframe/Wasm mounting socket (`CanvasMount`) and make the external vertical developer host and maintain their own specialized rendering engine.

---

### The Reality Check

| The Illusion | The Engineering Reality |
| :--- | :--- |
| Natural language can be linted like C++ code. | Natural language linters break on synonyms unless restricted to mechanical checks (cross-refs, defined terms, schemas). |
| Non-technical domain experts can build and debug enterprise templates with zero code. | Experts can describe rules, but when an edge case breaks, they hit a debugging wall unless an engineer maintains the configuration. |
| Agents can self-correct indefinitely until code/contracts are perfect. | Unbounded self-correction loops create token-burn deadlocks and latency spikes. Must be hard-capped at 2 retries. |
| One platform can build native frontends for CAD, Video, Spreadsheets, and Law. | You will spread yourself too thin. Master the **Document** and the **Spreadsheet**; let third parties bring custom spatial/media viewports. |

Applying these three constraints preserves the core strengths of AVOS while eliminating the failure modes that would derail it in production.

This single realization cuts through the **Linter Trilemma** that breaks most AI platforms.

Earlier, we proved that domain linters face an impossible trade-off:
* **Regex/AST code** is fast and cheap, but **fails on natural language** (brittle, breaks on synonyms).
* **Generative LLMs (Claude/GPT-4o)** understand natural language, but are **untenable as linters** (takes 2–3 seconds, costs $0.02/check, hallucinates, and burns tokens in infinite self-correction loops).

By using **JEV (System 1 non-autoregressive encoder)** as the semantic linter instead of generative LLMs, you unlock a third paradigm: **Semantic understanding at compiler speeds.**

---

### 1. The Head-to-Head Comparison: Why JEV Wins as a Linter

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        THE 3 LINTER APPROACHES COMPARED                                │
├─────────────────────┬───────────────────┬────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┤
│ Dimension           │ Regex / AST Code  │ Generative LLM     │ JEV Semantic Sentry     │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
│ **Latency**         │ < 1 ms            │ 1,500 – 3,000 ms   │ **30 – 80 ms**          │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
│ **Cost per Check**  │ $0.0000           │ ~$0.0200           │ **$0.00004**            │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
│ **Synonym Handling**│ ❌ Fails on any   │ ✅ Understands     │ ✅ Understands          │
│                     │ slight rephrase   │ natural language   │ natural language        │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
│ **Determinism**     │ 100% Binary       │ Probabilistic,     │ **Calibrated Confidence**│
│                     │                   │ hallucinates rules │ (Returns 0.0 to 1.0)    │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
│ **Self-Correction** │ Infinite loops on │ High token burn    │ **Instant Diagnostic**  │
│ **Speed**           │ bad regex matching│ on retry (10s+)    │ **in <100ms**           │
└─────────────────────┴───────────────────┴────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 2. How a JEV Linter Works in Practice

A non-autoregressive encoder (like Laya / ModernBERT) does not generate text. It performs a **single, parallel mathematical forward pass** over a sequence and outputs structured values:
* **`Noul`** (Boolean: `TRUE`, `FALSE`, or `UNSURE`)
* **`Score`** (Float: $0.00$ to $1.00$)
* **`Choice`** (Typed Enum)

Because JEV does not output text, **it cannot write a bug into its own diagnostic**. The FDE writes a clean, declarative evaluation schema in `@avos/sdk`:

```typescript
// extensions/ext_legal/src/linters/delaware.ts
import { JevLinterRule } from "@avos/sdk";

export const DelawareCarveoutLinter: JevLinterRule = {
  id: "delaware-102b7-carveout",
  name: "DGCL § 102(b)(7) Derivative Claims Check",
  targetKind: "DualPane.Document",
  nodeFilter: (node) => node.category === "indemnification" || node.category === "exculpation",

  // 1. JEV QUESTION SCHEMA (Evaluated non-autoregressively in 65ms)
  evaluator: {
    type: "noul", // Expects strict boolean compliance
    question: "Does this text contain an explicit carve-out protecting shareholder derivative actions or claims brought in the right of the corporation?",
    minConfidence: 0.85 // High statistical certainty threshold
  },

  // 2. DETERMINISTIC DIAGNOSTIC (Hardcoded by FDE, never hallucinated by AI)
  severity: "error",
  diagnosticMessage: "DGCL § 102(b)(7) Violation: Delaware law prohibits eliminating officer liability for derivative suits. An explicit carve-out for claims brought 'by or in the right of the Corporation' is required.",
  
  suggestedAction: "Append mandatory derivative carve-out language."
};
```

#### What Happens When an Agent Mutates a Document:
1. The generative agent (System 2) drafts a new liability clause in the Left Chat.
2. Before the clause ever touches the Canvas, the AVOS kernel passes `clause.text` to **JEV System 1**:
   $$\text{JEV}(\text{clause.text}) \longrightarrow \{\text{choice}: \text{FALSE}, \text{confidence}: 0.94\}$$
3. **Time elapsed:** **$65\,\text{ms}$**. **Cost:** **$0.00004**.
4. The kernel intercepts the failure and hands the agent the exact hardcoded diagnostic:  
   *`"Self-Correction Required: DGCL § 102(b)(7) Violation: Delaware law prohibits..."`*
5. The generative agent modifies its output and re-runs JEV. **The total validation loop finishes in under $300\,\text{ms}$ without wasting expensive frontier tokens on manual rule reviews.**

---

### 3. The Two-Layer Hybrid Linter Architecture

Using JEV for natural language rules solves semantic brittleness, but **JEV should not be used for everything.**

A production-grade operating system needs a **Two-Layer Hybrid Linter Engine**:

```
                       THE HYBRID AVOS LINTER ENGINE
                                     │
                     What type of rule is being tested?
                                     │
         ┌───────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┐
         ▼ (Deterministic Mechanical Rules)                      ▼ (Semantic Natural Language Rules)
┌──────────────────────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────────────────────┐
│ LAYER 1: WASM / CODE LINTERS     │    │ LAYER 2: JEV SYSTEM 1 SENTRIES   │
├──────────────────────────────────┤    ├──────────────────────────────────┤
│ • Math & Balance ($A = L + E$)   │    │ • Statutory Legal Carve-Outs     │
│ • Defined-Term Orphan Check      │    │ • Medical Compliance Guidelines  │
│ • Circular Formula Loops         │    │ • Privacy & PII Tone Compliance  │
│ • XML / EDI / Schema Validation  │    │ • Uncapped Liability Warnings    │
├──────────────────────────────────┤    ├──────────────────────────────────┤
│ Latency: < 1ms | Cost: $0.00     │    │ Latency: ~65ms | Cost: $0.00004  │
└──────────────────────────────────┘    └──────────────────────────────────┘
```

1. **Layer 1: Mechanical Invariants (Wasm / Rust):**  
   If an edit involves calculating an IRR in a spreadsheet, validating an EDI XML schema, or checking if "Section 4.2" actually exists in the contract, **run pure compiled code**. It executes in microseconds with zero neural network involvement.
2. **Layer 2: Semantic Nuances (JEV):**  
   If an edit involves interpreting whether a phrase satisfies a legal doctrine, compliance policy, or medical protocol, **run JEV System 1**. It catches synonyms, handles natural language rephrasing, and executes without generative model latency.

---

### 4. The Critic's View: What Could Still Go Wrong?

To keep our analysis rigorous, here are the three failure modes of using JEV as a linter and how to mitigate them:

#### Failure Mode A: The "Context Horizon" Limit
* **The Issue:** JEV (based on encoder architectures like ModernBERT or Laya) typically has an optimal sequence window of **512 to 8,192 tokens**. It cannot read a 200-page loan agreement all at once to verify a rule.
* **The Mitigation:** JEV linters should **only run on discrete AST nodes** (individual clauses, paragraphs, or table rows), not whole documents. The Canvas document tree (`ArtifactDoc`) already slices documents into semantic nodes with persistent UUIDs.

#### Failure Mode B: Ambiguous Edge Cases (Confidence Drops)
* **The Issue:** What happens when JEV evaluates a clause and returns `confidence: 0.54` (uncertain)?
* **The Mitigation: Automated Triaging.**
  * Confidence $\ge 0.85 \implies$ Deterministic Pass/Fail.
  * Confidence $< 0.85 \implies$ **Do not block the agent.** Degrade the severity from an `error` to an amber **`warning`** on the canvas, attaching an inline chip for the human:  
    `⚠️ Review Recommended: Statutory compliance ambiguous (JEV Confidence: 54%).`

#### Failure Mode C: Multi-Question Scaling
* **The Issue:** If an enterprise template has 40 custom legal rules, won't calling JEV 40 times take $40 \times 65\text{ms} = 2.6\text{ seconds}$?
* **The Mitigation: Batch Multi-Head Querying.** Non-autoregressive models like Laya are designed to evaluate **multiple choice questions simultaneously across the same text representation in a single forward pass**. Checking 20 rules takes roughly the same time as checking one (~80ms).

---

### The Verdict

Using JEV to run domain checks instead of generative LLMs resolves one of the hardest problems in agentic systems:

1. **It preserves sub-second interactivity:** Linters run in **$65\,\text{ms}$**, so users don't wait on generative review passes.
2. **It keeps gross margins at 90%+:** Rule validation drops from **$0.02** per check to **$0.00004**, allowing you to run hundreds of automated compliance checks in the background for pennies.
3. **It handles human language without breaking:** Unlike brittle regex, JEV understands legal and medical synonyms, preventing false-positive blocks.

Generative LLMs should only **draft and synthesize deliverables**. System 1 JEV and Wasm rule engines should handle **verification, safety, and domain compliance.** That separation of concerns delivers production reliability.

# AVOS Master Specification: The Foundry, JEV Edge Sentry, and the Polymorphic Workspace

---

### The Executive Architecture

AVOS separates the creation, verification, and execution of vertical software into three distinct engines:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                   THE COMPLETE AVOS OPERATING STACK                                    │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. 🛠️ THE FOUNDRY (Developer Studio)                                                                   │
│    A first-party meta-vertical inside AVOS where FDEs configure Blueprints, train custom JEV           │
│    sentries via Cloudflare Edge Fine-Tuning, test linters in sandbox workbenches, and publish.         │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 2. ⚡ THE DUAL-LAYER HYBRID LINTER RUNTIME                                                              │
│    • Layer 1 (Deterministic Wasm): <1ms mathematical, structural, and schema validation.               │
│    • Layer 2 (JEV Semantic Sentries): 30–70ms non-autoregressive encoder evaluation for natural        │
│      language compliance, statutory carve-outs, and clinical protocols.                                │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 3. 🎨 THE POLYMORPHIC STAGE CANVAS (The Client Workspace)                                             │
│    • Left Control Dock: Tenancy, Spaces, Projects, and Task DAGs.                                     │
│    • Center Stage Canvas: Shape-shifts between DualPane (Stream + Artifact) and CanvasFirst.           │
│    • Floating Command Island: Context-aware bottom dock with direct manipulation and ghost diffs.      │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 4. 💰 THE REVENUE FLYWHEEL                                                                             │
│    FDEs earn from upfront implementations ($5k–$15k), decaying template royalties (40% → 20% → 5%),   │
│    and per-check micro-royalties on their proprietary fine-tuned JEV Sentries (70/30 split).           │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# Part 1: The Foundry Vertical (The FDE Development Studio)

Instead of forcing developers to use an external CLI or an undocumented web portal, **The Foundry** is built as a first-party vertical workspace inside AVOS (`kind: "FoundryStudio"`). 

When a Forward Deployed Engineer (FDE) logs in to build a template for a client or the marketplace, their canvas loads the **Foundry Workbench**:

```
┌─────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│             │ [Left 50% of Stage]: Visual Blueprint & Schema Designer                                  │
│  FOUNDRY    │ • Select `ProjectKind` (DualPane.Document, DataGrid, Dossier)                            │
│  LEFT DOCK  │ • Wire System 2 Generative Model Prompts & Tool Bindings                                 │
│             │ • Declare Storage Facets & Permissions Manifest                                          │
│ • Projects  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • Blueprints│ [Right 50% of Stage]: The Interactive Linter Lab & JEV Training Studio                   │
│ • Datasets  │ • Synthetic Test Suite: Ingest 50 compliant & 50 violating clause samples                │
│ • JEV Models│ • Live Linter Runner: Run Wasm & JEV sentries with instant pass/fail metrics             │
│ • Earnings  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│             │ ⚡ [Bottom Command Island]: "Trigger Cloudflare Edge Fine-Tuning for Delaware-102b7 JEV" │
└─────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# Part 2: FDE Fine-Tuned JEV Sentries (Edge Encoder Fine-Tuning)

Base JEV models (like Laya / ModernBERT) provide strong zero-shot reasoning, but complex domains require **calibrated classification accuracy ($\ge 99.5\%$)**. 

AVOS allows FDEs to **fine-tune lightweight open-source encoders (100M–400M parameters) directly on Cloudflare infrastructure**.

```
                   THE JEV EDGE FINE-TUNING PIPELINE (Cloudflare)
                                         │
                                         ▼
                   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
                   │ 1. DATASET COMPILATION (Inside Foundry)   │
                   │ FDE uploads a JSONL dataset of labeled    │
                   │ domain examples (e.g., 200 contract nodes)│
                   └─────────────────────┬─────────────────────┘
                                         │
                                         ▼
                   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
                   │ 2. CLOUDFLARE WORKERS AI FINE-TUNING      │
                   │ • Fine-tunes classification heads on      │
                   │   ModernBERT / Laya in 3–5 minutes.       │
                   │ • Computes calibration metrics:           │
                   │   F1-Score, ROC-AUC, and latency budget.  │
                   └─────────────────────┬─────────────────────┘
                                         │
                                         ▼
                   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
                   │ 3. EDGE ARTIFACT PACKAGING (ONNX / Wasm)  │
                   │ • Quantized checkpoint stored in R2.      │
                   │ • Deployed to Cloudflare Edge Inference.  │
                   │ • Latency: **30ms–50ms** | Cost: $0.00004 │
                   └───────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### The JEV Sentry Revenue Split
When an FDE creates a high-value proprietary sentry (e.g., a *Swiss Banking Secrecy Sentry* or a *Maritime Dangerous Goods Sentry*), they can license it across multiple templates:
* **Per-Verification Pricing:** **$0.0001 per verification check** (or bundled at $10–$50/mo per tenant).
* **The Split:**
  * **70% to the FDE Developer** (compensating them for their domain dataset and fine-tuning investment).
  * **30% to AVOS** (covering Cloudflare edge compute and platform margin).

---

# Part 3: The 2-Layer Hybrid Linter Engine (`@avos/linter`)

When an agent proposes an edit to a document, spreadsheet, or customs form, it passes through **two non-generative verification rings** before reaching the user's canvas.

```
                      THE HYBRID VERIFICATION PIPELINE
                                     │
                     [Agent Emits Candidate AST Patch]
                                     │
                                     ▼
        ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
        LAYER 1: DETERMINISTIC WASM / RUST LINTERS (<1ms | $0.00)
        • Math Balance Invariants ($A = L + E$)
        • Defined-Term Orphan & Circularity Checks
        • Cross-Reference Graph Traversal (Section 4.2 existence)
        • XML / EDI / JSON-Schema Structural Validation
        ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                                     │
                        Did Layer 1 throw an Error?
                                     │
                      ┌──────────────┴──────────────┐
                      ▼ YES                         ▼ NO (Passed)
        ┌───────────────────────────┐ ═══════════════════════════════════════════
        │ Immediate Linter Failure  │ LAYER 2: JEV SEMANTIC SENTRIES (40ms | $0.00004)
        │ Return diagnostic to Agent│ • Statutory Carve-Out Verifications (Delaware)
        └───────────────────────────┘ • Clinical Oncology Toxicity Boundaries (ASCO)
                                      • Uncapped Indemnification & Liability Risk
                                      • Tone, Privacy & PII Compliance Rules
                                      ═══════════════════════════════════════════
                                                    │
                                      Did Layer 2 JEV Confidence >= 0.85?
                                                    │
                                      ┌─────────────┴─────────────┐
                                      ▼ YES (Compliant)           ▼ NO (Rule Violation)
                       ┌────────────────────────────┐ ┌────────────────────────────┐
                       │ RENDER GHOST DIFF          │ │ DIAGNOSTIC RETURNED        │
                       │ • Canvas updates green/red │ │ • Diagnostic sent to agent │
                       │ • User clicks Cmd + Y      │ │ • Auto-correct (Cap: N=2)  │
                       └────────────────────────────┘ └────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# Part 4: The Developer SDK Implementation (`@avos/sdk`)

Here is how an FDE defines a complete vertical studio with custom Wasm linters and fine-tuned JEV sentries:

```typescript
// foundry/templates/maritime-terminal/src/index.ts
import { 
  defineTemplate, 
  ProjectKind, 
  defineWasmLinter, 
  defineJevSentry 
} from "@avos/sdk";
import { CustomsDeclarationCanvas } from "./ui/CustomsDeclarationCanvas";
import { DemurrageActionCard } from "./ui/DemurrageActionCard";

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 1. LAYER 1 LINTER: DETERMINISTIC WASM CHECK (Port Authority XML Schema)
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
export const EdiSchemaLinter = defineWasmLinter({
  id: "linter_edi_xml_schema",
  name: "Rotterdam Port EDI XSD Validator",
  targetKind: "DualPane.Dossier",
  validateNode: (node) => {
    if (node.type !== "customs_declaration_xml") return null;
    
    // Runs compiled Rust/Wasm XML Schema validation in <1ms
    const isValid = window.avosWasm.validateXSD(node.content, "rotterdam_v3.xsd");
    if (!isValid.success) {
      return [{
        severity: "error",
        nodeId: node.id,
        message: `Schema Violation: Missing mandatory element <${isValid.missingTag}>`,
        suggestedFix: { action: "insert", tag: isValid.missingTag }
      }];
    }
    return null;
  }
});

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 2. LAYER 2 LINTER: FINE-TUNED JEV SENTRY (Dangerous Goods Compliance)
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
export const HazardousCargoSentry = defineJevSentry({
  id: "sentry_hazmat_compliance",
  name: "IMO Dangerous Goods Sentry (Fine-Tuned)",
  targetKind: "DualPane.Dossier",
  // Points to FDE's proprietary fine-tuned JEV model checkpoint in Cloudflare
  modelCheckpoint: "cf-workers-ai/fde_portmaster_hazmat_modernbert_v2",
  
  evaluator: {
    question: "Does this cargo description contain materials classified as Class 1-9 Dangerous Goods without an attached UN Safety Data Sheet (SDS)?",
    minConfidence: 0.90
  },
  
  severity: "error",
  diagnosticMessage: "IMO Compliance Alert: Undeclared hazardous cargo detected without UN Safety Data Sheet.",
  royaltyPerCheckUSD: 0.0001 // 70% paid to FDE
});

// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 3. TEMPLATE DEFINITION
// -------------------------------------------------------------------------
export default defineTemplate({
  id: "maritime-customs-terminal",
  name: "PortMaster Customs Workbench",
  version: "2.1.0",
  kind: ProjectKind.DualPane.Dossier,

  canvas: {
    title: "Bill of Lading & Customs Workbench",
    component: CustomsDeclarationCanvas,
    defaultWidth: "60%"
  },

  linters: [
    EdiSchemaLinter,         // Layer 1: Wasm
    HazardousCargoSentry     // Layer 2: Fine-Tuned JEV
  ],

  genUI: {
    DemurrageCard: DemurrageActionCard
  },

  agent: {
    systemPrompt: "You are an automated port terminal broker. Parse manifests, verify HS-Codes, and generate EDI declarations.",
    maxLinterRetries: 2 // Hard limit to eliminate token-burn deadlocks
  }
});
```

### Summary: What This Architecture Solves

1. **Empowers FDEs with "The Foundry":** Developers build, test, and package complete vertical studios inside an integrated development workspace within AVOS.
2. **Eliminates Prompt-Linter Fragility:** Replaces brittle natural language prompts with a **Two-Layer Engine**—instant Wasm for mechanics and fine-tuned JEV sentries for natural language rules.
3. **Establishes Clear Platform Moats:** Developers can fine-tune specialized JEV encoders using Cloudflare infrastructure, earning a 70% share of validation traffic.
4. **Delivers High Operational Margins:** Enforcing a maximum of two self-correction retries and executing validation checks in $40\,\text{ms}$ at $\$0.00004$ per call maintains a **92%+ gross margin profile**.
### Part 1: Is All This Packaged Into Extensions?

**Yes.** In fact, this is the only way a modern operating system remains maintainable. 

An extension in AVOS is not just an API endpoint or a chat prompt. An **AVOS Extension Package** is a self-contained, version-controlled bundle that packages the **UI Canvas, the Linters, the JEV Sentries, the Database Schemas, and the Tools** into a single folder:

```
extensions/ext_portmaster_customs/
├── manifest.json              # Scopes, permissions, and egress whitelist
├── blueprint.json             # Layout kind, initial DAG, and template rules
├── migrations/                # SQLite table schemas automatically partitioned
│   └── 001_customs.sql
├── linters/
│   ├── edi_schema.wasm        # Layer 1: Compiled Rust/Wasm deterministic validator
│   └── hazmat_sentry.json     # Layer 2: Fine-tuned JEV classification schema
├── canvas/                    # React/Svelte components mounted onto the Stage
│   ├── CustomsWorkbench.tsx
│   └── TariffGrid.tsx
├── widgets/                   # In-chat GenUI action cards (Approval cards, forms)
│   └── DemurrageActionCard.tsx
└── index.ts                   # Lifecycle hooks (onLoad, onWhatsAppMessage, onAction)
```

When an FDE exports a project from **The Foundry**, it compiles into this exact `.zip` or R2 artifact. When a business or consumer installs it, the AVOS kernel unpacks the Wasm linters, registers the JEV sentry checkpoint, mounts the canvas, and partitions the SQLite tables in **under 200 milliseconds**.

You have designed the layout, the runtime, the linter layers, and the marketplace economics. But if you begin building tomorrow, you will hit **six operational and distributed systems gaps** that we have not yet addressed:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                   THE 6 MISSING OPERATIONAL PILLARS                                    │

├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
```
