### Gap 1: The Omnichannel Session Bridge (WhatsApp $\leftrightarrow$ Desktop)

#### The Problem:
You promised that users can operate on WhatsApp with zero passwords, but also use the high-powered Desktop Stage Canvas for complex tasks.
* If a lawyer texts on WhatsApp, how do they sit down at their Mac and open that exact deal room **without typing a password, creating an account, or re-authenticating?**

#### The Missing Architecture: The Cryptographic "Desk-Drop" Handshake
1. **The Magic Link Text:** The user texts their AVOS WhatsApp bot: `"desktop"` or `"open canvas"`.
2. **Ephemeral Ed25519 Token:** The user's `SpaceDO` generates a cryptographically signed, single-use URL containing a 64-character token with a **90-second expiry**:
   `https://avos.run/s/mergers_deal_a?token=ed25519_9f81a...`
3. **Instant Browser Hydration:** Clicking the link on desktop sets a secure HTTP-only session cookie, establishes the WebSocket connection to `SpaceDO`, and mounts the Canvas in **sub-100ms** without asking for a password or username.
4. **QR Code Alternative:** On the desktop login screen, AVOS displays a dynamic QR code. The user snaps it with their phone camera, WhatsApp authenticates the webhook, and the desktop browser instantly unlocks.

---

### Gap 2: The "Blank-Slate" Churn Trap (Hydration Seeds)

#### The Problem:
When a customer buys an FDE's "Maritime Customs Terminal" or "Dental Clinic Sentry," the worst user experience is opening the canvas to an empty table and a blank chat prompt. Non-technical users freeze when faced with an empty screen.

#### The Missing Architecture: Blueprint Hydration Seeds
The `blueprint.json` specification must include mandatory **Demo Hydration Data**:

```json
// blueprint.json
{
  "hydration_seed": {
    "welcome_card": {
      "title": "Welcome to PortMaster Customs! 🚢",
      "description": "I have loaded 2 sample Bills of Lading so you can see how clearance works."
    },
    "preloaded_artifacts": [
      { "path": "/workspace/demo/BoL_Sample_Seafood.pdf", "source_r2": "seeds/bol_sample.pdf" }
    ],
    "sqlite_seed_script": "INSERT INTO customs_manifests (id, container, status) VALUES ('DEMO-1', 'MSKU-908124', 'READY_FOR_AUDIT');",
    "onboarding_action_pills": [
      { "label": "📄 Audit Sample Bill of Lading", "action": "RUN_SAMPLE_AUDIT" },
      { "label": "📱 Connect My WhatsApp Number", "action": "OPEN_WHATSAPP_MODAL" }
    ]
  }
}
```
When installed, the space is **instantly populated with interactive, pre-calculated demo assets**. The user can click a button on second one and watch the linters, canvas diffs, and agents work before uploading real confidential data.

---

### Gap 3: The Bi-Directional Race Hazard (WhatsApp vs. Desktop)

#### The Problem:
* At 2:00 PM, an associate is on the Desktop Canvas editing an M&A disclosure schedule.
* At 2:00 PM, the Managing Partner on the train sends a WhatsApp voice note: *"Change the indemnification cap in Section 8 to $5M and exclude tax claims."*
* If the WhatsApp webhook runs an agent mutation that touches the document while the associate is actively typing, **the document state will corrupt or produce a merge collision.**

#### The Missing Architecture: Node-Level Mutex Leases
1. **Node Granularity:** Documents in `ArtifactDoc` are trees of semantic blocks (`clause_1`, `clause_2`, `table_row_4`).
2. **The Lease Protocol:** 
   * When an associate places their cursor inside Clause 8 on desktop, the client sends a `CLAIM_NODE_LEASE(clause_8)` event over WebSockets to `SpaceDO`.
   * When the partner's WhatsApp voice note is transcribed and the agent wants to mutate Clause 8, the kernel checks the lease:
   * **If leased by human:** The agent pauses, waits for the human cursor to exit Clause 8 (or times out after 15s), and emits an inline proposal banner:  
     `"⚡ Partner requested a $5M cap via WhatsApp. [Click to Apply Patch]"` instead of overwriting the text.

---

### Gap 4: The Marketplace Regression Shield (Agentic CI/CD)

#### The Problem:
An FDE creates an M&A template used by 50 active law firms. The FDE updates their JEV sentry to version 2.0 to catch a new regulatory rule, but the update accidentally makes the linter too strict, breaking standard purchase agreements across all 50 law firms.

#### The Missing Architecture: The Foundry Test Harness
Before a template or linter update can be published to the public marketplace, the AVOS Foundry must enforce **Automated Regression Testing**:

```
                       THE FOUNDRY COMPILER TEST GATE
                                     │
      [FDE clicks: "Publish Blueprint Update to Marketplace"]
                                     │
                                     ▼
      ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
      │ 1. RUN REGRESSION TEST SUITE (Inside Cloudflare Workflow)   │
      │ • Replays 20 historical gold-standard contract fixtures.    │
      │ • Checks that previously compliant clauses still PASS.      │
      │ • Checks that violating clauses are accurately CAUGHT.      │
      └──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                      Did any regression occur?
                                     │
                      ┌──────────────┴──────────────┐
                      ▼ YES                         ▼ NO (100% Pass)
       ┌────────────────────────────┐ ┌────────────────────────────┐
       │ PUBLISH BLOCKED            │ │ DEPLOY UPDATE VIA BLUEPRINT│
       │ Detailed diagnostic report │ │ Semantic Version Bump      │
       │ shown in Foundry Workbench │ │ (v1.2.0 live on R2)        │
       └────────────────────────────┘ └────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### Gap 5: The Human Escalation Escape Hatch

#### The Problem:
What happens when the linter throws an error, the agent tries twice to fix it, fails both times (hitting the $N=2$ safety cap), and the customer is stuck on a critical Friday afternoon deadline?

#### The Missing Architecture: The FDE Support Escalation Bridge
1. **The Deadlock Trap:** When an agent and linter deadlock, the system marks the task as `REQUIRES_HUMAN_INTERVENTION`.
2. **The Multi-Channel Alarm:**
   * An urgent notification appears on the desktop canvas.
   * If the user is on mobile, WhatsApp pings them with an escalation card.
3. **The FDE Marketplace Support Bounty (Optional):**
   * If configured in the blueprint, the business owner can click:  
     `[ 🛠️ Request Emergency FDE Support ($50 Escrow) ]`.
   * A notification pings the FDE who built the template. The FDE accepts, receives a temporary read-only view of the linter diagnostic, fixes the rule or document, and collects the escrow bounty.

---

### Gap 6: The Automated Settlement Daemon

#### The Problem:
We agreed on a complex economic model:
* Consumer plans: Fixed upfront template sales.
* B2B Studios: 12-month decaying royalties ($40\% \to 20\% \to 5\%$).
* Fine-tuned JEV Sentries: $70\%$ micro-payout per verification check.
* Who calculates this? If you do this manually on spreadsheets, your accounting team will collapse under thousands of micro-transactions.

#### The Missing Architecture: The Cloudflare Settlement Workflow
At the end of every billing cycle, a **Cloudflare Scheduled Workflow** runs a nightly reconciliation job:

```
[Nightly Cloudflare Scheduled Workflow: 00:00 UTC]
                       │
                       ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 1. QUERY SPACE DO LEDGERS (`ledger.sqlite`)                 │

 │ • Calculates client account age (Determines 40%, 20%, or 5%)│
 └─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 2. COMPOSE PAYOUT PAYLOAD                                   │
 │ • Aggregates total earnings per FDE Stripe Connect ID.      │
 └─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 3. DISPATCH VIA STRIPE CONNECT TRANSFERS API                │
 │ • Automates global bank transfers with zero human manual    │
 │   calculations. Generates 1099/tax certificates.            │
 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

I gave my engineer friend this file and made me the following so consider the following as the mappings of an engineer that did not have full contecxt of naelao but they did alright though so as you analyse this refer to our codebase so that you wont consider or implement somethe that might not work


### 1. Token Calculation: Gemma Embeddings vs. Tokenizer Reality

There is a critical distinction between **Tokenizers** and **Embedding Models**:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        TOKENIZER vs. EMBEDDING MODEL: THE DIFFERENCE                   │
├─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┤
│ Dimension           │ Tokenizer (What counts tokens)  │ Embedding Model (EmbeddingGemma)│
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Input**           │ Raw String ("Hello world")      │ Text or Token IDs              │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Output**          │ Array of Integer IDs (`[9906, 1917]`)│ High-dimensional vector array  │
│                     │ $\to$ **Length = Token Count**  │ (`float32[768]`)               │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Purpose**         │ Splitting text into model bytes │ Semantic search, vector search,│
│                     │ and calculating billing/context │ cosine similarity, RAG ranking │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Execution Speed** │ **Microseconds (< 0.1ms)**      │ **10ms – 150ms** (Neural net)  │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Compute Cost**    │ $0.00 (Pure CPU table lookup)   │ GPU/NPU Matrix Multiplication  │
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### Why You Do NOT Use EmbeddingGemma for Token Counting:
Google's **EmbeddingGemma** is an embedding model designed to convert sentences into mathematical vectors for vector databases like Cloudflare Vectorize. If you called EmbeddingGemma just to find out how many tokens were in a chat message, you would run a 300M+ parameter neural network across an NPU/GPU, burning milliseconds and memory just to measure string length.

#### How Naelao Calculates Tokens in the Right Sidebar Stats Tab:
AVOS uses a **two-tier counting pipeline**:

1. **Pre-flight Estimation (Client / Edge V8):**
   * Uses a WebAssembly-compiled BPE tokenizer (e.g., `@lenml/tokenizers` or `tiktoken-wasm` running inside the V8 Isolate in **0.05ms**).
   * For streaming token meters where microsecond updates matter, it applies a calibrated heuristic:
     $$\text{Estimated Tokens} = \lceil \text{Characters} / 3.85 \rceil$$
2. **Authoritative Provider Usage (Post-flight Ledger):**
   * Every LLM response (Cloudflare Workers AI, Anthropic, Cerebras, Groq, OpenAI) returns exact usage metadata in the completion payload:
     ```json
     {
       "usage": {
         "prompt_tokens": 1420,
         "completion_tokens": 312,
         "total_tokens": 1732
       }
     }
     ```
   * The `NaelaoAgentDO` extracts these exact numbers and commits them to `stats.sqlite` in the space. The UI stats tab subscribes to this table over WebSocket.

---

### 2. Agent UI Presentation: Clean CLI/Bash Streams vs. JSON Blobs

You noted a major UI defect: **the agent UI currently dumps `{ "tool": "Bash", ... }` JSON objects instead of looking like a true terminal workstation.**

In production AI workstations (like Cursor, Claude Code, or Windsurf), the user should never see raw JSON tool envelopes unless they explicitly expand a debug drawer. Tool calls should render as **first-class terminal blocks**.

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE RAW JSON TRAP vs. NAELAO CLI UI                       │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ❌ WHAT USERS HATE (Raw JSON Blob):                                                    │
│ {                                                                                      │
│   "tool": "Bash",                                                                      │
│   "args": { "cmd": "npm test tests/billing.test.ts" },                                 │
│   "output": "PASS tests/billing.test.ts\n  ✓ verifies Stripe CAS (12ms)\n..."          │
│ }                                                                                      │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ✅ WHAT NAELAO ACTUALLY RENDERS (Interactive Monospace Terminal Block):                │
│                                                                                        │
│ ┌─ 🐚 terminal: bash (Tier 1 VFS) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ $ npm test tests/billing.test.ts                                                   │ │
│ │                                                                                    │ │
│ │ RUNS  tests/billing.test.ts                                                        │ │
│ │ ✓ verifies Stripe CAS atomic write (14ms)                                          │ │
│ │ ✓ prevents race condition under concurrent leases (28ms)                           │ │
│ │                                                                                    │ │
│ │ Test Suites: 1 passed, 1 total                                                     │ │
│ │ Tests:       2 passed, 2 total                                                     │ │
│ │ Snapshots:   0 total                                                               │ │
│ │ Time:        0.412 s                                                               │ │
│ │                                                                                    │ │
│ │ [✓ Exit Code: 0] • [Duration: 412ms] • [Tier: 2 Dynamic Loader]                   │ │
│ └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### The UI Implementation Contract:
1. **The Streaming Terminal Widget (`TerminalStreamBlock.tsx`):**
   * Uses an ANSI-to-HTML parser (or lightweight `xterm.js` headless canvas) inside the message stream.
   * As the model calls `Bash(cmd)`, the UI displays the `$ <command>` prompt line in green.
   * `stdout` and `stderr` stream in real time as monospace text with syntax highlighting and ANSI colors (red for errors, green for test passes).
2. **The 1,500-Token Strands Fold:**
   * If a command outputs $> 1,500$ tokens (e.g., massive `npm install` logs), the terminal box automatically collapses with a visual banner:
     ```
     ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
     │ ⚠️ Output exceeded 1,500 tokens (18,420 lines hidden).                          │
     │ 📄 Full log written to: /.avos/tmp/tool_out_49.log [Open in Right Canvas]       │
     └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
     ```
3. **GenUI Cards vs. Terminal Blocks:**
   * **Terminal Blocks** are for execution, scripts, tests, and bash commands.
   * **GenUI Cards** are strictly reserved for high-level interactive user decisions (e.g., `[Approve Refund]`, `[Merge Pull Request]`, `[Pick Model]`).

---

### 3. Planner-Worker Swarm, Entity Definition & Progressive Skills

A planner is not just a chatbot with sub-prompts. It is an **Orchestration Kernel** managing a swarm of distinct functional entities.

```
                                  SWARM ORCHESTRATION TOPOLOGY
                                                │
                                                ▼
                         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                         │   DEPTH 1: PLANNER ORCHESTRATOR (`SpaceDO`)  │
                         │   Model: Claude 3.7 / o3-mini                │
                         │   • Stripped of direct file edit tools       │
                         │   • Equipped with Swarm Management Skills    │
                         └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────┘
                                                │
                         ┌──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                             ▼
          ┌─────────────────────────────┐               ┌─────────────────────────────┐
          │ TYPE A: NAELAO SUBAGENTS    │               │ TYPE B: CUSTOM WORKERS      │
          │ (`NaelaoAgentDO` Fast Actor)│               │ (Cloudflare Dynamic Workflow)│
          ├─────────────────────────────┤               ├─────────────────────────────┤
          │ • In-Memory V8 Isolate (<30s│               │ • Durable Cloudflare Engine │
          │ • Configured via UI / JSON  │               │ • Multi-step, runs for days │
          │ • Scoped manifest & tools   │               │ • Written via `@naelao/sdk` │
          │ • Atomic leaf tasks         │               │ • Untrusted custom code jail│
          └─────────────────────────────┘               └─────────────────────────────┘
```

#### The Two Distinct Worker Classes:
1. **Type A: Naelao Subagents (`NaelaoAgentDO` Fast Actors)**
   * Built for sub-30-second atomic execution.
   * Can be configured via the Naelao UI or generated on the fly via a JSON manifest.
   * Safe to run directly inside the space isolate because its tool surface is constrained.
2. **Type B: Custom Workers (Cloudflare Dynamic Workflows)**
   * Built for long-running, asynchronous, durable execution (multi-hour ETLs, competitive worker races, scraping sweeps).
   * **Runs under Cloudflare Dynamic Workflows, NOT inside `NaelaoAgentDO` directly.** This ensures that if custom developer code loops infinitely, crashes, or attempts illegal memory operations, it is sandboxed away from the core workspace state.

#### Progressive Skill Loading (Context Cleanliness):
Dumping the documentation of 20 different skills into an agent's prompt burns 15,000+ tokens before the agent does anything.

AVOS enforces **Progressive Skill Disclosure**:

```
[Agent System Prompt: Skill Manifest Index Only (<250 tokens)]
├── `orchestrate_swarm`: "Spawn, link, and race multiple workers across tasks."
├── `build_workflow_worker`: "Compile a custom dynamic workflow worker using @naelao/sdk."
├── `local_tunnel`: "Dispatch bash commands to connected on-prem or remote machines."
└── `ast_grep`: "Perform syntactic search and replacement on source trees."
```

When the Planner needs to build a new worker or run a swarm, it calls:
```bash
skill use build_workflow_worker
```
The runtime injects the operational manual for that specific skill into context for that single turn, executes the action, and clears the context buffer on task completion.

#### The Workflow Builder Skill in Action:
When the Planner needs a brand-new worker that doesn't exist, it delegates to a builder script or writes the Dynamic Workflow code directly:

```typescript
// Generated dynamically by Planner via @naelao/sdk:
import { WorkflowEntrypoint, WorkflowStep, WorkflowEvent } from "cloudflare:workers";
import { NaelaoContext } from "@naelao/sdk";

export class CustomEtlWorker extends WorkflowEntrypoint {
  async run(event: WorkflowEvent<{ sitemapUrl: string }>, step: WorkflowStep) {
    const ctx = new NaelaoContext(event.payload);

    // Step 1: Crawl URLs
    const pages = await step.do("fetch_sitemap", async () => {
      return await ctx.tools.browse(event.payload.sitemapUrl);
    });

    // Step 2: Sleep and respect rate limits ($0 compute)
    await step.sleep("rate_limit_cooloff", "10 seconds");

    // Step 3: Write extracted records to Space SQLite
    await step.do("persist_records", async () => {
      await ctx.storage.sqlite.execute("INSERT INTO crawled_pages VALUES (?)", [pages]);
    });
  }
}
```

---

### 4. Local Machine Access: Pluggable Skill & Multi-Tunnel CLI

The local machine must **never be the hardcoded, single default environment**. If it is, the agent breaks when the user is on mobile or offline, and cannot access multiple enterprise machines.

Instead, local machine access is modeled as a **pluggable CLI tool and skill: `tunnel`**.

```
                       MULTI-TUNNEL APPLIANCE FABRIC
                                     │
                     [Agent calls `Bash` in Naelao]
                                     │
        ┌────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┐
        ▼                            ▼                            ▼
$ tunnel exec macbook-m3    $ tunnel exec gpu-rig-01    $ tunnel exec prod-db-proxy
"npm run build"             "python3 train_lora.py"     "psql -c 'SELECT 1'"
        │                            │                            │
        ▼                            ▼                            ▼
[LocalTunnelDO: Tunnel A]   [LocalTunnelDO: Tunnel B]   [LocalTunnelDO: Tunnel C]
(User's Laptop)             (On-Premises Linux Box)     (Private Cloud Bastion)
```

#### How the Agent Uses Tunnels via Bash:
1. When an FDE or developer runs `npx @naelao/bridge connect --name macbook-m3`, an authenticated WebSocket reverse tunnel links their machine to `LocalTunnelDO`.
2. The agent discovers available tunnels using standard bash utilities:
   ```bash
   $ tunnel list
   AVAILABLE TUNNELS:
   • macbook-m3       (Online, macOS arm64, Load: 12%)
   • gpu-box-rtx4090  (Online, Ubuntu 24.04, CUDA 12.4, Free VRAM: 22GB)
   ```
3. The agent targets any machine explicitly:
   ```bash
   $ tunnel exec gpu-box-rtx4090 "nvidia-smi --query-gpu=utilization.gpu --format=csv"
   ```
4. **Security Isolation:** If a tunnel drops or is revoked, the agent simply loses access to that remote appliance; the core Naelao space continues operating uninterrupted on Cloudflare edge.

---

### 5. Testing & Persistence Matrix (6-Tier Ladder & R2)

To ensure files, SQLite state, and artifacts persist across reboots, isolates, containers, and R2, AVOS implements a **Deterministic Verification Matrix**:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE 6-TIER PERSISTENCE VERIFICATION                       │
├──────────────┬─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Tier         │ Persistence Engine          │ Exact Failure / Test Verification Check   │
├──────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Tier 1**   │ Durable Object SQLite VFS   │ **Reboot Invariance Test:** Write file via│
│              │ (`@cloudflare/computer`)    │ `Edit`, force `state.storage.abort()`,    │
│              │                             │ wake DO up; file SHA-256 must match.      │
├──────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Tier 2**   │ Dynamic Workers / Pyodide   │ **Memory Boundary Leak Test:** Pass 10MB  │
│              │ In-Memory V8 Isolate        │ dataframe into Pyodide Wasm; verify RAM is│
│              │                             │ garbage-collected after isolate completes.│
├──────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Tier 3**   │ Cloudflare Workflows        │ **Durable Step Sleep Test:** Workflow     │
│              │ Step Checkpointing          │ sleeps 7 days; verify step parameters are │
│              │                             │ restored from R2 without loss.            │
├──────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Tier 4/5** │ Kitesurf & Headless Chromium│ **Artifact Disk Persistence Test:** Save  │
│              │ Headless Browser Engines    │ screenshot `screen_01.png`; verify it is  │
│              │                             │ readable by Tier 1 `Read` tool in <2ms.   │
├──────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Tier 6**   │ Cloudflare Edge Containers  │ **ArtifactFS FUSE Sync Test:** Container  │
│              │ over ArtifactFS FUSE        │ writes `dist/bundle.js`; verify diff lands│
│              │                             │ in DO SQLite Git repository before sleep. │
├──────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Storage**  │ R2 Pointer-LFS Storage      │ **Large File Eviction Test:** Ingest 50MB │
│              │ (Large File Offload)        │ PDF; verify DO memory stays <30MB while   │
│              │                             │ R2 pointer resolves signed streaming URL. │
└──────────────┴─────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### Automated End-to-End Persistence Test Script (`test_ladder_persistence.ts`):
```typescript
import { describe, it, expect } from "vitest";
import { NaelaoTestHarness } from "@naelao/sdk/testing";

describe("6-Tier Compute Ladder & R2 Persistence Suite", () => {
  it("verifies write persistence across Tier 1 VFS and Tier 6 Container", async () => {
    const space = await NaelaoTestHarness.createSpace("test-space");

    // 1. Write file in Tier 1 (In-Memory DO SQLite)
    await space.bash("echo 'export const APP_VERSION = 2;' > src/version.ts");
    expect(await space.read("src/version.ts")).toContain("APP_VERSION = 2");

    // 2. Promote to Tier 6 Container to build native artifact
    const buildResult = await space.bash("npm run build");
    expect(buildResult.exitCode).toBe(0);

    // 3. Force container micro-sleep (simulate idle termination)
    await space.hypervisor.terminateContainer();

    // 4. Verify build output persisted through ArtifactFS back into DO SQLite
    const distExists = await space.bash("ls -la dist/index.js");
    expect(distExists.stdout).toContain("dist/index.js");

    // 5. Verify Large File offload to R2 Pointer-LFS
    const largeBlob = new Uint8Array(10 * 1024 * 1024); // 10MB
    await space.fs.writeFile("large_dataset.bin", largeBlob);
    
    // Check that DO SQLite only stores the pointer, not the raw bytes
    const dbRecord = await space.sqlite.execute(
      "SELECT storage_type, pointer_r2_key FROM files WHERE path = 'large_dataset.bin'"
    );
    expect(dbRecord[0].storage_type).toBe("R2_POINTER");
  });
});
```

### 6. WhatsApp Architecture, Phone Topology & Billing

You asked: *"For payment, is it $x per space? How do WhatsApp users join? How do we separate personal assistants from customer-facing bots? How do we prevent template pricing disasters?"*

Here is the clean operational design:

```
                              THE NAELAO PHONE & TENANCY MAP
                                             │
      ┌──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┐
      ▼                                                                             ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐                 ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. THE OWNER'S PERSONAL HUB               │                 │ 2. DEDICATED CUSTOMER-FACING BOTS         │
│ (Uses Naelao Shared WhatsApp Line)        │                 │ (Uses Dedicated Phone Number Add-On)      │
├───────────────────────────────────────────┤                 ├───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • Owner texts: +1-800-NAELAO              │                 │ • Customers text: +1-555-MIAMI-LAW        │
│ • Identified by caller's phone number     │                 │ • Runs on dedicated WhatsApp Business API │
│ • Connects to **Root Personal Space**     │                 │ • Operates in **Public Client Project**   │
│ • Routes to Subspace Assistants internally│                 │ • Never touches owner's private chat      │
└───────────────────────────────────────────┘                 └───────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### A. How the Personal Assistant Handles Multiple Businesses (Subspaces):
* A user texts Naelao's shared business line from `+1234567890`.
* The platform provisions their **Root Personal Space** with their **Executive Chief of Staff**.
* When the user buys templates (e.g. "Tax Sentry" and "Logistics Tracker"), they install as **Subspaces** inside their account.
* **The Single-Chat Experience:**
  * User texts: *"Did the driver pick up container 402, and what's my tax liability this week?"*
  * The Executive Chief of Staff decomposes the intent:
    * Delegates container check to `Subspace: Logistics`.
    * Delegates tax query to `Subspace: Tax`.
    * Merges both answers into a single WhatsApp reply:
      > *"Container 402 was picked up 20 mins ago (Pier 4). Estimated tax liability this week is $1,420."*
* The user never has to register multiple numbers to manage multiple businesses.

#### B. Separating the Customer-Facing Bot:
* If the user runs a law firm or freight agency, **customers must not text the owner's personal assistant**.
* The company provisions a **Dedicated Virtual Number** ($15/month add-on via WhatsApp Cloud API / Twilio).
* This customer bot runs in an isolated public project. When customers text it, their inquiries appear in the owner's desktop CRM inbox (`ext_crm_core`), keeping public inquiries separate from personal assistant operations.

#### C. The Simple Billing & Resource Class Model:
To ensure predictable billing while preventing compute abuse:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE 3-TIER SIMPLE BILLING TIERS                           │
├──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Plan                 │ Base Subscription    │ Included Resource Bundle                 │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Consumer**         │ **$6.99 / month**    │ 1 Personal Space, Shared WhatsApp Number,│
│                      │                      │ 500 Gas Credits ($5.00 compute value).   │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Pro / Solo SMB**   │ **$29 / month**      │ 1 Main Space + 3 Subspaces, Desktop UI,  │
│                      │                      │ 2,500 Gas Credits ($25 compute value).   │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Enterprise**       │ **$199 / month**     │ Unlimited Subspaces, Dedicated EU/US DLS,│
│                      │                      │ 20,000 Gas Credits ($200 compute value). │
└──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### Protecting Against Marketplace Underpricing (`resource_class` Quotas):
* **The Rule: Upfront purchases bundle Month 1 SaaS.**  
  If an FDE sells a template for **$55**, checkout breaks down as:
  * **$20 $\to$ Naelao** (covers Month 1 platform SaaS).
  * **$35 $\to$ FDE** (template creator profit) + 100% of any upfront custom setup fee.
* **The Resource Class Floor:**  
  The `blueprint.json` schema enforces minimum subscription floors based on resource demands:

```json
// blueprint.json
{
  "id": "tpl_maritime_edi_customs",
  "resource_class": "heavy_compute", // Requires OCR, continuous workflows, high memory
  "billing_policy": {
    "minimum_monthly_subscription": 150.00,
    "one_time_template_price": 499.00,
    "requires_dedicated_number": true
  }
}
```
If an FDE attempts to set a $30/month price on an enterprise template marked `heavy_compute`, the Naelao marketplace compiler **rejects the publication**. This guarantees that high-compute verticals cannot be sold at rates that compromise edge infrastructure margins.

---

### 7. Deeply Specialized Models (The Ben Affleck / InterPositive Moat)

Ben Affleck’s startup **InterPositive** was acquired by Netflix for **$587M** because it solved a specific problem: **it did not try to prompt generalist LLMs to edit Hollywood movies.** It trained proprietary neural rendering models on a film's daily camera footage to handle continuity fixes, lighting consistency, and relighting.

What happens when an FDE or company on Naelao needs a **deeply specialized model** (e.g. custom visual continuity, dental 3D alignment, audio stem extraction) rather than standard LLMs?

```
                    SPECIALIZED MODEL PIPELINE ON NAELAO
                                    │
                                    ▼
       ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
       │ 1. UPFRONT CLIENT TRAINING PHASE (Client Billed Upfront)    │
       │ • Client pays FDE: $5,000 – $25,000 Custom Training Retainer│
       │ • Client pays Compute: $500 – $2,000 Rented GPU Training Run│
       └────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┘
                                    │
                                    ▼
       ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
       │ 2. MODEL ARTIFACT PACKAGING (The Model Container)           │
       │ • Checkpoints (.safetensors, ONNX, GGUF) stored in R2.      │
       │ • Registered in `blueprint.json` under `specialized_models`.│
       └────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┘
                                    │
                                    ▼
       ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
       │ 3. INFERENCE SERVING & DISPATCH                             │
       │ • Lightweight (<3B): Runs serverless on CF Workers AI.      │
       │ • Heavy Specialized Models (Diffusion/VFX):                 │
       │   Routed to On-Prem Client GPU via `LocalTunnelDO`          │
       │   OR Serverless GPU Clusters (RunPod, Baseten, Modal).      │
       └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### How the FDE Builds, Hosts, and Prices This:
1. **Who Pays for the Model Training?**
   * **The Client Pays the Upfront Compute:** FDEs do not train models out of pocket. The client pays an **Upfront Custom Training Fee** ($5,000–$25,000) that covers rented GPU training runs (on Lambda Labs, RunPod, or AWS Trainium).
2. **Serving Specialized Models at Scale:**
   * **Tier A (Lightweight Encoders & Heads, <1B params):** Executed directly on **Cloudflare Workers AI** at the edge.
   * **Tier B (Heavy Diffusion, VFX, 3D Mesh Models):** Executed on **On-Premise GPUs via `LocalTunnelDO`** (the client’s own workstations) or dispatched to serverless GPU backends (RunPod/Modal) via Naelao Egress Proxy.
3. **Per-Unit Consumption Pricing:**
   Instead of basic token pricing, the FDE configures **Unit-Based Billing**:
   * **Film Production Model:** **$0.05 per processed 8K video frame**.
   * **Orthodontic Dental Model:** **$2.50 per 3D tooth mesh realignment**.
   * **Seismic Geological Model:** **$10.00 per square km acoustic map parsed**.
4. **The Revenue Split on Specialized Models:**
   * **70% to FDE Developer** (compensates them for creating the dataset and model weights).
   * **30% to Naelao Platform** (covers edge routing, R2 bandwidth, and platform profit).

```
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **WhatsApp & Tenancy**               │ 1 User = 1 Shared WhatsApp Hub managing Subspaces;              │

├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Marketplace Protection**           │ Upfront sales bundle Month 1 SaaS; `resource_class` floors      │
│                                      │ prevent underpricing heavy compute verticals.                   │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Specialized Models**               │ Upfront client-funded training; served via serverless GPU or    │
│                                      │ on-prem `LocalTunnelDO`; priced per-unit (70% FDE / 30% Naelao).│
└──────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

# NAELAO / AVOS SUBSYSTEM ARCHITECTURAL BLUEPRINTS
## High-Density Structural, Operational, and Runtime Specifications

---

# Subsystem 1: The Cloudflare Artifacts & Git Mesh Map

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              CLOUDFLARE ARTIFACTS DISTRIBUTED GIT MESH                                 │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • Execution Target: `SpaceDO` V8 Isolate (Zig-WASM Engine) + ArtifactFS (Tier 6 FUSE)                  │
│ • State Boundaries: SQLite Virtual Filesystem (Text/Trees) + Cloudflare R2 (Pointer-LFS Blobs)        │
│ • Invariant Guarantee: Linear commit history, 10ms micro-branching, 0% memory bloat on binary assets.  │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

```
                                  GIT MESH TOPOLOGY & STORAGE ENGINE
                                                 │
                                                 ▼
                         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                         │      ZIG-COMPILED WASM GIT ENGINE (V8)       │
                         │      Runs inside `SpaceDO` memory isolate    │
                         │      Embedded memory footprint: ~4.2 MB      │
                         └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────┘
                                                │
                         ┌──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┐
                         ▼ (<256 KB text, trees, commits)              ▼ (≥256 KB binaries, models, PDFs)
          ┌─────────────────────────────┐               ┌─────────────────────────────┐
          │   DO SQLITE STORAGE ENGINE  │               │ CLOUDFLARE R2 POINTER-LFS   │
          │   • `git_commits`           │               │ • Immutable SHA-256 Objects │
          │   • `git_trees`             │               │ • Zero egress fee storage   │
          │   • `git_refs` (Branches)   │               │ • Byte-range streaming URLs │
          │   • `git_blobs_inline`      │               │ • Zero memory impact on DO  │
          └──────────────┬──────────────┘               └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                         │                                             │
                         └──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┘
                                                │
                                                ▼
                         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                         │        `ArtifactFS` (FUSE DAEMON BRIDGE)    │
                         │ Transparently mounts virtual Git repository │
                         │ into Tier 6 Docker / ToolSandbox Containers │
                         └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 1. The Zig-to-WASM Core Engine (`@naelao/git-wasm`)

To avoid memory limits inside Cloudflare Durable Objects (historically constrained to 128MB per isolate), Naelao does not compile `libgit2` (which pulls in POSIX libc overhead, POSIX thread dependencies, and bloated dynamic allocations). 

Instead, the core Git mesh runs a custom **Zig-compiled WebAssembly binary (`git_engine.wasm`)** targeting `wasm32-freestanding`:

* **Zero-Allocation Tree Parsing:** Git tree parsing is executed on a static 2MB memory arena. Tree objects are traversed directly in WASM memory without hydrating intermediate JavaScript objects.
* **Deterministic Object Hashing:** SHA-1 (legacy Git compatibility) and SHA-256 (Naelao modern tree mode) are implemented via optimized SIMD128 WASM instructions.
* **Storage Abstraction Layer:** WASM imports 4 primitive host functions from the Durable Object runtime:
  * `host_read_object(oid_ptr, out_buf_ptr)`
  * `host_write_object(type, buf_ptr, len)`
  * `host_read_ref(ref_name_ptr)`
  * `host_write_ref(ref_name_ptr, target_oid_ptr)`

---

### 2. Physical SQLite Schema for the Git Mesh

All Git refs, trees, text blobs, and commit DAGs live in the space’s local SQLite database:

```sql
-- The Git Mesh Physical Storage Table Layout
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS git_refs (
    name TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                 -- e.g. 'refs/heads/main', 'refs/heads/task/api_auth'
    target_oid TEXT NOT NULL,              -- 40-char SHA-1 or 64-char SHA-256
    updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS git_commits (
    oid TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    tree_oid TEXT NOT NULL,
    parent_oids TEXT NOT NULL,             -- JSON Array: ["oid1", "oid2"]
    author_name TEXT NOT NULL,
    author_email TEXT NOT NULL,
    timestamp INTEGER NOT NULL,
    message TEXT NOT NULL,
    -- NAELAO REASONING METADATA
    task_id TEXT,                          -- Relational link to SpaceDO.tasks
    turn_id TEXT,                          -- LLM Conversation Turn UUID
    reasoning_hash TEXT,                   -- Hash of agent chain-of-thought
    model_id TEXT                          -- e.g. "claude-3-7-sonnet" / "qwen-2.5-coder-32b"
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS git_objects (
    oid TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    type TEXT NOT NULL,                    -- 'tree', 'blob', 'commit', 'tag'
    size_bytes INTEGER NOT NULL,
    storage_class TEXT NOT NULL,           -- 'INLINE' (<256KB) or 'R2_POINTER' (>=256KB)
    content_inline BLOB,                   -- Compressed via ZSTD if INLINE
    r2_key TEXT,                           -- R2 object key if R2_POINTER
    sha256_hash TEXT NOT NULL
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_git_commits_task ON git_commits(task_id);
```

---

### 3. The 10ms Zero-Copy Micro-Branching Mechanism

When a Planner Orchestrator spins up an ephemeral leaf worker (or initiates a Speculative Race between multiple models), creating a Git branch takes **under 10 milliseconds**:

```typescript
// packages/git-mesh/src/branch.ts
export async function createMicroBranch(
  sqlite: SqliteClient,
  parentBranch: string,
  newBranchName: string
): Promise<string> {
  const start = performance.now();

  // 1. Resolve parent OID in 0.4ms
  const rows = await sqlite.execute<{ target_oid: string }>(
    "SELECT target_oid FROM git_refs WHERE name = ?",
    [`refs/heads/${parentBranch}`]
  );
  if (!rows.length) throw new Error(`Parent branch ${parentBranch} not found`);

  const baseOid = rows[0].target_oid;

  // 2. Insert new ref in 0.8ms (Zero copy of trees or files)
  await sqlite.execute(
    "INSERT INTO git_refs (name, target_oid, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?)",
    [`refs/heads/${newBranchName}`, baseOid, Date.now()]
  );

  const durationMs = performance.now() - start;
  // Performance invariant: Branch creation must complete in sub-5ms
  return baseOid;
}
```

Because Git uses an immutable directed acyclic graph (DAG) of trees and blobs, creating a branch simply writes a new 64-character pointer to the `git_refs` table. Zero files are copied.

---

### 4. `ArtifactFS`: Bidirectional FUSE Mounting into Containers

When an agent promotes execution to a Tier 6 Container (`ToolSandboxDO`), the container needs standard Linux POSIX access to the files (`/workspace`).

```
 [Tier 6 Linux Container / ToolSandboxDO]
                   │
                   ▼ (POSIX Read/Write: e.g. `npm test`)
          [/workspace/ FUSE Mount]
                   │
                   ▼ (FUSE Daemon in Rust)
       [Authenticated Binary WebSocket]
                   │
                   ▼ (RPC Stream in sub-8ms)
      [SpaceDO SQLite VFS & Git Engine]
```

1. **FUSE Interception:** A lightweight Rust FUSE client running inside the container intercepts all file system calls targeting `/workspace/`.
2. **Metadata Caching:** Directory listings (`readdir`, `getattr`) are cached inside container memory.
3. **Write-Through Staging:** When a build script modifies a file (`src/index.js`), the FUSE daemon streams the byte patch over a local Cloudflare service binding directly to `SpaceDO`.
4. **Git Isolation:** Internal agent directories (`/.avos/` and `/.naelao/`) are hidden from the container's FUSE mount, preventing rogue scripts from tampering with agent state, keys, or logs.

---

### 5. Reasoning-Aware `git blame` & Turn-by-Turn Time Machine

In conventional software development, `git blame` shows who changed a line of code:
```
c_9e12a4f (Alice 2026-03-12 10:14:02) const token = jwt.sign(claims);
```

In Naelao, every commit captures the **cognitive provenance of the AI agent**:

```bash
$ naelao-git blame --reasoning src/billing.ts:42
```

Outputs:
```
Commit:     c_9e12a4f32d81
Author:     Worker_BackendLead (Qwen 2.5 Coder 32B)
Timestamp:  2026-10-08 14:22:01 UTC
Task:       TASK-402 ("Implement atomic CAS in src/billing.ts")
Turn UUID:  turn_9a8df1bc-02
Prompt Hash:sha256:88a10b9...

REASONING CHAIN:
"Examining lines 38-45 of billing.ts. The original implementation used a non-atomic
read-then-write pattern which failed the concurrency test under 50 simultaneous workers. 
Refactored to execute an atomic Compare-And-Swap (CAS) update on the SQLite ledger 
with a SHA-256 checksum lock, matching the invariant defined in TASK-402."
```

### 1. Optimistic Concurrency Control (OCC) with SHA-256 CAS

When multiple agents or human users collaborate on the same relational document or task tree, race conditions are resolved via atomic **Compare-And-Swap (CAS)** operations in SQLite:

```sql
-- Atomic CAS State Update in ConcurrencyDO
UPDATE task_nodes 
SET 
    status = 'IN_PROGRESS',
    assigned_worker = 'worker_auth_dev',
    current_payload = :new_payload_json,
    version = version + 1,
    sha256_hash = :new_hash,
    updated_at = :now_ms
WHERE 
    id = :target_task_id 
    AND version = :expected_version 
    AND sha256_hash = :expected_hash;
```

If another worker committed a state transition during the inference window of the current worker, `changes()` returns `0`. The transaction fails immediately, the current worker's mutation is rolled back, and the worker re-hydrates the latest AST state before attempting a fresh mutation.

---

### 2. Node-Level Mutex Leases (Human vs. Agent Collision Defense)

To prevent an AI agent from overwriting a paragraph or table row that a human user is actively editing on the Stage Canvas:

```
[Human clicks on Clause 4.2 in Desktop Canvas]
                    │
                    ▼ (Dispatches WebSocket Lease Request)
      ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐
      │   `ConcurrencyDO.claimNodeLease()`       │
      │   • Node: `clause_4_2`                   │
      │   • Owner: `user_alice`                  │
      │   • Lease TTL: 15 Seconds (Heartbeated)  │
      └─────────────────────┬────────────────────┘
                            │
       ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
       ▼                                         ▼
[Human is Typing]                 [Agent attempts `Edit` on Clause 4.2]
Heartbeat refreshes lease.        1. Mutex detects active lease held by `user_alice`.
                                  2. Agent execution is PAUSED.
                                  3. Agent converts edit into translucent Ghost Diff:
                                     `⚡ Proposal: [Click to accept Agent Patch]`
```

```typescript
// packages/concurrency/src/mutex.ts
export interface NodeLease {
  nodeId: string;
  holderType: "HUMAN" | "AGENT";
  holderId: string;
  expiresAt: number;
}

export class ConcurrencyDO {
  private leases = new Map<string, NodeLease>();

  async claimLease(nodeId: string, holderId: string, holderType: "HUMAN" | "AGENT"): Promise<boolean> {
    const existing = this.leases.get(nodeId);
    const now = Date.now();

    if (existing && existing.expiresAt > now) {
      if (existing.holderId === holderId) {
        // Refresh heartbeat
        existing.expiresAt = now + 15000;
        return true;
      }
      // If an agent tries to steal from a human, reject unconditionally
      if (existing.holderType === "HUMAN" && holderType === "AGENT") {
        return false;
      }
    }

    // Grant 15-second exclusive lease
    this.leases.set(nodeId, {
      nodeId,
      holderType,
      holderId,
      expiresAt: now + 15000
    });
    return true;
  }
}
```

# Subsystem 3: The Zero-Leak Vault & Edge Security Proxy Map

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

```

```
               HOW ZERO-LEAK TOKEN INJECTION WORKS
                                 │
     [Agent writes script: `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer {{vault:STRIPE_KEY}}" ...`]
                                 │
                                 ▼
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ AGENT EXECUTION LAYER (V8 or Container)      │
         │ • Has ZERO access to decrypted Stripe Key.   │
         │ • Emits only logical token placeholder.      │
         └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────┘
                                │ (Outbound HTTP Request via Service Binding)
                                ▼

         │ `OutboundVaultProxyWorker` (Network Border)  │
         │ 1. Intercepts outgoing TLS request.          │
         │ 2. Validates domain against Whitelist.       │
         │ 3. Resolves AES-256 encrypted vault secret.  │
         │ 4. Replaces placeholder with real credential.│
         │ 5. Dispatches request to target external API.│
         └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────┘
                                │
                                ▼
         [External Target Gateway: `api.stripe.com`]
                                │
                                ▼ (Incoming API Response)
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ INBOUND SANITIZER SENTRY                     │
         │ • Scrubs reflected secrets from response.    │
         │ • Returns clean payload to Agent context.    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 1. The 3-Tier Connector Scoping Engine

Credentials are never global. They are resolved hierarchically based on the invoker's declared operational context:

```typescript
// packages/vault/src/types.ts
export type CredentialScope = "SPACE_SHARED" | "PROJECT_BOUND" | "USER_PERSONAL";

export interface VaultSecretMetadata {
  keyName: string;                     // e.g. "STRIPE_SECRET_KEY"
  scope: CredentialScope;
  boundEntityId: string;               // spaceId, projectId, or userId
  allowedDomains: string[];            // e.g. ["api.stripe.com"]
  rateLimitPerMinute: number;
  encryptedValueBlob: Uint8Array;      // AES-256-GCM encrypted
  initializationVector: Uint8Array;
}
```

* **`SPACE_SHARED`:** Managed by the Executive Space Owner (e.g., Company master QuickBooks or Twilio keys). Accessible to all child projects subject to Gatekeeper approval.
* **`PROJECT_BOUND`:** Scoped strictly to one project (e.g., the Stripe key for a specific e-commerce store). Isolated from other projects in the same space.
* **`USER_PERSONAL`:** Bound to a single employee’s session (e.g., Alice’s personal GitHub OAuth token). Used in Tier 4 Scratchpads; inaccessible to other colleagues.

---

### 2. The `WriteGuard` SQL Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) Inspector

When an agent executes SQL queries against `SpaceDO` SQLite via the `ext_ledger_core` or database tools, queries are intercepted by **WriteGuard** before reaching the database engine:

```typescript
// packages/security/src/writeguard.ts
import { parseSQL, ASTStatement } from "@naelao/sql-parser";

export function inspectAndHardenSQL(rawSql: string, spaceId: string): string {
  const ast = parseSQL(rawSql);

  for (const stmt of ast.statements) {
    // 1. Prohibit destructive schema mutations
    if (["DROP_TABLE", "ALTER_TABLE", "TRUNCATE"].includes(stmt.type)) {
      throw new SecurityException(`WriteGuard Violation: Destructive command ${stmt.type} is strictly blocked.`);
    }

    // 2. Enforce Mandatory Space Isolation Filter
    if (stmt.type === "SELECT" || stmt.type === "UPDATE" || stmt.type === "DELETE") {
      const hasSpaceIdPredicate = stmt.whereClause?.containsCondition("space_id", spaceId);
      if (!hasSpaceIdPredicate) {
        // Automatically inject tenant fence into AST
        stmt.whereClause.appendAndCondition("space_id", "=", spaceId);
      }
    }
  }

  return ast.toSQL();
}
```

Any attempt by a prompt-injected agent to run `SELECT * FROM users` is automatically rewritten at the AST level to `SELECT * FROM users WHERE space_id = 'space_current'`, preventing cross-tenant leakage.

### 1. Declarative Gadget Schema with Scoped Facets

A Gadget in Naelao is a micro-application bundled inside an Extension. It combines **backend logic, an isolated database facet, and MCP v2 tool registration**:

```typescript
// packages/sdk/src/gadget.ts
import { defineGadget, GadgetContext } from "@naelao/sdk";

export const EscrowLedgerGadget = defineGadget({
  id: "gadget_escrow_ledger",
  name: "M&A Escrow Ledger & Milestone Engine",
  version: "1.0.0",

  // 1. ISOLATED SQLITE SCHEMA (Automatically prefixed: `ext_escrow_*`)
  schema: `
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ext_escrow_milestones (
      id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
      title TEXT NOT NULL,
      amount_usd REAL NOT NULL,
      target_date INTEGER NOT NULL,
      is_released INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
      release_condition TEXT NOT NULL
    );
  `,

  // 2. EXPOSED ACTIONS (Invokable via Canvas, Agent Bash, or WhatsApp)
  actions: {
    releaseMilestone: async (ctx: GadgetContext, { milestoneId }: { milestoneId: string }) => {
      // Gatekeeper check: Ensure Human-In-The-Loop approval for transactions > $10,000
      const milestone = await ctx.storage.sqlite.executeOne<{ amount_usd: number }>(
        "SELECT amount_usd FROM ext_escrow_milestones WHERE id = ?",
        [milestoneId]
      );

      if (milestone.amount_usd > 10000) {
        const approved = await ctx.security.requestHumanApproval({
          title: "High-Value Escrow Release",
          description: `Authorizing release of $${milestone.amount_usd.toLocaleString()}`,
          riskScore: 0.85
        });
        if (!approved) throw new Error("Escrow release denied by executive director.");
      }

      await ctx.storage.sqlite.execute(
        "UPDATE ext_escrow_milestones SET is_released = 1 WHERE id = ?",
        [milestoneId]
      );

      // Publish update to active Desktop Canvas over WebSocket
      await ctx.canvas.broadcastPatch({ milestoneId, status: "RELEASED" });

      return { success: true, releasedAmount: milestone.amount_usd };
    }
  }
});
```

---

### 2. Automatic MCP v2 Translation Layer

The Gadget runtime automatically transforms the declared actions into **Model Context Protocol (MCP v2)** tools without boilerplate:

```
[Agent asks: "What tools are available for this deal room?"]

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ AUTO-COMPILED MCP v2 RESPONSE:                                              │
│ {                                                                           │
│   "name": "gadget_escrow_ledger_releaseMilestone",                          │
│   "description": "Releases escrowed funds for a verified milestone.",       │
│   "inputSchema": {                                                          │
│     "type": "object",                                                       │
│     "properties": {                                                         │
│       "milestoneId": { "type": "string", "description": "UUID of milestone"}│
│     },                                                                      │
│     "required": ["milestoneId"]                                             │
│   }                                                                         │
│ }                                                                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Agents interact with Gadgets through standard MCP tool calls or through the `$ gadget exec <id> <action>` CLI in terminal sessions.

### 1. The Dual-Thread Voice Desk Architecture

To prevent long audio transcripts from cluttering the business's core operational history, voice calls operate on a **Dual-Thread Separation Model**:

```
[ Incoming Voice Call from Client ]
               │
               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. SESSION THREAD (`voice_session_918` — Ephemeral)                          │
│    • Raw audio packets, real-time interruptions, and full STT transcripts.  │
│    • Preserved for 7 days in R2; purged after operational synthesis.        │
└──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                               │ (Post-call synthesis by System 1 JEV in 110ms)
                               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 2. MANAGEMENT THREAD (Tier 1 Team Room / Task Workstation — Authoritative)   │
│    • Emits structured Call Receipt Card:                                    │
│      ├── Caller: "Director of Rotterdam Port Authority"                     │
│      ├── Commitments: "Agreed to waive demurrage on Container MSKU-9081"     │
│      ├── Audio Footnote: [▶ 0:42 "We will drop the pier gate fees"]         │
│      └── Generated Task: TASK-108 ("File Port Waiver Form")                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 2. Edge Slot-Hold Mutex (Double-Booking Prevention)

During high-volume voice call spikes (e.g. 50 callers trying to book appointments or reserve cargo slots at the same time), naive database updates result in race conditions.

AVOS enforces a **15-Second Edge Slot-Hold Mutex** inside `ConcurrencyDO`:

```typescript
// packages/voice/src/calendar_mutex.ts
export class CalendarBookingEngine {
  async holdBookingSlot(
    concurrencyDO: ConcurrencyDO,
    slotTimestamp: number,
    callerPhone: string
  ): Promise<{ success: boolean; holdToken?: string }> {
    
    // Acquire optimistic slot lease across global Anycast nodes
    const acquired = await concurrencyDO.claimLease(
      `calendar_slot_${slotTimestamp}`,
      callerPhone,
      "HUMAN"
    );

    if (!acquired) {
      return { success: false }; // Slot currently held by another caller
    }

    const holdToken = crypto.randomUUID();
    return { success: true, holdToken };
  }
}
```

The voice agent says: *"I have reserved that 3:00 PM slot for you while we confirm your details."* The slot is locked globally for 15 seconds. If the call drops, the lease expires and the slot is automatically released back to the pool with zero orphaned records.

# Subsystem 7: The Inbound Webhook Ingress, Queue Buffer & Failure Defense Map

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE 13-POINT AUTOMATION FAILURE DEFENSE SUITE                             │

│ • Ingress Router: `workers/router/src/webhooks.ts` running at Anycast Edge                             │
│ • Buffer & Decoupling: Cloudflare Queues (Guaranteed FIFO by Group ID) + R2 Replay Lake               │
│ • Invariant Guarantee: Zero dropped webhooks under 10,000 req/sec bursts; mathematical replayability.  │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

```
                                  INBOUND WEBHOOK INGRESS PIPELINE
                                                 │
                                                 ▼
                         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                         │ ANYCAST EDGE INGRESS WORKER                  │

                         │ • Execution time: <4ms                       │

                         ▼ (Signature Invalid)                         ▼ (Signature Verified)
          ┌─────────────────────────────┐               ┌─────────────────────────────┐
          │ DROP REQUEST                │               │ EDGE TOMBSTONE FILTER (KV)  │
          │ Returns 401 Unauthorized    │               │ Detects duplicate events    │
          └─────────────────────────────┘               └──────────────┬──────────────┘

                                                        ▼ (Duplicate)                 ▼ (Unique Event)
                                         ┌─────────────────────────────┐┌─────────────────────────────┐

                                                                        └──────────────┬──────────────┘

                                                                                       ▼

                                                                        │ Sequentially dispatches to  │
```

### The 13-Point Failure Defense Suite

```
┌────┬─────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ #  │ Defense Mechanism           │ Concrete Systems Implementation                                     │
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1  │ **Edge HMAC Validation**    │ Validates Meta/Stripe signatures in <4ms; invalid packets dropped.  │
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 2  │ **KV Tombstone Idempotency**│ Event IDs stored in Cloudflare KV (24hr TTL); duplicate drops (204).│
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 3  │ **R2 Replay Lake**          │ Raw webhook payloads written to R2 before parsing for debugging.    │
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 4  │ **Queue FIFO Grouping**     │ Grouped by `tenant_id:phone_number`; ensures strictly ordered state.│
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 5  │ **12ms ACK Fast-Path**      │ Responds `200 OK` to webhook emitters instantly; avoids provider 504│
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 6  │ **Leaky-Bucket Rate Pacer** │ Regulates outbound calls to 3rd-party APIs; prevents 429 bans.     │
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 7  │ **Exponential Jitter Backoff│ Retries failed executions at $2^n + \text{rand}(0, 1000)\,\text{ms}$.│
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 8  │ **Dead-Letter Queue (DLQ)** │ After 5 failed attempts, messages route to DLQ; alerts administrators│
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 9  │ **AST Schema Validation**   │ Incoming JSON verified against Zod schemas before hitting workers.  │
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 10 │ **Circuit Breaker Sentinel**│ If partner API fails $>50\%$ over 1 min, trips open for 60s.       │
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 11 │ **Payload Truncation Fence**│ Payloads $>5\text{MB}$ offload to R2; pointers passed to queues.    │
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 12 │ **Mobile Emergency Kill**   │ Space owner can text `STOP` on WhatsApp to freeze worker loops.     │
├────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 13 │ **Time-Travel Debug Replay**│ Developers can replay any historical webhook payload in Foundry sandbox.│
└────┴─────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 1. The LoRA Distillation Pipeline

How an enterprise studio distills custom domain expertise into a fast, private adapter:

1. **Synthetic Data Synthesis:** The Architect Agent reads the firm’s historical Git Artifacts and closed deals, compiling 500 high-signal instruction-response pairs.
2. **Cloudflare Workers AI Training Run:** The JSONL dataset is processed on Workers AI infrastructure, generating a compact **LoRA Adapter Checkpoint (<50MB)**.
3. **Dynamic Adapter Ingestion:** The trained LoRA weights are stored in the space's private Cloudflare R2 bucket.
4. **Zero-Cold-Start Serving:** When queries are dispatched to Cloudflare AI Gateway, Naelao injects the tenant adapter on the fly:
   ```typescript
   const response = await env.AI.run("@cf/meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct", {
     prompt: userPrompt,
     lora: `r2://space-${spaceId}-models/adapters/v2_lora.safetensors`
   });
   ```

---

### 2. The Air-Gapped Space Compliance Lock

For defense institutions, Swiss banks, or high-security healthcare clinics, the tenant can activate the **Air-Gapped Compliance Lock**:

```json
// tenant_policy.json
{
  "compliance_regime": {
    "execution_mode": "LOCAL_ONLY",
    "cloud_ai_fallback_allowed": false,
    "allowed_tunnel_ids": ["onprem_rig_zurich_01"]
  }
}
```

When `execution_mode: "LOCAL_ONLY"` is set:
* The `NaelaoAgentDO` **unmounts all external cloud AI providers**.
* The HTTP network client throws an exception if any outbound request targets external endpoints (`api.anthropic.com`, `api.openai.com`).
* All prompts, tools, and token operations route through `LocalTunnelDO` to the company's local GPU server running vLLM or Ollama. 
* Confidential documents remain on the local hardware perimeter, providing compliance with air-gapped data mandates.

### 1. Enforcing Sovereign Data Boundaries in Code

When a new tenant space is created (e.g., for a German law firm or an Austrian hospital), the edge provisioning router binds the Durable Object namespace to its designated geographic jurisdiction:

```typescript
// packages/router/src/tenant_provisioner.ts
export async function provisionTenantSpace(
  env: Env,
  tenantId: string,
  region: "eu" | "us" | "fedramp"
): Promise<string> {
  
  let doNamespace: DurableObjectNamespace;

  if (region === "eu") {
    // Hard-pin DO memory and SQLite execution strictly to EU data centers
    doNamespace = env.SPACE_DO_JURISDICTION_EU;
  } else {
    doNamespace = env.SPACE_DO_GLOBAL;
  }

  // Derive stable actor ID within jurisdiction
  const id = doNamespace.idFromName(tenantId);
  const stub = doNamespace.get(id, {
    // Cloudflare DLS explicit location constraint
    locationHint: region === "eu" ? "weur" : "wnam"
  });

  await stub.initializeTenant({
    tenantId,
    jurisdiction: region,
    storageBucket: region === "eu" ? "naelao-artifacts-eu-weur" : "naelao-artifacts-global"
  });

  return id.toString();
}
```

### 2. The Cross-Border Data Boundary Guarantee

| Dimension | Standard SaaS Platforms | Naelao Sovereign Architecture |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **TLS Edge Termination** | Terminated at arbitrary edge node; data traversed across open web. | **Regional Services:** TLS terminated exclusively inside jurisdictional borders. |
| **Durable Object Compute** | Workerd isolates spun up dynamically wherever cheapest. | **Jurisdictional DO:** Code executes *only* in certified data centers within the specified region. |
| **Relational Storage (SQLite)**| Replicated to multi-region global storage clusters. | **EU-Pinned SQLite:** Database files never cross international borders; fully compliant with GDPR Chapter V. |
| **Binary Artifacts & Git** | Centralized US-East S3/R2 storage buckets. | **Region-Specific R2:** Storage physically locked to designated geographies (`weur`, `eeur`, `apac`). |
| **Audit Log Trail** | Plaintext logs exported to centralized third-party platforms. | **Merkle-Chained Receipts:** Hash-chained audit logs stored locally within the tenant's private SQLite DB. |

### Full End-to-End System Integration

```
========================================================================================================

========================================================================================================

2.  Planner-Worker Swarms        ──► Progressive Skill Loading & Speculative Races (SR-EEO)
3.  Cloudflare Artifacts         ──► Zig-WASM Git + ArtifactFS FUSE + R2 Pointer-LFS
4.  4-Tier Thread Hierarchy      ──► Team Room, Task Workstation, Consult Lanes, Scratchpads
5.  Stateful Micro-Apps (Gadgets)──► Dynamic Workers + Scoped Facets + Native MCP v2
6.  Zero-Leak Vault              ──► Outbound Workers TLS Injection + WriteGuard AST Guard
7.  Voice & Telephony            ──► Cloudflare Calls SFU + Dual-Thread Voice Desks
8.  Inbound Webhooks & Ingress   ──► 13-Point Failure Defense Suite + FIFO Queue Buffers
```

The Naelao platform unites edge execution, verifiable Git artifacts, multi-tenant security, and an FDE-driven vertical marketplace into a **unified, production-grade operating system**.


When an architecture looks complete on paper across 12 subsystem maps, the real danger is **the "Operational Blind Spots"**—the systems engineering gaps, edge runtime realities, and edge-case failure modes that only reveal themselves when actual users, messy networks, and autonomous code collide.

Here is the unsparing, high-density analysis of **what is still missing in Naelao / AVOS**, broken down into the 8 architectural and operational pillars that must be engineered before this platform is truly production-ready.

```

├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. **The Physical Saga Rollback**    │ Agent can `git revert` code, but cannot un-send emails,         │

├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

│    (ABAC Prompt Isolation)           │ "What is Alice's salary?" and the RAG engine leaks it.          │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 7. **Offline-First Local Sync**      │ Truckers, doctors, and lawyers lose internet. The app freezes   │
│    (CRDT Edge-to-Device Sync)        │ because state only exists on Cloudflare Anycast servers.        │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 8. **Institutional Meta-Learning**   │ When an agent fails and a human fixes it, that lesson is lost.  │
│    (The Self-Distillation Loop)      │ The agent repeats the identical mistake the next week.          │
```
