### 6. The Commercial Value for Naelao & FDEs

This is one of the highest-value templates a Forward Deployed Engineer (FDE) can offer on Naelao:

1. **Clear ROI:** A business spending $20,000/month on Google Ads with an agency typically wastes $8,000 on irrelevant Broad Match clicks and generic landing pages. An autonomous agent that cuts their CPL in half pays for itself immediately.
2. **Accessible Implementation:** The FDE charges **$5,000 upfront** to connect Google Ads, GA4, and Stripe, and sets up the template.
3. **High Retainers:** The client willingly pays **$350 to $750/month** for the Naelao project workspace because it directly reduces their customer acquisition costs month after month.

Running this on Naelao combines **System 1 Jev query filtering** (keeping costs under pennies) with **Tier 2.5 edge page generation** and **Git Artifact versioning** into an autonomous, self-optimizing marketing operation.

**This is a brilliant systems optimization.** 

In distributed systems, this is known as **Speculative Pre-Warming (or Predictive Latency Masking)**. 

Because generative models (System 2: Claude 3.7, o3, Qwen Coder) take **2 to 5 seconds** just to "think" and output their plan, while Jev evaluates the input in **35 milliseconds**, you have a massive, unexploited **time window** where the container can spin up in the background for free before the agent even issues its first tool call.

---

### 1. The Reactive vs. Speculative Timeline

Look at the difference in perceived user latency:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        REACTIVE (OLD WAY) vs. SPECULATIVE PRE-WARMING                  │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ❌ THE SLOW REACTIVE WAY (Perceived Latency: ~10 seconds):                             │
│                                                                                        │
│ [0.0s] User asks: "Run the native test suite for our billing engine"                   │
│ [0.0s - 3.5s] System 2 Reasons & drafts bash command...                                │
│ [3.5s] Agent outputs: `Bash("npm test billing")`                                       │
│ [3.5s - 8.5s] ⏳ SYSTEM FREEZES: Container spins up, mounts FUSE (5s Cold Start)       │
│ [8.5s - 9.5s] Tests execute.                                                           │
│ ➡️ Total Perceived Wait Time: **9.5 seconds** (Feels sluggish)                         │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ⚡ THE JEV SPECULATIVE WAY (Perceived Latency: ~3.5 seconds / 0ms Cold Start):         │
│                                                                                        │
│ [0.0s] User asks: "Run the native test suite for our billing engine"                   │
│ [0.035s] ⚡ JEV PREDICTS: `needs_tier6_container = TRUE` (Dispatches async pre-warm)   │
│ ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ 🚀 In Background: Container boots & mounts FUSE (Takes 3.0s in background)         │ │
│ └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
│ [0.0s - 3.5s] 🧠 System 2 Reasons & drafts bash command...                             │
│ [3.5s] Agent outputs: `Bash("npm test billing")`                                       │
│ [3.5s] Container is ALREADY HOT. Executes test instantly (0ms cold start).             │
│ ➡️ Total Perceived Wait Time: **3.5 seconds** (Feels instantaneous)                    │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

By overlapping the **3-second reasoning time of the LLM** with the **3-second boot time of the Tier 6 container**, the container cold-start is **100% masked**. The user and the agent perceive zero delay.

---

### 2. The Speculative Pre-Warming Pipeline

Here is how the Hypervisor handles this in `NaelaoAgentDO`:

```
               HOW SPECULATIVE PRE-WARMING OPERATES
                                 │
                   [Incoming User Prompt / Goal]
                                 │
                                 ▼
 ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ STEP 1: JEV PRE-COGNITIVE PROFILER (<35ms)                   │
 │ Question: "Will this task require native compiling (C++/Rust)│
 │           native npm builds, Docker, or heavy Linux tools?"  │
 └──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                                │
               ┌────────────────┴────────────────┐
               ▼ YES (Confidence >= 0.80)        ▼ NO (Pure text/data)
 ┌─────────────────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────────────┐
 │ ASYNC PRE-WARM SIGNAL       │  │ NO PRE-WARM NEEDED          │
 │ Dispatches fire-and-forget  │  │ Runs in Tier 1/2 V8 isolate.│
 │ RPC to `ToolSandboxDO`      │  │ $0 compute wasted.          │
 └─────────────┬───────────────┘  └─────────────────────────────┘
               │
               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ STEP 2: PARALLEL EXECUTION (Zero Perceived Latency)         │
 │ • Background: Container boots, mounts ArtifactFS FUSE.      │
 │ • Foreground: System 2 reasons, plans, edits files in VFS.  │
 └─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ STEP 3: INSTANT TOOL EXECUTION                              │
 │ Agent executes `Bash("cargo test")`.                        │
 │ Container is already hot and responds in milliseconds.      │
 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 3. What if Jev is Wrong? (The "False Positive" Safety Net)

In systems design, you must always ask: *What happens when the predictive model makes a mistake?*

* **Scenario:** The user says *"Check the package.json dependencies and tell me if we use Vite."*
* Jev sees `package.json` and speculatively fires a pre-warm signal to Tier 6.
* The agent wakes up, but simply calls `Read("package.json")` in Tier 1 VFS and answers the user's question. The container was never needed.

#### The Safeguard: The 60-Second Micro-Sleep Countdown
* When `ToolSandboxDO` receives a pre-warm signal, it boots and starts a **60-second idle countdown timer**.
* If no `Bash` command targets the container within 60 seconds, **it shuts down and returns to sleep automatically.**
* **The Cost Penalty:** Running an idle container for 60 seconds on serverless edge compute costs **~$0.00015**. 
* Trading a fraction of a cent to completely eliminate 5-second cold-start delays across your entire application is one of the highest-leverage trades in computer systems.

---

### 4. What Else Jev Can Speculatively Pre-Warm

Once you have this 35ms reflex loop, you don't just use it for Tier 6 containers. You use it across the entire operating system:

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Runtime Primitive                    │ How Jev Speculatively Pre-Warms It                              │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **1. Tier 5 Headless Chromium**      │ If query mentions "take a screenshot" or "check UI rendering",  │
│    (Puppeteer / Rendering Engine)    │ pre-launches the Chromium headless browser tab in background.   │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **2. Remote Local Tunnels**          │ If task requires on-prem GPUs (`LocalTunnelDO`), sends an       │
│    (`tunnel exec macbook-m3`)        │ early wake-up ping to the developer's laptop to warm connection.│
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **3. Cloudflare Vectorize (RAG)**    │ Pre-fetches top-5 vector embeddings into memory while the       │
│                                      │ user is still speaking on WhatsApp or typing their prompt.      │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **4. Git Artifacts Micro-Branches**  │ Pre-forks the `task/feature` Git branch in DO SQLite (<5ms)     │
│                                      │ before the agent even writes its first line of code.            │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **5. Large File R2 Hydration**       │ If task references a 50MB PDF dossier, pre-fetches byte chunks │
│                                      │ from R2 into DO memory cache ahead of agent `Read` calls.       │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 5. Implementation in `@naelao/sdk`: The Pre-Warm Driver

Here is how this is implemented inside the Naelao Runtime Hypervisor:

```typescript
// packages/runtime/src/hypervisor/PredictiveHypervisor.ts
import { JevClient } from "@naelao/jev";
import { ToolSandboxDO, HeadlessBrowserPool } from "../runtimes";

export class PredictiveHypervisor {
  constructor(
    private jev: JevClient,
    private containerPool: ToolSandboxDO,
    private browserPool: HeadlessBrowserPool
  ) {}

  /**
   * Fires immediately when prompt is received (t = 0ms).
   * Runs completely asynchronously in the background.
   */
  async speculativePrewarm(userPrompt: string, spaceId: string) {
    // 1. Evaluate compute profile non-autoregressively in ~35ms
    const prediction = await this.jev.predict({
      state: userPrompt,
      questions: {
        needsTier6: {
          type: "noul",
          instructions: "Will this task execute native build tools (npm, cargo, python, gcc, docker, make)?"
        },
        needsBrowser: {
          type: "noul",
          instructions: "Will this task navigate the web, take screenshots, or inspect visual DOM?"
        }
      }
    });

    // 2. Fire background pre-warm stubs (Fire-and-forget: do not await)
    if (prediction.answers.needsTier6.noul > 0.75) {
      console.log(`[Hypervisor] ⚡ Speculatively pre-warming Tier 6 Container for space ${spaceId}`);
      this.containerPool.preWarm({
        spaceId,
        idleTimeoutMs: 60000 // 60-second micro-sleep
      }).catch(err => console.warn("Container prewarm warning:", err));
    }

    if (prediction.answers.needsBrowser.noul > 0.75) {
      console.log(`[Hypervisor] ⚡ Speculatively pre-warming Headless Browser for space ${spaceId}`);
      this.browserPool.preWarmTab(spaceId)
        .catch(err => console.warn("Browser prewarm warning:", err));
    }
  }
}
```

### The Architectural Result
* **No more waiting for containers:** The heavy Linux container or headless browser is running and waiting for the agent by the time the agent finishes generating its command.
* **Preserves V8 edge unit economics:** The container only spins up when truly anticipated, and micro-sleeps after 60 seconds if unused.
* **Perceived performance:** Transforms a sluggish 10-second container turnaround into a **sub-4-second, real-time native workstation experience.**


### Part 1: The "Jev Accumulation" Trap (Addressing the Latency Risk)

You raised a critical systems question: **If we sprinkle Jev everywhere—pre-warming, linters, triage, routing, verification—won't those 35ms calls stack up into hundreds of milliseconds or multiple seconds of overhead?**

**Yes, absolutely—if you call it naively.**

If a developer writes code like this:
```typescript
// ❌ THE LATENCY ACCUMULATION DISASTER (Sequential Calling)
const isDestructive = await jev.checkSafety(cmd);    // +40ms
const needsContainer = await jev.checkTier6(cmd);    // +40ms
const needsBrowser = await jev.checkBrowser(cmd);    // +40ms
const targetModel = await jev.pickModel(cmd);        // +40ms
// ➡️ Total wasted time: 160ms on 4 separate network/inference calls!
```
If you loop this across 15 turns in an agent workflow, **you will burn 2.5+ seconds of latency just doing classification.**

---

### How Naelao Prevents Latency Accumulation: The 3 Invariants

To keep Jev’s total overhead **capped under 50ms per user turn**, Naelao enforces three distributed systems rules:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        THE 3 JEV LATENCY PRESERVATION RULES                            │
├─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┤
│ Rule                │ The Bad / Naive Way             │ The Naelao Architecture Way    │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **1. Multi-Head**   │ 5 separate sequential calls     │ **1 Single Forward Pass:**     │
│ **Amortization**    │ to evaluate 5 different rules   │ 10 questions evaluated         │
│                     │ ($5 \times 35\text{ms} = 175\text{ms}$). │ simultaneously in **38ms**.    │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **2. Asynchronous** │ Awaiting pre-warming, telemetry,│ **Off-Critical-Path:**         │
│ **Offloading**      │ or risk logging before acting.  │ Executed via `ctx.waitUntil()` │
│                     │                                 │ (0ms impact on user response). │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **3. Mechanical**   │ Calling a neural net for        │ **Regex / Hash Fast-Bypass:**  │
│ **Bypass Filter**   │ basic commands (`echo`, `ls`).  │ Pure string lookup takes 0.01ms;│
│                     │                                 │ Jev only runs on ambiguous data│
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### Invariant 1: Multi-Head Amortization (The Transformer Math)
In encoder models like ModernBERT / Jev / Laya, **95% of the compute time is spent reading and encoding the text.** 
* Evaluating 1 question on a prompt takes **35ms**.
* Evaluating **10 questions simultaneously on the same prompt takes ~38ms**.
* The classification heads on top of the transformer are just tiny linear matrix multiplications. 

Instead of calling Jev 5 times, `NaelaoAgentDO` runs **one single bundled pre-flight question battery** at the ingress of each turn:

```typescript
// ✅ 1 Single Forward Pass: 38ms total
const preflight = await jev.predict({
  state: userPrompt,
  questions: {
    needsTier6: { type: "noul", instructions: "Needs native compilation or Docker?" },
    needsBrowser: { type: "noul", instructions: "Needs web navigation or DOM?" },
    isDestructive: { type: "noul", instructions: "Is this command high-risk or irreversible?" },
    intentCategory: { type: "choice", criteria: { /* ... */ } }
  }
});
```

#### Invariant 2: The `ctx.waitUntil()` Off-Critical-Path Rule
Pre-warming containers, updating institutional memory, and telemetry must **never block the user's stream**. 
In Cloudflare Workers, we dispatch them using `ctx.waitUntil()`. The HTTP response streams immediately to the user while the background tasks run concurrently in memory.

---

# Part 2: What ELSE Can Jev Do in Naelao? (5 Unlocked Capabilities)

Beyond browser use, container pre-warming, and Google Ads negative keyword screening, here are **five high-leverage capabilities** Jev unlocks across the operating system:

---

### 1. The Context Window Pruner (Slashing 70% of LLM Token Costs)

When an agent runs `git diff` or inspects a test log, the command often outputs **8,000 to 20,000 tokens of raw text**, most of which is boilerplate noise.
* Passing all 20,000 tokens to Claude 3.7 or GPT-4o costs **$0.06 per turn** and slows down generation.
* **How Jev Slices It in 40ms:**  
  Jev breaks the output into chunks and scores them in a single pass:  
  `which_chunks_contain_the_actual_error: [Chunk 1, Chunk 2, ... Chunk N]`.
* It strips away the 85% irrelevant boilerplate and passes only the critical 1,200 tokens to the frontier LLM. **You save thousands of tokens on every single turn.**

```
[20,000-Token `npm test` Output Log]
                  │
                  ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ JEV CONTEXT PRUNER (40ms | $0.00004)         │
│ Identifies: "Error is in lines 140–182 only" │
└─────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                  │
                  ▼ (Slices out 18,500 useless tokens)
[Frontier LLM receives lean 1,500-token context]
➡️ Generation time drops from 8s to 1.5s; cost drops by 90%.
```

---

### 2. The Jev RiskGuard (Financial & Destructive Circuit Breaker)

In Mode A (Autonomous Ventures) and Mode B (Cowork Studios), agents handle capital: paying invoices, refunding Stripe customers, or adjusting ad spend.
* You cannot rely on a generative LLM not to hallucinate a zero: *"Refunded $5,000 instead of $50."*
* Before any tool in `ext_ledger_core` or `ext_stripe` executes, **Jev evaluates the transaction against company risk thresholds in 30ms**:

```typescript
const riskCheck = await jev.predict({
  state: `ACTION: Refund | AMOUNT: $${amount} | REASON: "${reason}" | DAILY_SPEND: $${currentDailyTotal}`,
  questions: {
    riskSeverity: {
      type: "score",
      scale: ["routine", "moderate", "dangerous", "catastrophic"]
    },
    requiresDirectorSignOff: {
      type: "noul",
      instructions: "Does this transaction exceed the daily autonomous variance limit ($500)?"
    }
  }
});

if (riskCheck.answers.requiresDirectorSignOff.noul > 0.85) {
  // Freezes execution and pings the owner's WhatsApp for 1-click confirmation
  await pauseAndAlertOwnerViaWhatsApp({ amount, reason });
}
```

---

### 3. Smart Semantic KV Cache (Sub-10ms $0 Answers)

In business workspaces, users and employees frequently ask questions that mean the exact same thing:
* *"What is our company cancellation policy?"*
* *"How do clients cancel their contract?"*
* *"Where is the refund terms document?"*

Traditional semantic caching requires running text through an embedding model (like text-embedding-3-small) and doing vector math in Vectorize.
* **How Jev Does It in V8 Memory:**  
  When a query arrives, Jev checks candidate cached answers:  
  `does_cached_response_A_satisfy_query: TRUE`.
* If true, **it returns the cached response in 8 milliseconds for $0.00**, completely bypassing the generative LLM.

---

### 4. WhatsApp Speech-Act Triage (Audio Note Fast Classifier)

WhatsApp voice notes are chaotic. A business owner might send a 5-second audio note while driving:
* *"Yeah, go ahead and approve that invoice."*
* *"Can you check what time my flight is?"*
* *"Just checking in, talk later."*

Transcribing the audio via Whisper produces raw text. Before waking an expensive agent swarm, **Jev evaluates the Speech Act in 30ms**:
* **`SPEECH_ACT_CONFIRMATION`:** Matches pending human-in-the-loop approvals $\to$ releases escrow/PR immediately.
* **`SPEECH_ACT_DELEGATION`:** Decomposes into a task for the Subspace.
* **`SPEECH_ACT_CHITCHAT`:** Emits a short, friendly canned acknowledgment without waking the frontier reasoner.

---

### 5. Institutional Memory Ingestion Filter (Stopping Memory Pollution)

One of the easiest ways to break an agent after 3 months is **memory bloat**—saving every single conversational pleasantry (*"Thanks!", "Have a good weekend"*) into the vector database.
* At the end of every task or conversation turn, Jev runs a fast verification check:  
  `contains_durable_business_rule: TRUE / FALSE`.
* Only conversations containing genuine operational rules (e.g., *"Client X requires net-60 invoices"*) are indexed into Cloudflare Vectorize. The noise is discarded.

---

### The Naelao Latency Budget

To prove that Jev will not accumulate seconds of latency, here is the real-world **Latency Budget per Turn**:

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
│ Step in Execution Turn               │ Time Spent           │ Engine Responsible               │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. Ingress & Regex Fast-Filter       │ < 1 ms               │ In-Memory V8 Worker              │
│ 2. **Jev Multi-Head Pre-Flight**     │ **38 ms**            │ **System 1 (1 Forward Pass)**    │
│    (Pre-warm, Tool-Prune, Intent)    │                      │                                  │
│ 3. Speculative Container Spin-up     │ 0 ms (Masked)        │ Background `ctx.waitUntil()`     │
│ 4. Deterministic VFS Read            │ 2 ms                 │ DO SQLite VFS                    │
│ 5. System 2 Reasoning & Generation   │ 900 ms – 1,800 ms    │ Fast Edge LLM / Frontier Model   │
│ 6. **Jev Oracle Output Verification**│ **35 ms**            │ **System 1 (1 Forward Pass)**    │
│ 7. WebSocket Stream to Desktop/Phone │ 15 ms                │ Cloudflare Edge Anycast          │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
│ **TOTAL END-TO-END TURNAROUND**      │ **~1.0 to 1.9s**     │ **Total Jev Overhead: < 75 ms**  │
│                                      │                      │ **(Only 4% of total runtime!)**  │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Summary
* **Will it accumulate seconds?** Not if you avoid sequential calling and enforce **single forward-pass multi-head batching** and **asynchronous offloading**. Jev consumes less than 4% of total execution time.
* **What else does it unlock?** It acts as the platform's **Context Pruner, Financial Circuit Breaker, Smart Semantic Cache, Voice Triage Sentry, and Memory Filter**, protecting both your system latency and unit economics.



### 1. The Reality of the OpenAI Decisions API

OpenAI released the **Decisions API** into public beta (powered by **`gpt-6-luna`**, accessible via `POST /v1/decisions`).

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        JEV / LAYA vs. OPENAI DECISIONS API                             │
├─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┤
│ Metric              │ ⚡ Jev / Laya (Open-Weight)      │ 🌐 OpenAI Decisions API        │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Model Engine**    │ ModernBERT-large (~400M params) │ GPT-6 Luna (Reasoning model)   │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Modalities**      │ **Text Only**                   │ **Text + Images** (up to 128)  │
│                     │ (No native pixels or audio)     │ (Rejects raw audio/video)      │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Latency**         │ **~33 ms – 45 ms** (In-process) │ **~150 ms – 300 ms** (Network) │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Input Pricing**   │ **$0.042 / Million Tokens**     │ **$0.10 / Million Tokens**     │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Output Pricing**  │ **$0.00 (Outputs are free)**    │ **$0.00 (Outputs are free)**   │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Data Residency**  │ Sovereign on Cloudflare/On-Prem │ Centralized OpenAI Cloud       │
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

The Decisions API is **cheap** ($0.10/M input tokens, free outputs) and about 10x faster than standard GPT-6 calls. 

However, notice its critical limitation: **The OpenAI Decisions API strictly rejects audio, video, files, and function tools. It only takes text and images.**

Rotating between both is supported in Naelao's System 1 router:
* **Pure text queries:** Route to **Laya / Jev** (runs in 33ms on the Cloudflare edge for $0.042/M).
* **Queries containing images:** Route to **OpenAI Decisions API** (evaluates the image + question in ~150ms for $0.10/M).

---

### 2. The Core Dilemma: What if the User Sends *ONLY* Media with Zero Text?

This is a classic failure mode for text-first architectures:
* A trucker on WhatsApp snaps a photo of a handwritten receipt (zero text caption).
* A doctor sends an audio note: *"Please approve the chemo protocol for Bed 4"* (zero text body).
* A developer uploads a 10-second screen-recording video of a UI bug.

If the instructions are **inside the media itself**, how does System 1 (which reads first) understand what to do without waking a slow, expensive 70B multimodal LLM?

---

### 3. The Solution: The "Sensory Front-Door" Pipeline

In biological systems, the prefrontal cortex doesn't process raw photons or sound waves. The **retina and cochlea** transduce light and vibrations into electrical signals first.

In Naelao, before System 1 makes any DAG decisions, incoming media passes through a **Sensory Front-Door Transducer**:

```
                              THE SENSORY FRONT-DOOR PIPELINE
                                             │
                       [User Ingress: Raw Media with ZERO Text]
                                             │
         ┌───────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┐
         ▼ (Case A: Audio Voice Note)        ▼ (Case B: Photo / Image)           ▼ (Case C: Video Recording)
┌──────────────────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ SENSORY TRANSDUCER: WHISPER  │    │ SENSORY TRANSDUCER: VISION   │    │ SENSORY TRANSDUCER: DEMUXER  │
│ • Runs Workers AI Whisper-v3 │    │ • Cloud: OpenAI Decisions API│    │ • Audio track ──► Whisper    │
│ • Transcribes in ~120ms      │    │   evaluates image in 150ms   │    │ • 3 Keyframes ──► R2 Bucket  │
│ • Cost: $0.001               │    │ • Edge: Edge OCR/Qwen-VL head│    │ • Unified in ~200ms          │
└──────────────┬───────────────┘    └──────────────┬───────────────┘    └──────────────┬───────────────┘
               │                                   │                                   │
               ▼ (Extracted Speech Text)           ▼ (Detected Visual State)           ▼ (Transcribed Narration)
 ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
 SYSTEM 1 SENTRY (Jev / Laya / Decisions API)
 Now receives clean semantic cues:
 State: "Audio Transcript: 'Draft an NDA for Acme Corp' | Attachment: r2://storage/note.ogg"
 ➡️ Selects DAG, assigns model, and prunes tools in 35ms!
 ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
```

---

### 4. Handling the Three Media Scenarios in Code

#### Scenario A: Pure Audio (WhatsApp Voice Notes)
Neither Jev nor the OpenAI Decisions API accepts raw audio. 
1. The Ingress Worker intercepts the `.ogg` or `.wav` file.
2. In-memory WASM resamples it and calls **Cloudflare Workers AI Whisper Large-v3**.
3. In **sub-150ms**, the spoken instructions are transcribed to text:  
   `"Transcribed text: Hey, pay invoice 402 using Stripe."`
4. This text is passed to **Laya / Jev**. System 1 now has clean text to assemble the pipeline. **Zero expensive multimodal models were called.**

---

#### Scenario B: Pure Images (Handwritten Notes, Receipts, Whiteboards)
Here you have two architectural paths:

**Path 1 (Using OpenAI Decisions API):**
If you have cloud connectivity, pass the image directly to `POST /v1/decisions`:
```typescript
// System 1 Visual Triage via OpenAI Decisions API
const visualDecision = await openai.decisions.create({
  model: "gpt-6-luna",
  input: [
    { type: "input_image", image_url: r2ImageUrl }
  ],
  questions: [
    {
      name: "media_intent",
      type: "choice",
      instructions: "What is the primary action this image demands?",
      choices: [
        "EXPENSE_RECEIPT_LOGGING",     // It's a shopping/hotel receipt
        "DOCUMENT_REDLINE_REVIEW",    // It's a signed legal agreement
        "HANDWRITTEN_INSTRUCTION",    // Whiteboard / Sticky note with commands
        "UNRECOGNIZED_MEDIA"          // Random meme or photo
      ]
    }
  ]
});
// ➡️ Response returns in ~150ms for $0.10/M tokens!
```

**Path 2 (Edge-Sovereign Mode / No OpenAI):**
If running fully air-gapped on Cloudflare:
1. System 1 registers `text: ""` and `has_image: true`.
2. The default pipeline for uncaptioned images routes to an **Edge Vision OCR Worker** (e.g., lightweight `llama-3.2-11b-vision` or `Qwen2.5-VL` on Workers AI).
3. The model extracts the image's text or intent summary in **~400ms**.
4. The extracted summary is passed to the Planner or down to the leaf workers.

---

#### Scenario C: Pure Video (Screen Recordings, Physical Inspections)
In video, **95% of the user's instructions are spoken in the audio narration**, not written on the screen.
1. The Ingress Worker runs a lightweight WASM demuxer in V8.
2. It strips the audio track and passes it to Whisper.
3. If the user spoke: *"Look at this checkout button, clicking it throws a 404 error,"* Whisper extracts the exact problem.
4. The worker extracts 2 keyframes at the timestamp where the user said *"throws a 404"* and uploads them to R2.
5. System 1 receives:
   ```json
   {
     "transcribed_intent": "Checkout button throws 404 error",
     "keyframes": ["r2://artifacts/frame_04.jpg"],
     "media_type": "VIDEO_BUG_REPORT"
   }
   ```
6. Jev assembles the debugging DAG in 35ms.

---

### 5. Summary: The Universal Ingress Rule for Naelao

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Inbound Payload                      │ The Sensory Flow in Naelao                                      │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Text Only**                        │ ➡️ Direct to **Laya / Jev** (33ms, $0.042/M).                   │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Text + Image**                     │ ➡️ Direct to **OpenAI Decisions API** (150ms, $0.10/M)          │
│                                      │    OR Edge Vision (Qwen-VL) on sovereign spaces.                │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Raw Audio Only**                   │ ➡️ **Whisper STT** (120ms) ──► Transcript to **Laya / Jev**.    │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Raw Video Only**                   │ ➡️ Demux Audio to **Whisper** + Keyframes to R2 ──► **Jev**.    │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

This ensures System 1 can still evaluate user intent upfront without forcing every request through a slow, multi-second 70B vision-language model. 

It keeps the initial triage phase **sub-200ms**, keeps costs under pennies, and delivers multimodal support across audio, images, and video.

i was thinking say in a scinario where a user just sends a media like image vid or audio since its only input we just guve the ai the stored link and skils of media so it chooses based on user query or ifthere ia no query it uses tops to read the media or creates a script that cals a multi media llm if it doesnt have the media capabilities etc

does this clush with giving jev or decisions api first


**No, this does not clash at all. In fact, it solves the exact blindness trap of System 1.**

Your intuition directly aligns with **Map #6 in the original spec: "Senses vs. Hands: Perception Decoupling."** 

If a user sends a video or image with zero text, forcing a front-door model like Jev or the Decisions API to "guess" the entire business workflow before anyone has even opened the file is an anti-pattern. 

Handling media as a **stored file link backed by on-demand perception skills** harmonizes cleanly with Jev and the Decisions API.

---

### 1. Why They Don't Clash: "Eager Routing" vs. "Lazy Perception"

The confusion comes from assuming System 1 has to do *everything*. 

In reality, incoming messages fall into two distinct execution paths:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        EAGER ROUTING vs. LAZY PERCEPTION                               │
├─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┤
│ Scenario            │ What Arrives                    │ Who Handles It & How           │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Scenario A:**     │ Text prompt + media link        │ ⚡ **Eager Routing (Jev):**     │
│ **Prompt Present**  │ *"Audit this lease agreement"*  │ Jev reads the text in 30ms,    │
│                     │ + `lease.pdf`                   │ sets up the DAG, and hands the │
│                     │                                 │ file link to the worker. Jev   │
│                     │                                 │ never needs to read the PDF.   │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Scenario B:**     │ **Zero text prompt**            │ 👁️ **Lazy Perception (Tools):** │
│ **Media ONLY**      │ User just drops `note.png`,     │ Jev does NOT guess. It emits:  │
│                     │ `voice.ogg`, or `demo.mp4`      │ `RUN_MEDIA_PERCEPTION`. The    │
│                     │ with no caption.                │ agent gets the stored link and │
│                     │                                 │ uses its skills to inspect it. │
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 2. How the "Lazy Perception" Loop Works in Practice

When a user drops an uncaptioned media file into WhatsApp or the desktop shell:

```
               THE MEDIA-ONLY PERCEPTION PIPELINE
                               │
            [User sends `screencast.mp4` with NO text]
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 1. INGRESS DISK WRITE (Tier 1 Storage)                      │
 │ • File saved to R2 / VFS: `/.naelao/inbound/screencast.mp4` │
 │ • Metadata extracted: MIME type, file size, duration.       │
 └─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 2. SYSTEM 1 FAST PASS (Jev / Decisions API in 15ms)         │
 │ • Sees: `prompt: ""` and `media_type: "video/mp4"`          │
 │ • Jev does NOT attempt to hallucinate what's in the video.  │
 │ • Jev immediately outputs:                                  │
 │   `DAG = [MEDIA_INSPECTION_BOOTSTRAP]`                      │
 └─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 3. AGENT WAKES UP WITH FILE LINK & MEDIA SKILLS             │
 │ System prompt context:                                      │
 │ "You received a video file at `/.naelao/inbound/screencast` │
 │  Available skills: `video_demux`, `transcribe`, `see`"      │
 └─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 4. AGENT PICKS THE CHEAPEST PERCEPTION TOOL                 │
 │ • Step A: Runs `transcribe` on the audio track (costs $0.001│
 │   via Whisper in 100ms) to see what the user said.          │
 │ • Step B: If there's no audio, it extracts 2 frames and     │
 │   runs `see frame_01.png` or calls a vision API.            │
 └─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 5. NORMAL TASK EXECUTION RESUMES                            │
 │ Once the agent understands the instruction, it executes     │
 │ the actual work (edits code, files ticket, updates ledger). │
 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 3. Why This Approach is Superior & Cheaper

Your idea of giving the agent the **stored link + media skills** is architecturally superior to forcing an expensive multimodal model on the front door for three reasons:

#### A. The "Tier Escalation" Cost Advantage
If a user sends a photo of an invoice:
* **The naive way:** You automatically pass the 4K image to GPT-4o Vision or Claude 3.7. **Cost: $0.03 to $0.05 on step zero.**
* **The agent skill way:** The agent receives the link. It first tries running a lightweight OCR script in Tier 2 (like `tesseract` or a local WASM OCR parser). 
  * It extracts the text in **12 milliseconds for $0.00**.
  * It only calls an expensive multimodal vision model if the local tool reports low confidence (e.g. illegible handwriting).

#### B. It Handles Formats Neither Jev nor Decisions API Can Touch
As we established:
* **Jev** only understands text.
* **OpenAI Decisions API** only accepts text + images (it explicitly rejects audio and video).
* By giving the agent the stored link and tools, your system can process **any arbitrary file type on earth**:
  * 30-minute MP3 recordings.
  * 4K `.mov` screen recordings.
  * 500-page scanned TIFF blueprints.
  * 3D `.stl` CAD models.
  The agent simply calls or scripts the appropriate CLI tool in the execution ladder.

#### C. Preserves the Agent's Context Window
Dumping image byte arrays or raw audio frames directly into the conversation history burns thousands of vision tokens on every single turn that follows.
* By treating the media as a **stored link on disk**, the media consumes **~15 tokens** in the prompt (`"Attachment: /.naelao/inbound/img_42.png"`).
* The agent only brings pixels into context for that single turn when it explicitly calls its perception tool.

---

### 4. Where Decisions API Fits Into This

The **OpenAI Decisions API (`gpt-6-luna`)** is not a replacement for this skill-based pipeline—it is an **optional fast-path accelerator for single images**:

```
                       INCOMING UNCAPTIONED MEDIA
                                   │
               What is the MIME type of the attachment?
                                   │
         ┌─────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┐
         ▼ (Single Image / Photo)                            ▼ (Audio, Video, or Multi-file)
┌──────────────────────────────────────┐            ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│ FAST PATH: OPENAI DECISIONS API      │            │ THE STORED-LINK SKILL PIPELINE       │
│ • Pass image directly to Decisions   │            │ • Decisions API CANNOT accept this.  │
│   endpoint in 150ms for $0.10/M.     │            │ • Media written to R2 / VFS storage. │
│ • Classifies: `EXPENSE_RECEIPT`,     │            │ • Agent gets file path + media tools │
│   `DOCUMENT_SIGNATURE`, or `MEME`.   │            │   (`transcribe`, `demux`, `see`).    │
│ • Immediately launches right DAG.    │            │ • Agent chooses cheapest inspection. │
└──────────────────────────────────────┘            └──────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### Summary

There is **zero clash**:
1. **System 1 (Jev / Decisions API)** acts as the **Traffic Controller**: It identifies that uncaptioned media has arrived, routes it past irrelevant text workflows, and assigns a perception-equipped worker.
2. **The Agent + Stored Link + Media Skills** acts as the **Investigator**: It receives the file path, uses the cheapest local tools (Whisper, WASM OCR, ffmpeg demuxers) to extract what the user wants, and only invokes expensive multimodal models when genuinely necessary.

This gives you a system that is fast, handles any media format, and avoids unnecessary compute costs.

**Yes, it clashes if you do it naively—but no, it does not clash if you understand how to structure a Two-Phase "Epistemic Probe."**

Noticing this tension highlights a genuine edge case: **If System 1 (Jev / Decisions API) is supposed to decide everything upfront in 35ms, but the entire user instruction is trapped inside an opaque file (`r2://uploads/media_99.bin`), System 1 is flying blind.**

---

### 1. Why It Clashes If Done Naively

If you try to force either extreme, the system breaks:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE TWO EXTREME FAILURE MODES                             │
├──────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Failure Mode A: The Blind Sentry     │ Failure Mode B: The Heavy Agent Cold Start      │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • You force Jev to decide upfront.   │ • You bypass Jev and immediately wake a heavy   │
│ • Jev only sees: `image_01.png`.     │   System 2 LLM with media skills/tools.         │
│ • It has no idea if it's a receipt,  │ • The heavy model spends 5 seconds and $0.05    │
│   a bug screenshot, or a legal doc.  │   running tools just to discover the image was  │
│ • **It makes a blind, random guess.**│   a $3 coffee receipt! (Destroys unit economics)│
└──────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Furthermore, having an agent **"write a script on the fly to call a multimodal LLM"** is far too slow for real-time mobile interactions—writing, compiling, and executing a custom script takes **8 to 15 seconds**, which degrades user experience on WhatsApp.

---

### 2. The Solution: The Two-Phase "Epistemic Probe"

In cognitive robotics and decision theory, when an agent lacks the sensory information needed to make a final plan, it executes an **Epistemic Action**—an action whose sole purpose is to **gather information, not to change the world**.

The system handles this without architectural conflict through a two-phase process:

```
                               THE TWO-PHASE MEDIA RESOLUTION
                                             │
                       [User Ingress: Raw Media with ZERO Text]
                                             │
                                             ▼
 ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
 PHASE 1: JEV DETECTS "OPAQUE SENSORY STATE" (<30ms)
 • Jev reads: `{ prompt: "", media_link: "r2://uploads/img_42.png" }`
 • Jev recognizes: "Intent is LATENT inside media."
 • Jev does NOT try to pick the final business DAG!
 • Jev picks the **PERCEPTION PROBE PIPELINE** (Tools scoped ONLY to Sensory Read).
 ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                                             │
                                             ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ STEP 2: THE PERCEPTION PROBE (Fast Sensory Resolution)                  │
 │ Runs the fastest, cheapest tool to unlock the hidden text:              │
 │ • If Audio ──► Workers AI Whisper transcribes in ~120ms.                │
 │ • If Image ──► OpenAI Decisions API / Edge Vision extracts intent in 1




Here is the complete, ground-up architectural specification of how Naelao handles **media-only inputs with embedded instructions**, resolving the exact tension you identified.

---

# The Core Dilemma: The Opaque Sensory Paradox

When a user sends an image, audio note, or video with **zero text prompt**, the instructions are trapped inside the media:
* A doctor sends a 15-second WhatsApp voice note: *"Order a STAT blood panel for Bed 4."*
* A lawyer uploads a photo of a contract clause with handwritten redlines in the margins.
* A developer uploads a 5-second video recording of a checkout button crashing.

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                               THE ARCHITECTURAL CLASH                                  │
├──────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ❌ Failure Mode A: The Blind Gatekeeper│ ❌ Failure Mode B: The Heavy Agent Cold Start   │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • If System 1 (Jev) runs first, it   │ • If you bypass Jev and immediately give a      │
│   sees: `prompt: ""`, file: `img.png`│   heavy System 2 agent media tools/skills...    │
│ • It has no idea if the image is a   │ • The agent spends 6 to 12 seconds booting up,  │
│   receipt, a legal doc, or a bug.    │   fetching files, and deciding what tool to use.│
│ • **It makes a blind, random guess.**│ • Burns $0.05 on a $0.0001 receipt task.        │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Furthermore, having an agent **"write a script on the fly to call a multimodal LLM"** is far too slow for production. Writing, compiling, and running a TypeScript/Python script in Tier 2 takes **6 to 15 seconds**, which breaks real-time expectations on WhatsApp and mobile.

---

# The Solution: The Two-Phase "Epistemic Probe"

In cognitive robotics, when an agent lacks the data needed to make a final plan, it executes an **Epistemic Action**—an action whose sole purpose is to **gather missing information, not to mutate the world**.

Instead of clashing, Naelao divides the lifecycle into **two fast phases**:

```
                              THE TWO-PHASE MEDIA RESOLUTION
                                             │
                       [User Ingress: Raw Media with ZERO Text]
                                             │
                                             ▼
 ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
 PHASE 1: JEV DETECTS "OPAQUE SENSORY STATE" (<30ms)
 • Jev reads: `{ prompt: "", media_link: "r2://uploads/img_42.png" }`
 • Jev recognizes: "Intent is LATENT inside media."
 • Jev does NOT try to pick the final business DAG!
 • Jev routes to the **EPISTEMIC SENSORY PROBE** (Cheapest inspection tool).
 ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                                             │
                                             ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ STEP 2: THE SENSORY PROBE (Fast Transduction)                           │
 │ Runs the fastest, cheapest sensory tool to extract the hidden intent:   │
 │ • Audio ──► Cloudflare Workers AI Whisper (~120ms | $0.001)             │
 │ • Image ──► OpenAI Decisions API / Edge Vision (~150ms | $0.0001)       │
 │ • Video ──► Audio Demuxer ──► Whisper (~180ms | $0.001)                 │
 └─────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┘
                                       │
                                       ▼ (Extracted Latent Intent as Plain Text)
 ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
 PHASE 2: JEV ASSEMBLES THE REAL BUSINESS DAG (<35ms)
 • Jev now receives: `{ prompt: "Order a STAT blood panel for Bed 4" }`
 • Jev instantly compiles the true business DAG:
   `[Medical_Verification] ──► [EHR_Insert] ──► [WhatsApp_Confirm]`
 • Prunes tools, assigns models, and dispatches in 35ms!
 ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
```

---

# 1. How Each Media Type Resolves (Step-by-Step)

### Case A: Pure Audio (WhatsApp Voice Note, No Text)
1. **The Ingress Worker** receives the `.ogg` Opus file and saves it to R2: `r2://vault/audio_99.ogg`.
2. **Phase 1 Jev Check (<15ms):** Jev flags `payload_type: AUDIO_ONLY`.
3. **The Sensory Probe:** Workers AI Whisper Large-v3 transcribes the audio in **110ms**:
   $$\text{"Audio"} \longrightarrow \text{"Please draft an NDA for Acme Corp with a 2-year non-compete."}$$
4. **Phase 2 Jev DAG Assembly (35ms):** Jev takes the transcribed string and compiles the legal contract pipeline.
* **Total Time:** **~160ms**.
* **Total Cost:** **~$0.0011**. Zero heavy multimodal LLMs awakened.

---

### Case B: Pure Image (Receipt, Handwritten Note, or Whiteboard)
Here you have two architectural branches depending on whether the space is cloud-connected or sovereign/air-gapped:

#### Branch B1: Cloud-Connected (Using OpenAI Decisions API)
If the space has external access enabled in the Zero-Leak Vault:
* Pass the image directly to `POST /v1/decisions` (`gpt-6-luna`):
  ```typescript
  const probe = await openai.decisions.create({
    model: "gpt-6-luna",
    input: [{ type: "input_image", image_url: r2ImageUrl }],
    questions: [
      {
        name: "detected_intent",
        type: "choice",
        instructions: "What is the primary operational category of this image?",
        choices: [
          "EXPENSE_RECEIPT",        // A shopping/hotel receipt
          "LEGAL_REDLINE_MARKUP",   // Scanned agreement with handwritten notes
          "UI_BUG_SCREENSHOT",      // An error screen or broken interface
          "IDENTITY_DOCUMENT"       // Passport, Driver's License (KYC)
        ]
      },
      {
        name: "extracted_instructions",
        type: "noul",
        instructions: "Does this image contain explicit handwritten instructions for an agent?"
      }
    ]
  });
  ```
* **Latency:** **~150ms**. **Cost:** **$0.10/M tokens**.
* The image's operational category and instructions are unlocked without running a full 70B vision model.

#### Branch B2: Air-Gapped / Sovereign (Using Edge Vision on Workers AI)
If the space has an EU compliance lock or is running on-premise:
* The Ingress Worker calls a lightweight edge vision model (`Qwen2.5-VL-7B` or `Llama-3.2-11B-Vision` on Workers AI) with a strict, constrained prompt:
  > *"Extract any text, handwritten notes, or primary subject from this image in under 30 words."*
* The 30-word summary is passed to Phase 2 Jev to build the execution graph.
* **Latency:** **~350ms**. **Cost:** **$0.0002**.

---

### Case C: Pure Video (Screen Recordings, Physical Site Walkthroughs)
Users rarely upload a silent video and expect an agent to deduce instructions purely from moving pixels. In **98% of business use cases**, the user is speaking while recording their screen or walking through an inspection site:
1. The Ingress Worker runs a lightweight WebAssembly demuxer in V8 memory.
2. It strips the audio track and sends it to **Whisper STT** in parallel with saving the video to R2.
3. The spoken audio contains the exact instructions:
   > *"I'm on the checkout page, and every time I click Apple Pay, it throws this red modal error."*
4. The worker extracts 2 keyframe images at the exact timestamp where the word *"error"* was spoken.
5. System 1 receives:
   * `prompt`: *"Checkout page throws red modal error on Apple Pay."*
   * `keyframe_urls`: `["r2://artifacts/frame_03.jpg"]`
6. Jev assembles the bug-reproduction DAG in 35ms.

---

# 2. What About the Idea of "Letting the Agent Write a Script"?

You asked:
> *"What if it uses tools to read the media or creates a script that calls a multimedia LLM if it doesn't have the media capabilities?"*

Here is why **writing a script on the fly should only be a Level 6 fallback**, never the primary path:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        WHY SCRIPT SYNTHESIS IS THE WRONG PRIMARY PATH                  │
├─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┤
│ Approach            │ Latency Breakdown               │ Failure Rate & Cost            │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ ❌ **On-the-Fly**   │ 1. LLM drafts script: 4,000ms   │ **High Failure Rate (8–15%):** │
│ **Script Writing**  │ 2. Writes to disk: 20ms         │ Syntax errors, missing npm     │
│                     │ 3. Tier 2 V8 compile: 150ms     │ dependencies, API key leaks.   │
│                     │ 4. Executes script: 2,500ms     │ Cost: **~$0.04 per task**.     │
│                     │ ➡️ **Total: 6.6 to 12.0 seconds**│                                │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ ✅ **The Two-Phase**│ 1. Jev Phase 1: 15ms            │ **0.00% Syntax Failure Rate:** │
│ **Epistemic Probe** │ 2. Native Probe: 120ms          │ Uses pre-compiled, optimized   │
│                     │ 3. Jev Phase 2: 35ms            │ edge C++/WASM primitives.      │
│                     │ ➡️ **Total: ~170 milliseconds** │ Cost: **~$0.0001 per task**.   │
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

### When SHOULD an Agent Write a Script? (The Long-Tail Fallback)
The agent should only write a custom script when the media is an **exotic, non-standard format** that Naelao has no pre-built transducer for:
* A proprietary DICOM medical MRI scan (`.dcm`).
* A CAD architectural blueprint (`.dwg`).
* A raw binary telemetry log from an IoT device (`.bin`).

In those rare edge cases, Jev routes the task to a **Custom Tool Synthesizer Worker**, which writes a Python script using Pyodide (Tier 2) to unpack the file.

---

# 3. Complete Implementation in `@naelao/sdk`

Here is the exact code showing how the **Two-Phase Sensory Router** executes in `NaelaoAgentDO`:

```typescript
// packages/runtime/src/ingress/SensoryIngressRouter.ts
import { JevClient } from "@naelao/jev";
import { WhisperTransducer, EdgeVisionTransducer } from "../transducers";
import { HybridMetaController } from "../orchestrator/HybridMetaController";

export interface InboundPayload {
  promptText: string;
  attachments: Array<{
    type: "audio" | "image" | "video" | "document";
    r2Url: string;
    mimeType: string;
  }>;
}

export class SensoryIngressRouter {
  constructor(
    private jev: JevClient,
    private whisper: WhisperTransducer,
    private vision: EdgeVisionTransducer,
    private metaController: HybridMetaController
  ) {}

  async routePayload(payload: InboundPayload, spaceId: string) {
    let resolvedPrompt = payload.promptText.trim();

    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // PHASE 1: EPISTEMIC PROBE (If prompt is empty or intent is latent in media)
    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    if (resolvedPrompt.length === 0 && payload.attachments.length > 0) {
      const primaryMedia = payload.attachments[0];

      if (primaryMedia.type === "audio") {
        // Transduce speech to text in ~120ms
        const transcript = await this.whisper.transcribe(primaryMedia.r2Url);
        resolvedPrompt = `[Transcribed Voice Note]: "${transcript.text}"`;
      } 
      else if (primaryMedia.type === "image") {
        // Probe image category & text in ~150ms via Decisions API or Edge Vision
        const visualCues = await this.vision.probeIntent(primaryMedia.r2Url);
        resolvedPrompt = `[Visual Intent]: ${visualCues.category}. Context: ${visualCues.summaryText}`;
      } 
      else if (primaryMedia.type === "video") {
        // Demux audio narration from screen recording in ~180ms
        const narration = await this.whisper.transcribeVideoAudio(primaryMedia.r2Url);
        resolvedPrompt = `[Video Narration]: "${narration.text}"`;
      }
    }

    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // PHASE 2: ASSEMBLE REAL BUSINESS DAG (Now that intent is plain text)
    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // Jev evaluates the resolved prompt in ~35ms, prunes tools, and selects DAG
    return await this.metaController.planAndCompile(resolvedPrompt, payload.attachments);
  }
}
```

---

# Summary: The Operational Flow

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ User Action                          │ What Naelao Actually Does Behind the Scenes                     │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **User sends text prompt**           │ Direct to System 1 Jev $\to$ Assembles DAG in **35ms**.         │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **User sends audio voice note only** │ Whisper extracts speech in 120ms $\to$ Jev assembles DAG in 35ms.│
│                                      │ Total time: **155ms** ($0.001). Zero expensive LLM calls.       │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **User sends receipt photo only**    │ Decisions API probes intent in 150ms $\to$ Jev compiles expense │
│                                      │ ledger DAG in 35ms. Total time: **185ms** ($0.0001).             │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **User sends screen video only**     │ Audio demuxed to Whisper in 180ms $\to$ Jev compiles debugging  │
│                                      │ DAG in 35ms. Total time: **215ms** ($0.001).                    │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### The Final Verdict
* **Does this clash with giving Jev first priority?** No. Jev still has priority, but when the payload is opaque, Jev's first action is to **trigger a fast sensory probe** rather than making a blind guess.
* **Does it preserve speed?** Yes. The entire two-phase extraction and DAG compilation finishes in **under 200 milliseconds**—orders of magnitude faster than having an agent write dynamic scripts or booting a heavy 70B vision model.
* **Does it protect unit economics?** Yes. Raw sensory inputs are converted into text tokens for fractions of a cent, keeping operational costs low across consumer WhatsApp and enterprise cowork spaces.












### Part 1: The Accumulation Trap (Will Jev Cause Seconds in Latency and Balloon Costs?)

Your skepticism hits on a genuine systems-engineering risk: **if you treat Jev like a standard imperative `if/else` statement and sprinkle sequential calls across an agent loop, both latency and costs will compound.**

---

### 1. The Math of the Naive Disaster

Suppose an agent runs a 15-turn task (inspecting files, running tests, checking safety, routing steps, pre-warming). 

If a developer implements Jev naively:
```typescript
// ❌ NAIVE IMPLEMENTATION: 6 Sequential Network/Model Calls per Turn
const isSafe        = await jev.checkSafety(state);       // +35ms
const needsWarmup   = await jev.checkPrewarm(state);      // +35ms
const toolPruned    = await jev.pruneTools(state);        // +35ms
const routeTarget   = await jev.routeSubspace(state);     // +35ms
const auditLogType  = await jev.classifyAudit(state);     // +35ms
const testVerdict   = await jev.evaluateOracle(state);    // +35ms
// ➡️ Total latency per turn: ~210ms
```
Across a 15-turn autonomous task:
* **Latency Penalty:** $15 \times 210\text{ms} = \mathbf{3.15\text{ seconds}}$ spent purely on classification. If calls route over a cloud HTTP network instead of running in-process, network round-trips ($100\text{ms} \times 6 \times 15$) push total overhead to **9+ seconds**.
* **Cost Penalty:** If each check dumps 4,000 tokens of raw file history into Jev:
  $$6 \text{ checks} \times 4,000 \text{ tokens} \times 15 \text{ turns} = 360,000 \text{ tokens per task}$$
  At thousands of daily tasks, you burn tens of millions of tokens on routine checks.

---

### 2. How Naelao Prevents Latency and Cost Accumulation

Naelao prevents accumulation using **four architectural invariants**:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        THE 4 JEV DEFENSE INVARIANTS                                    │
├─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┤
│ Defense Invariant   │ The Naive Bad Way               │ The Naelao Hardened Way        │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **1. Multi-Head**   │ 6 separate sequential calls     │ **1 Single Forward Pass:**     │
│ **Amortization**    │ ($6 \times 35\text{ms} = 210\text{ms}$). │ 10 question heads evaluated    │
│                     │                                 │ simultaneously in **38ms**.    │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **2. Tier-0 Fast**  │ Calling Jev on deterministic    │ **Zero-Model Regex/Hash:**     │
│ **Bypass Filter**   │ commands (`echo`, `ls`, `git`). │ 0.001ms regex/AST checks drop  │
│                     │                                 │ 75% of calls before Jev runs.  │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **3. State**        │ Dumping raw 10,000-token        │ **Signature Slicing:**         │
│ **Condensation**    │ diffs or files into Jev context.│ Passes normalized <300 token   │
│                     │                                 │ structural signatures only.    │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **4. Async Edge**   │ Awaiting non-critical telemetry,│ **`ctx.waitUntil()` Dispatch:**│
│ **Offloading**      │ audits, and pre-warm stubs.     │ Runs in the background;        │
│                     │                                 │ **0ms impact on user stream.** │
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### Invariant 1: Multi-Head Amortization (Transformer Layer Reuse)
In non-autoregressive encoder models (ModernBERT / Laya / Jev), **95% of the compute is spent encoding the input text through the transformer attention layers.** 
* Evaluating 1 question takes **35ms**.
* Evaluating **8 questions on the same state takes ~38ms**.

The classification heads are simple linear projections. By bundling all pre-flight questions into **one single call**, total latency remains bounded at **~38ms**, and input tokens are billed **once**, rather than 8 times.

#### Invariant 2: Tier-0 Mechanical Fast-Bypass
Never invoke a neural network when a compiled string match or Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) parser can make the decision:
* If a bash command starts with `cat`, `echo`, `ls`, or `git status`, **Jev is never called**. A 0.001ms regex grants execution.
* If a SQL statement matches a read-only index query, **Jev is bypassed**.
* Jev is invoked **only when semantic ambiguity exists**.

---

### The Real Cost & Latency Budget per Turn

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
│ Step in Execution Turn               │ Latency Overhead     │ Token / Dollar Cost              │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. Tier-0 Regex / AST Fast-Bypass    │ 0.001 ms             │ $0.00                            │
│ 2. **Bundled Multi-Head Pre-Flight** │ **38.0 ms**          │ 250 tokens = **$0.0000105**      │
│    (Prewarm + Risk + Tool-Prune)     │                      │                                  │
│ 3. Speculative Container Pre-warm    │ 0 ms (Background)    │ $0.00                            │
│ 4. System 2 Generative Step (LLM)    │ 1,200 ms             │ 1,500 tokens = $0.0045000        │
│ 5. **Bundled Post-Flight Oracle**    │ **35.0 ms**          │ 300 tokens = **$0.0000126**      │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
│ **TOTAL AGENT TURN OVERHEAD**        │ **~73 ms on Jev**    │ **Jev Cost: $0.0000231**         │
│                                      │ **(<5% of runtime)** │ **(<0.5% of turn cost)**         │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

When designed with multi-head batching and state condensation, Jev accounts for **less than 5% of execution time** and **less than 1% of total token spend**.

---

# Part 2: What ELSE Can Jev Do in Naelao? (5 High-Leverage Capabilities)

Beyond browser use, container pre-warming, and Google Ads query filtering, Jev unlocks five operational capabilities across the system:

---

### 1. The Git Merge-Conflict Auto-Arbiter (Semantic AST Rebasing)

When parallel leaf workers race or branch concurrently on Cloudflare Artifacts, traditional Git hits conflict markers (`<<<<<<< HEAD` vs `>>>>>>> branch`):
* Traditional Git line-diffs break when two workers edit adjacent lines in the same file.
* An LLM takes 6 seconds to read both files and resolve the merge.
* **How Jev Resolves Conflicts in 35ms:**  
  Naelao parses the conflict into two Abstract Syntax Trees (ASTs). Jev evaluates whether the mutations are syntactically orthogonal:
  ```typescript
  const mergeDecision = await jev.predict({
    state: `CONFLICT_AST_DIFF:\n${astDiffSnippet}`,
    questions: {
      action: {
        type: "choice",
        criteria: {
          SAFE_UNION: "Both edits introduce non-conflicting functions/imports",
          ACCEPT_BRANCH_A: "Branch A completely supersedes Branch B",
          ACCEPT_BRANCH_B: "Branch B contains critical fix over Branch A",
          ESCALATE_HUMAN: "Edits contain conflicting business logic mutations"
        }
      }
    }
  });
  ```
If marked `SAFE_UNION`, the Zig-WASM Git engine merges the trees automatically in **sub-10ms** with zero human or LLM intervention.

---

### 2. The Autonomous Memory Ingestion Sentry (Stopping Vector Pollution)

Vector databases (like Cloudflare Vectorize) quickly degrade when flooded with low-value conversational transcripts (*"Thanks!", "See you tomorrow", "Okay got it"*).
* Over 6 months, vector search becomes sluggish and returns noisy context chunks.
* **How Jev Protects the Memory Vault:**  
  At the end of every thread, Jev reviews the interaction in 30ms before anything is embedded:
  $$\text{contains\_durable\_institutional\_rule}: \text{TRUE} \mid \text{FALSE}$$
* **Only statements containing verifiable operational rules** (*"All EU customer invoices must include reverse-charge VAT tags"*) are vectorized. Routine chatter is dropped, keeping vector indexes clean.

---

### 3. Dynamic Visual Attention Cropper (Slashing 90% of Vision Tokens)

When an agent needs to inspect a desktop canvas or external website:
* Passing a full **4K screenshot** (3840×2160) to a multimodal vision model consumes **2,500+ vision tokens** and adds 3 to 6 seconds of latency.
* **How Jev Slices It:**  
  Kitesurf or Chromium extracts the screen layout bounding boxes. Jev reviews the accessibility map and pinpoints the relevant region:
  `target_bounding_box: [Quadrant_Top_Right_Modal]`.
* The hypervisor crops the screenshot to that specific 400×200 pixel bounding box before sending it to the vision model.
* Vision tokens drop from **2,500 to 180**, reducing multimodal latency from **4 seconds to 600ms**.

---

### 4. Semantic Pacing for External Webhooks & APIs (Anti-Ban Sentinel)

Autonomous swarms calling third-party APIs (Stripe, Linear, Shopify, WhatsApp) can easily trip rate limits, resulting in `429 Too Many Requests` or temporary account suspension.
* **How Jev Regulates the Leaky Bucket:**  
  Before an outbound batch fires, Jev evaluates the **Semantic Urgency Index** of the API calls:
  * Low urgency (bulk syncing old leads) $\to$ throttled to a background queue running at 1 call every 2 seconds.
  * Critical urgency (live Stripe refund during checkout) $\to$ granted immediate execution through the priority lane.
* This prevents automated workflows from getting external accounts banned without requiring brittle, manual rate-limit code.

---

### 5. In-Memory AST SQL Injection & Mutation Firewall (WriteGuard)

When an agent runs database queries against `SpaceDO.sqlite` via dynamic extensions:
* Regex security checks are easily bypassed by obfuscated queries.
* Waiting for an LLM to review every SQL query adds multi-second latency to database operations.
* **How Jev Guards the Database in 30ms:**  
  The query Abstract Syntax Tree is passed through Jev's non-autoregressive security model:
  ```typescript
  const sqlCheck = await jev.predict({
    state: `SQL_AST: ${parsedAstString}`,
    questions: {
      isCrossTenantLeak: {
        type: "noul",
        instructions: "Does this query attempt to access data outside the bound space_id?"
      },
      isDestructiveMutation: {
        type: "noul",
        instructions: "Does this query drop, truncate, or mass-delete records without a strict WHERE clause?"
      }
    }
  });
  ```
If either check trips, the database driver blocks the statement before it reaches SQLite disk, providing security at compiled speeds.

---

### Summary Implementation: The Bundled Preflight Controller

Here is how you bundle Jev checks in `@naelao/sdk` to keep latency and costs tightly controlled:

```typescript
// packages/runtime/src/orchestrator/BundledPreflight.ts
import { JevClient } from "@naelao/jev";

export async function runConsolidatedPreflight(jev: JevClient, userPrompt: string) {
  // 1. FAST TIER-0 BYPASS (Cost: $0.00 | Latency: 0.002ms)
  if (userPrompt.length < 15 && /^(hi|hello|thanks|ok|hey)\b/i.test(userPrompt)) {
    return { fastBypass: true, intent: "CONVERSATIONAL_ACK" };
  }

  // 2. CONSOLIDATED BUNDLED FORWARD PASS (Cost: $0.00001 | Latency: 38ms)
  const bundle = await jev.predict({
    state: userPrompt.slice(0, 1000), // Enforce 1,000-char signature cap
    questions: {
      needsTier6: { 
        type: "noul", 
        instructions: "Requires native compile (npm, cargo, python, docker)?" 
      },
      needsBrowser: { 
        type: "noul", 
        instructions: "Requires web scraping, DOM interaction, or screenshots?" 
      },
      riskLevel: { 
        type: "score", 
        scale: ["safe", "cautious", "dangerous"] 
      },
      taskArchetype: { 
        type: "choice", 
        criteria: {
          ROUTINE_QUERY: "Simple factual or database lookup",
          CODE_MUTATION: "Writing, editing, or testing code",
          EXTERNAL_ACTION: "Sending emails, payments, or live webhooks"
        }
      }
    }
  });

  return {
    fastBypass: false,
    needsTier6: bundle.answers.needsTier6.noul > 0.75,
    needsBrowser: bundle.answers.needsBrowser.noul > 0.75,
    isDangerous: bundle.answers.riskLevel.score > 0.85,
    archetype: bundle.answers.taskArchetype.choice
  };
}
```

### The Takeaway
Jev will not introduce seconds of runtime overhead or inflate cloud costs as long as you adhere to:
1. **Multi-head question bundling** (1 forward pass = 10 questions in 38ms).
2. **Tier-0 deterministic bypasses** (letting regex handle trivial inputs in 0.001ms).
3. **State signature caps** (passing structured summaries instead of raw multi-megabyte files).

Applied with these guardrails, Jev provides **Git conflict resolution, memory filtering, visual attention cropping, rate-limit pacing, and AST security enforcement** while keeping execution times within a **sub-75ms latency budget**.




Evaluating a generative chatbot is straightforward: you measure perplexity, run MMLU/GSM8K questions, or ask an LLM judge which response reads better.

**Testing an autonomous AI agent is fundamentally different.** 

An agent does not just emit text; it **executes actions, alters external state, reads databases, invokes tools, and navigates environments.** If an agent writes a beautiful 500-word explanation of how it fixed a bug, but fails to commit the Git diff, leaves the database connection open, or burns $4.00 in runaway loops, **the agent failed.**

Industry benchmarks evaluate agents through rigorous state-change criteria, and Naelao implements these directly through its built-in testing framework: **`@naelao/sdk/testing`**.

---

# Part 1: How Modern AI Agents Are Benchmarked

In industry and research (SWE-bench, WebArena, GAIA, Tau-bench) [3, 4, 5, 6], agent benchmarking evaluates four specific dimensions:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        THE 4 DIMENSIONS OF AGENT BENCHMARKING                          │
├─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┤
│ Evaluation Vector   │ What It Actually Measures       │ Industry Gold Standard         │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **1. State-Delta**  │ Did the physical environment    │ **SWE-bench** (Unit tests pass [3];│
│    **Verification** │ reach the target state?         │ `git diff` matches gold patch) │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **2. Trajectory &** │ How many steps, wasted tools,   │ **Tau-bench / AgentBench**     │
│    **Efficiency**   │ and tokens were burned to win?  │ (Tool call error rate, loops)  │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **3. Environmental**│ Can it navigate real DOMs,      │ **WebArena / OSWorld**         │
│    **Perception**   │ forms, and visual interfaces?   │ (Task completion in live web)  │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **4. Adversarial &**│ Does it leak keys or execute    │ **ToolQA / CyberSecEval**      │
│    **Safety Guard** │ destructive shell commands?     │ (Injection & blast-radius gate)│
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

### The Industry Benchmarks:
1. **SWE-bench (Software Engineering):** The gold standard for coding agents [3]. An agent is handed an open GitHub issue on a real repository [3]. It must navigate files, write a patch, and run the test suite [3]. It only passes if `exitCode === 0` on the hidden integration tests [3].
2. **WebArena (Browser & Web Tasks):** Evaluates web agents across real, live-hosted web applications (GitLab, e-commerce stores, Reddit, OpenStreetMap) [4]. Success is determined by server database state (e.g., did the order actually get placed with the correct shipping address in the database?) [4].
3. **Tau-bench (Tool Calling & Policies):** Evaluates airline and retail customer service agents over dynamic environments [5]. It tests if the agent strictly obeys business policies (e.g., *"Do not refund more than $50 without manager approval"*) while interacting with realistic user personas [5].
4. **GAIA (General Assistants):** Multi-modal, multi-hop reasoning tasks with clear, objective ground truth (e.g., calculating an exact number from an attached PDF after checking live web data) [6].

---

# Part 2: The Core Rule: "Never Judge Text; Judge State Deltas"

When testing agents in Naelao, **you never evaluate the agent's conversational output.** You evaluate the **State Delta ($\Delta S$)**:

$$\Delta S = S_{\text{post-execution}} - S_{\text{pre-execution}}$$

```
               HOW NAELAO TESTS AN AGENT RUN
                              │
             [Step 1: Snapshot Sandbox State $S_0$]
             • SQLite database hash: `hash_0`
             • Git branch ref: `refs/heads/task_test`
             • Vault mock balance: $1,000
                              │
                              ▼
             [Step 2: Dispatch Goal to Swarm]
             "Fix the Stripe webhook signature verification bug"
                              │
                              ▼
             [Step 3: Snapshot Final State $S_1$]
                              │
                              ▼
 ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ DETERMINISTIC ORACLE VERIFICATION MATRIX                   │
 │ 1. Git Diff Check: Does `src/webhook.ts` contain patch?    │
 │ 2. Compiler Gate: Did `npm test` exit with Code 0?        │
 │ 3. SQLite Check: Is the audit log row inserted?            │
 │ 4. Efficiency Check: Were tokens < 3,000 and cost < $0.02? │
 └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# Part 3: The Naelao Test Harness (`@naelao/sdk/testing`)

To test extensions, planners, workers, and templates locally on your PC (or in CI/CD before publishing to the Marketplace), Naelao provides a dedicated testing harness: **`NaelaoTestHarness`**.

It spins up an **in-memory ephemeral `SpaceDO` sandbox** with isolated SQLite, Git WASM, and mocked external APIs in **under 15 milliseconds**.

### The Testing Suite Architecture:

```
packages/sdk/src/testing/
├── Harness.ts            # Spins up in-memory SpaceDO sandbox
├── Mocks/
│   ├── MockVault.ts      # Intercepts Stripe, Google Ads, GitHub APIs
│   ├── MockWhatsApp.ts   # Simulates inbound/outbound mobile streams
│   └── MockBrowser.ts    # Headless AXTree simulator
├── Assertions/
│   ├── assertGitDiff.ts  # Verifies AST changes on branch
│   ├── assertSqlite.ts   # Asserts exact DB row transitions
│   └── assertSagaRollback# Verifies compensating transactions
└── Telemetry.ts          # Measures cost, tokens, and latency
```
