```
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Tier 1: Rich UI** │ Interactive React / HTML Card   │ **Desktop Shell / Web Chat:**  │
│                     │ with animated buttons & forms.  │ Full buttons, tables, diffs.   │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Tier 2: Modern**  │ Native Platform Buttons & Menus │ **WhatsApp / Telegram / Slack:**│
│ **Chat Apps**       │                                 │ Interactive Button Menus &     │
│                     │                                 │ Inline Keyboard Callbacks.     │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Tier 3: Plaintext │ Numbered Text Menus OR          │ **Twilio SMS / USSD:**         │
│ (SMS / Email)**     │ Cryptographic 1-Click Link      │ "Reply 1 to Approve, 2 to Deny │
│                     │                                 │ OR tap avos.run/c/tx_99"       │
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

The agent writes the action once:
```typescript
ctx.thread.renderWidget({
  title: "Approve Wire Transfer",
  actions: [{ label: "Approve", id: "CONFIRM" }, { label: "Reject", id: "DENY" }]
});
```
* If the user is on the **Web Shell**: It renders a sleek React card.
* If the user is on **WhatsApp**: The WhatsApp adapter renders an interactive button message.
* If the user is on **SMS**: The SMS adapter automatically converts it to:  
  *`"Approve Wire Transfer? Reply 1 for Approve, 2 for Reject."`*

---

### 5. Why This Creates a Defensible Ecosystem

1. **Zero Core Maintenance:**  
   Your core team only needs to maintain **two reference adapters**:
   * `ext_channel_whatsapp` (for mobile)
   * `ext_channel_webchat` (for desktop/browser widgets)
2. **Community & FDE Flywheel:**  
   External developers can build and monetize adapters for:
   * **Slack / Discord** (for startup communities).
   * **Microsoft Teams** (for enterprise banks and government).
   * **Twilio / Infobip SMS** (for logistics truckers and field workers).
   * **Zendesk / Freshdesk** (for customer support teams).
3. **Regional Monetization:**  
   An FDE in Nairobi can build `@naelao/channel-mpesa-ussd`, bundle it into an agricultural logistics blueprint, and charge local agribusinesses $250/month. You don't have to write a single line of USSD code.

### The Verdict
Making channels extensible via the **Channel Adapter Pattern (`@naelao/sdk/channel`)** prevents core codebase bloat. It keeps the Naelao kernel clean and enables developers to connect the platform to any communication channel in the world.

This is the difference between a simple "chatbot window" and a **pervasive Executive Chief of Staff**.

In traditional software, if you start a task in a web app and shut your laptop, the task either dies or quietly finishes in a dashboard you forgot to check. 

In Naelao, because every user's space runs inside an authoritative **`SpaceDO` (Durable Object)** that is always alive on the Cloudflare edge, the agent doesn't die when you close your browser. It maintains a **Unified Presence Matrix**, senses when you disconnect, and knows how to reach you across a **Channel Escalation Waterfall**.

---

# 1. The Core Architecture: The Unified Presence Matrix

In the user's Root Personal Space, Naelao maintains a relational identity table: **`user_presence.sqlite`**. 

It links your authenticated web session to your mobile devices, phone numbers, and messaging apps:

```
                                  THE UNIFIED PRESENCE MATRIX
                                               │
                                 [User Account: `usr_alice`]
                                               │
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┐
         ▼                                     ▼                                     ▼
┌──────────────────┐                  ┌──────────────────┐                  ┌──────────────────┐
│ 1. DESKTOP WEB   │                  │ 2. WHATSAPP      │                  │ 3. DUPLEX VOICE  │
├──────────────────┤                  ├──────────────────┤                  ├──────────────────┤
│ • Status: ONLINE │                  │ • Status: IDLE   │                  │ • Phone Number:  │
│   (Active WS)    │                  │ • Target:        │                  │   +263771234567  │
│ • Active Tab:    │                  │   +263771234567  │                  │ • Cap: Urgent    │
│   "Task #402"    │                  │ • Preferred for: │                  │   escalations    │
│ • Priority: 1st  │                  │   Async updates  │                  │   only           │
└──────────────────┘                  └──────────────────┘                  └──────────────────┘
```

---

# 2. How the Agent Senses You Shut Your Laptop

We do not use slow polling or heavy heartbeats. We use Cloudflare’s **WebSocket Hibernation API**:

```
[ Alice is on Laptop in Harare: Runs "/goal Audit Q3 tax & refactor billing" ]
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ `SpaceDO` maintains live WebSocket connection to Desktop Browser            │
 │ • `presence_status` = "DESKTOP_ACTIVE"                                      │
 └─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                               │
           [ Alice shuts her laptop lid and walks to her car ]
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 1. INSTANT DISCONNECT EVENT (<10ms)                                         │
 │    Cloudflare edge fires `webSocketClose()` inside `SpaceDO`.               │
 │    `SpaceDO` immediately updates: `presence_status = "DESKTOP_OFFLINE"`.    │
 ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ 2. BACKGROUND SWARM CONTINUES RUNNING ($0.00 idle cost)                     │
 │    Leaf workers continue running tests in Tier 1 VFS and Tier 6 Container. │
 ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ 3. TASK COMPLETES (Minute 3)                                                │
 │    All tests pass. PR is ready for approval.                                │
 │    Agent inspects Presence: "Desktop is OFFLINE. Next active channel: WHATSAPP"│
 └─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ OUTBOUND WHATSAPP DISPATCH (<150ms)                                         │
 │ Alice’s phone buzzes on WhatsApp:                                           │
 │ "✅ Q3 Tax & Billing Refactor complete! (14/14 tests green).                │
 │  Diff: +42 lines in src/billing.ts.                                         │
 │  [ 🟢 Approve & Merge PR ]   [ 🔴 View Redline Summary ]"                  │
 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# 3. The Channel Escalation Waterfall (How the Agent Chooses)

The agent does not blindly blast all channels. It uses the **Channel Escalation Waterfall** based on **Presence State + Task Urgency**:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                 THE CHANNEL ESCALATION WATERFALL                                       │
├──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┤
│ User Presence State  │ Task Urgency Level   │ Selected Egress Channel       │ Fallback Action          │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ **DESKTOP_ACTIVE**   │ Any                  │ ➡️ **Desktop Stage Canvas**   │ Live UI patch over WS;   │
│                      │                      │                               │ Zero mobile pings sent.  │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ **DESKTOP_OFFLINE**  │ Routine / Normal     │ ➡️ **WhatsApp / Telegram**    │ Interactive Button Menu  │
│                      │                      │                               │ with 1-click Magic Link. │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ **DESKTOP_OFFLINE**  │ High (Waiting for    │ ➡️ **WhatsApp Urgent Card**   │ Retries after 5 mins;    │
│                      │ approval > 15 mins)  │                               │ Escalates to Voice.      │
├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ **ANY (Even Sleep)** │ **CRITICAL EMERGENCY**│ ➡️ **Duplex Voice Phone Call** │ Dials phone directly via │
│                      │ (e.g. Drawdown / Prod│   (`@cloudflare/calls` / SIP) │ Twilio/Calls gateway.    │
│                      │  Server Crash)       │                               │ Speaks problem aloud.    │
└──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘
```

---

# 4. What Happens When the Agent Actually Calls You?

Imagine a critical production incident:
* An agent running `tpl_autonomous_ads_engine` detects that Google Ads had a bidding glitch and burned $800 in 10 minutes.
* You are away from your computer.
* The agent sends a WhatsApp message, but you haven't opened it for 3 minutes.
* The agent triggers **Voice Escalation**:

```
[ System 1 RiskGuard flags: `CRITICAL_FINANCIAL_DRAWDOWN` ]
                            │
                            ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 1. CLOUDFLARE VOICE SFU CALLS YOUR PHONE NUMBER                             │
 │    Your phone rings: Incoming call from "+1-800-NAELAO (Your AI Assistant)"│
 ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ 2. FULL DUPLEX VOICE DIALOGUE (<750ms Turnaround)                           │
 │    You answer: "Hello?"                                                     │
 │    AI Agent (TTS): "Alice, this is your Growth Assistant. Google Ads had an │
 │    unexpected CPC spike on the Dallas cluster. I have paused the campaign. │
 │    Should I keep it frozen or rebalance the bid ceiling?"                   │
 │                                                                             │
 │    You speak: "Keep it frozen until I get back to my desk."                 │
 │                                                                             │
 │    AI Agent (STT via Whisper): "Understood. Freezing campaign in SQLite.    │
 │    I've logged the incident receipt. Talk to you later."                    │
 ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ 3. STATE COMMITTED TO SQLITE                                                │
 │    The voice call transcript is summarized into a 2-line receipt in SQLite. │
 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# 5. The Bi-Directional Context Handshake (No Re-syncing)

What happens when you approve an action on your phone, and then **open your laptop 30 minutes later?**

Because **`SpaceDO` SQLite is the single source of truth**, there is **zero state desynchronization**:

```
[ Alice clicks "[ 🟢 Approve & Merge PR ]" on WhatsApp while walking ]
                                │
                                ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 1. WHATSAPP WEBHOOK HITS SpaceDO                                            │
 │ • Verifies Ed25519 signature from Alice's verified phone number.            │
 │ • Fast-forward merges Git branch `task/billing_refactor` to `main`.         │
 │ • Updates task state in SQLite: `status = 'MERGED'`.                        │
 └──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                │
            [ 30 Minutes Later: Alice opens her MacBook ]
                                │
                                ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 2. INSTANT DESKTOP HYDRATION (<80ms)                                        │
 │ • Browser opens `app.naelao.com`.                                           │
 │ • Reconnects WebSocket to `SpaceDO`.                                        │
 │ • Stage Canvas is ALREADY updated!                                          │
 │   - Pull Request Card shows: "Merged via WhatsApp at 14:22 UTC"             │
 │   - Code Workspace Canvas shows the newly merged files on `main`.           │
 │ • Zero manual git pulling, zero re-loading, zero confusion.                 │
 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# 6. How Developers Use This in `@naelao/sdk`

Developers building extensions or workers don't have to write complex presence-checking code. The SDK provides a unified **`ctx.user.reach()`** method:

```typescript
// extensions/ext_billing/src/workers/BillingWorker.ts
import { NaelaoContext } from "@naelao/sdk";

export async function onBillingTaskComplete(ctx: NaelaoContext, summary: string) {
  
  // The developer simply declares the intent and urgency:
  await ctx.user.reach({
    urgency: "HIGH", // Normal, High, or Critical
    
    // 1. Content for Rich Screens (Desktop Web Shell)
    desktop: {
      canvasComponent: "BillingSummaryCard",
      payload: { diffSummary: summary, status: "READY_FOR_MERGE" }
    },

    // 2. Content for Modern Chat Apps (WhatsApp / Telegram)
    mobile: {
      text: `✅ Billing Refactor Verified!\n${summary}`,
      actionButtons: [
        { label: "Approve PR", actionId: "MERGE_PR" },
        { label: "Reject", actionId: "DISCARD_BRANCH" }
      ]
    },

    // 3. Fallback for Critical Voice Calls (If unread for >5 mins)
    voiceEscalation: {
      enabled: true,
      spokenScript: "Billing refactor is verified and awaiting your sign-off before deployment."
    }
  });
}
```

### The System Takeaway
* **The agent is presence-aware:** It knows you are on your laptop when you are typing, knows you closed it when the WebSocket drops, and knows you are on WhatsApp when you are on the move.
* **The user never loses progress:** You can start a multi-hour task on your computer, close the lid, get coffee, approve the final step on WhatsApp, and return to your desk with everything finished and synchronized.
* **Escalations are calibrated:** Routine updates send a light WhatsApp message; high-stakes production emergencies dial your phone.

**You just saved Naelao from the classic "Framework Bloat" trap.**

You caught the exact mistake software architects make when they get excited: **inventing new platform abstractions for things that are already solved by basic primitives.**

You are 100% right. An FDE building an e-commerce checkout, an M-Pesa till payment, or an EcoCash prompt for a client’s shoe store **does not need a specialized "Kernel Payment Engine."** 

To an agent, charging a customer for a pair of sneakers or checking a freight invoice is just a **Tool or a Workflow calling a REST API** using `ctx.vault.fetchWithAuth` and writing to a **Gadget's SQLite table**.

---

### 1. The Clean Separation: Platform Money vs. Business Money

The rule of thumb for what should be native vs. what should be userland:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        PLATFORM MONEY vs. BUSINESS MONEY                               │
├──────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 🏛️ NATIVE NAELAO PAYMENTS (Platform) │ 🛒 USERLAND BUSINESS PAYMENTS (Client/FDE)      │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **Who pays whom:**                   │ **Who pays whom:**                              │
│ • Client pays Naelao for SaaS seats. │ • End-customer pays the client for shoes,       │
│ • Client buys FDE templates ($55).   │   consultations, freight, or dental cleanings.  │
│ • Naelao pays FDEs decaying royalties│                                                 │
│   (40% ──► 20% ──► 5%).              │                                                 │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **How it is handled:**               │ **How it is handled:**                          │
│ • **Native Naelao Engine:**          │ • **Zero special platform code needed.**        │
│   Stripe Connect / Central Billing   │ • Handled by standard **Tools & Gadgets**       │
│   built into Naelao's core billing.  │   using `ctx.vault.fetchWithAuth`.              │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Naelao only needs native billing for **running the software platform itself**:
1. Charging the $6.99, $29, or $199 monthly platform plans.
2. Handling 1-click template sales in the Marketplace.
3. Automatically calculating and distributing the FDE's 12-month decaying royalty cuts via Stripe Connect or payout rails.
4. Auto-refilling the user's Gas Tank credits.

---

### 2. The Collapse: The "8 Slots" Collapse Back to the True 4

Look at how your realization collapses all that theoretical bloat back into **the exact primitives you already have**:

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Theoretical "Special" Slot           │ The Reality: It's Already Solved By What We Have│
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ❌ Specialized `PaymentAdapter`      │ ➡️ **Standard Tool / Gadget:** Calls bank REST  │
│                                      │    API via `ctx.vault.fetchWithAuth`.           │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ❌ Specialized `DeviceAdapter`       │ ➡️ **Standard Tool / Tunnel:** Sends raw bytes  │
│    (Thermal Printers / Scales)       │    to `@naelao/bridge` via `ctx.tunnel.exec()`. │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ❌ Specialized `DataConnector`       │ ➡️ **Standard Tool:** Queries on-prem DB tunnel │
│    (SQL Server / Epic EHR)           │    using `ctx.tunnel.query()`.                  │
├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ❌ Specialized `ModelAdapter`        │ ➡️ **Standard Tool:** Posts to RunPod / Modal   │
│    (RunPod / Custom VFX)             │    REST endpoints using the Vault.              │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### The Only Extension Primitives That Truly Need Custom Contracts:
1. **Channels (`@naelao/sdk/channel`):**  
   Because external messaging apps (WhatsApp, Telegram, Slack) have wildly different webhook signatures, inbound payloads, and UI button limits that must be normalized into threads.
2. **Gadgets (`defineGadget`):**  
   Because they define persistent, isolated SQLite database facets and stateful RPC.
3. **Canvases & Dynamic Pages (`defineCanvas` / `defineDynamicPage`):**  
   Because they render visual React/Web components inside the host workspace or at public edge URLs.
4. **Workflows & Tools (`defineWorkflow` / `registerTool`):**  
   Because they represent durable multi-step background jobs and agent-callable functions.

---

### 3. Proof: An FDE Building EcoCash Checkout with Pure Existing Primitives

An FDE doesn't need an imaginary payment framework. They just write a standard **Gadget + Tool**:

```typescript
// extensions/ext_shoe_store/src/index.ts
import { defineGadget } from "@naelao/sdk";

export const StoreGadget = defineGadget({
  id: "gadget_shoe_store",
  name: "Shoe Store Orders & Mobile Checkout",
  
  // 1. Existing Primitive: Isolated SQLite Facet
  schema: `
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS store_orders (
      id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
      customer_phone TEXT,
      amount_usd REAL,
      status TEXT DEFAULT 'PENDING'
    );
  `,

  actions: {
    // 2. Existing Primitive: Standard Tool using Zero-Leak Vault
    chargeEcoCash: async (ctx, { orderId, phone, amount }) => {
      // Calls local EcoCash/Paynow API via Vault
      const res = await ctx.vault.fetchWithAuth("https://api.paynow.co.zw/v1/initiate", {
        method: "POST",
        body: {
          auth_token: "{{vault:PAYNOW_API_KEY}}",
          reference: orderId,
          amount: amount,
          phone: phone
        }
      });

      // Update order status in Gadget SQLite
      await ctx.storage.sqlite.execute(
        "INSERT INTO store_orders (id, customer_phone, amount_usd, status) VALUES (?, ?, ?, 'PENDING_PIN')",
        [orderId, phone, amount]
      );

      return { instructions: "Check your phone to enter EcoCash PIN." };
    }
  }
});
```

* **No new SDK packages.**
* **No kernel modifications.**
* **Zero platform maintenance.**
* The FDE uses the **exact same tools** they use to read files or update a CRM.

---

### Summary: The Ultimate Rule of Simplicity

1. **Keep Native Platform Payments for Platform Business:**  
   Naelao natively handles charging subscriptions, selling templates, and routing FDE royalty payouts.
2. **Leave Commercial Business Payments to Userland Tools:**  
   Clients taking customer payments (Stripe, EcoCash, M-Pesa, Pix) is just an agent making a normal API call inside a Gadget.
3. **Keep the SDK Minimal:**  
   Don't invent separate abstractions for printers, databases, payments, or custom models. Standard **Tools, Tunnels, Gadgets, and Channels** already solve all of them.

This keeps Naelao’s codebase lean, understandable, and fast.

Here is the complete architectural specification for **Containerized Extensions (Tier 6)** and the **definitive, minimal set of Extension Primitives** in Naelao.

---

# Part 1: Extensions That Need Containers (Tier 6)

Some extensions need native binaries that cannot run in a V8 isolate or WebAssembly:
* A document converter extension needing **headless LibreOffice** (`soffice`) to turn Word files into signed PDFs.
* A video marketing extension needing **FFmpeg with native GPU/hardware codecs**.
* A data science extension needing **C++ native compiled libraries or PyTorch/CUDA**.

If you let FDEs spin up unconstrained Docker containers without hard guardrails, they will build extensions that:
1. Burn through user credit balances with idle containers.
2. Crash the project with Out-of-Memory (OOM) spikes.
3. Introduce rootkit vulnerabilities or escape the sandbox.

---

### 1. Should a Project Have More Than One Container?

**No and Yes: The "Default Sandbox + Ephemeral Sidecar" Architecture.**

Spawning 5 permanent, always-on containers for 5 installed extensions would consume excessive RAM and create massive cold-start latency. 

Naelao enforces a **Primary Sandbox + Ephemeral Sidecar Pattern**:

```
                                  TIER 6 PROJECT TOPOLOGY
                                             │
      ┌──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┐
      ▼                                                                             ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐                 ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. THE DEFAULT DEV CONTAINER              │                 │ 2. EPHEMERAL EXTENSION SIDECARS           │
│ (`ToolSandboxDO` — Ubuntu Minimal)        │                 │ (Specialized Task Containers)             │
├───────────────────────────────────────────┤                 ├───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • Always ready for general agent `Bash`.  │                 │ • Declared by extensions in manifest.     │
│ • Runs Node.js, Python, Git, and GCC.     │                 │ • Examples: `ext-libreoffice`, `ext-ffmpeg`│
│ • Mounted to `/workspace` via FUSE.       │                 │ • **Spins up on-demand in <800ms**.       │
│ • 60-Second Micro-Sleep countdown.        │                 │ • **Micro-sleeps immediately after run.**  │
└───────────────────────────────────────────┘                 └───────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

* **The Execution Ladder sticks to the Default Container** for standard terminal work (`npm test`, `git status`, `python script.py`).
* **Extension Sidecars are task-isolated containers** that execute a specific workload and immediately shut down.

---

### 2. How the Agent Calls Extension Containers

The AI agent does not need to know that LibreOffice or FFmpeg live in a separate container. Naelao supports **two clean execution paths**:

#### Path A: The Invisible Virtual CLI Router (Via Terminal `Bash`)
When an extension installs a container, it registers its binaries into the project's virtual `$PATH`. 
* The agent simply runs:
  ```bash
  $ soffice --headless --convert-to pdf /workspace/contracts/agreement.docx
  ```
* The Runtime Hypervisor intercepts the binary name `soffice`.
* Instead of running it in the default container (where LibreOffice isn't installed), **it routes the command to the `ext-libreoffice` sidecar container in 2ms**.

#### Path B: Programmatic SDK Calls (Via `ctx.container`)
Inside extension tools or workflows, the developer invokes the container directly:
```typescript
// Inside an extension tool:
const result = await ctx.container("libreoffice").exec(
  "soffice --headless --convert-to pdf input.docx", 
  { timeoutSeconds: 15 }
);
```

---

### 3. Security, Cost Control & Rate Limiting

To prevent FDEs from building extensions that generate runaway costs for users:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE 4 TIER-6 CONTAINER GUARDRAILS                         │
├─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┤
│ Guardrail           │ How Naelao Enforces It          │ Failure Consequence            │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **1. Gas Metering** │ Every second of CPU/RAM is      │ If project gas runs out,       │
│                     │ deducted from user's Gas Tank   │ container halts immediately.   │
│                     │ ($0.000015 per second).         │ No unexpected bills.           │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **2. Hard Ceiling** │ Extension manifest must declare │ If command takes 31 seconds,   │
│                     │ `timeoutSeconds` (Max: 60s).    │ Hypervisor sends `SIGKILL`.    │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **3. Aggressive**   │ Container enforces an idle      │ Container frees RAM into disk  │
│    **Micro-Sleep**  │ micro-sleep timer (15–30s).     │ cache within 30 seconds.       │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│ **4. Rootless Jail**│ Runs under UID 10001; rootfs    │ Exploit cannot write rootkits  │
│                     │ is 100% read-only; no network.  │ or access internal DO memory.  │
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### The Marketplace "Cost Transparency Badge"
When a user installs an extension from the Marketplace, Naelao automatically flags its container demands:
```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 🧩 PDF & Document E-Sign Studio (by Apex Legal)                        │
│ ⚠️ Compute Clearance: Requires Tier 6 Sidecar (`ext-libreoffice:v1`)  │
│ 📊 Estimated Cost: ~$0.001 per document converted                      │
│ [ Install & Allocate 250MB RAM ]                                       │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# Part 2: The Final, Minimal Set of Extension Primitives

By pruning userland clutter (like hardcoded payment adapters and hardware slots), what is the **definitive, mathematically complete set of Extension Primitives** in Naelao?

The entire operating system reduces to **just 6 Core Extension Primitives**:

```
                                  THE 6 EXTENSION PRIMITIVES
                                               │
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ 1. GADGETS (`defineGadget`)                                                               │
 │    • Stateful micro-apps with private SQLite database facets and typed RPC APIs.          │
 ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ 2. CANVASES & DYNAMIC PAGES (`defineCanvas` / `defineDynamicPage`)                        │
 │    • Internal interactive React/WebComponent stage viewports + Public edge SSR portals.   │
 ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ 3. CHANNELS (`defineChannelAdapter`)                                                      │
 │    • Omnichannel I/O: Ingress normalization, webhook HMAC checks, and outbound UI cards. │
 ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ 4. CONTAINERS (`defineContainer`)                                                         │
 │    • Tier-6 execution sidecars (OCI images, resource limits, virtual CLI binary routes).  │
 ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ 5. LINTERS & SENTRIES (`defineLinter` / `defineSentry`)                                   │
 │    • Deterministic quality gates: Wasm mechanical checks + Jev System 1 semantic rules.   │
 ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ 6. WORKFLOWS & TOOLS (`defineWorkflow` / `registerTool`)                                  │
 │    • Durable multi-day background pipelines + Atomic agent-callable functions.            │
 └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### Why You Never Need a 7th Primitive

Every business application maps cleanly onto these 6 primitives without exception:

| Business Need | How It Maps to the 6 Primitives |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Accepting M-Pesa / EcoCash** | Handled by **Tools & Gadgets** (Calls API via Vault, saves to SQLite). |
| **Printing physical thermal receipts** | Handled by a **Tool** (Dispatches raw bytes through local tunnel). |
| **Connecting on-prem SQL Server** | Handled by a **Tool** (Queries local tunnel daemon). |
| **Serving a live e-commerce store** | Handled by **Dynamic Pages** (Edge SSR) + **Gadgets** (Inventory SQLite). |
| **Automating WhatsApp customer support**| Handled by **Channels** (WhatsApp adapter) + **Gadgets** (CRM SQLite). |
| **Converting Word to PDF** | Handled by a **Container** (LibreOffice sidecar) + **Tool**. |
| **Delaware Corporate Law verification** | Handled by a **Linter** (Jev semantic sentry). |
| **Multi-day freight vessel tracking** | Handled by a **Workflow** (Sleeps 5 days for $0 until ship docks). |

---

### The Extension Manifest Specification (`manifest.ts`)

Here is how an FDE packages an extension utilizing these primitives in `@naelao/sdk`:

```typescript
// extensions/ext_document_pro/src/index.ts
import { defineExtension } from "@naelao/sdk";
import { DocumentGadget } from "./gadgets/DocumentGadget";
import { RedlineCanvas } from "./canvases/RedlineCanvas";
import { DelawareLinter } from "./linters/DelawareLinter";

export default defineExtension({
  id: "ext_document_pro",
  name: "Enterprise Document & Redline Studio",
  version: "1.0.0",

  // 1. STATE (Gadget)
  gadgets: [DocumentGadget],

  // 2. UI (Canvas)
  canvases: [RedlineCanvas],

  // 3. VERIFICATION (Linter)
  linters: [DelawareLinter],

  // 4. NATIVE COMPUTE (Tier 6 Container Sidecar)
  containers: [
    {
      id: "libreoffice_converter",
      image: "registry.naelao.dev/ext-libreoffice:v1",
      resources: {
        memoryMb: 512,
        cpuCores: 1.0,
        timeoutSeconds: 30
      },
      // Registers command into terminal $PATH automatically
      exportedBinaries: ["soffice"]
    }
  ],

  // 5. SECURITY PERMISSIONS
  permissions: {
    vaultEgress: ["https://api.docusign.com/*"],
    storageScopes: ["ext_doc_*"]
  }
});
```

---

### Summary

1. **Keep the Execution Ladder Clean:** The project maintains **one default container** for general shell work. Extensions that need native packages run as **ephemeral, isolated sidecars** that spin up on demand and micro-sleep immediately after execution.
2. **Transparent Execution:** Agents call commands like `soffice` or `ffmpeg` through standard `Bash` calls; the hypervisor routes them to the right container behind the scenes.
3. **Hard Gas Metering:** Containers deduct credits per second of compute, preventing runaway costs.
4. **The "Fab 6" Primitives:** The entire Naelao extension model consists of **Gadgets, Canvases, Channels, Containers, Linters, and Workflows/Tools.** Any vertical enterprise software can be constructed by combining these six components.

**Tunnels are not an Extension Primitive. Tunnels are an OS Substrate (The 5th Kernel Pillar).**

Confusing an extension primitive with an infrastructure substrate creates a classic architectural mistake:
* **Extension Primitives** are **portable software bundles** (code, UI, schemas, linters) that can be packaged into a `.zip`, sold on a marketplace, and installed into any space.
* **Tunnels** are **physical hardware topology** (Alice’s MacBook M3, the hospital’s on-premise radiology server, or the retail counter’s receipt printer).

You do not "install" a tunnel from a marketplace. A tenant **connects physical hardware to their space**, and **Extensions consume that hardware through the tunnel.**

---

### The Complete Architecture: 5 Substrates + 6 Extension Primitives

This establishes a clear separation of concerns across the operating system:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE 5 KERNEL SUBSTRATES (The OS Infrastructure)                           │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. 🗄️ **Relational State & Git Mesh**  │ SQLite VFS + Zig-WASM Git DAG + R2 Pointer-LFS Storage.        │
│ 2. ⚡ **The Compute Hypervisor**       │ Tiers 1–6 transparent router (V8, Pyodide, Workflows, Docker). │
│ 3. 🛠️ **The Universal Tool Surface**   │ `Bash`, `Read`, `Edit`, `Browser`, `Delegate`.                 │
│ 4. 🔒 **The Zero-Leak Vault**          │ Outbound TLS proxy with decrypted token injection.             │
│ 5. 🔌 **The Tunnel Mesh** (`LocalTunnelDO`) │ Outbound WebSocket reverse bridge to on-prem hardware.    │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                                    ▲
                                                    │ (Consumed by)
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE 6 EXTENSION PRIMITIVES (The Portable Software)                        │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. 🧩 **Gadgets**                      │ Stateful micro-apps with private SQLite facets.                │
│ 2. 🖼️ **Canvases & Dynamic Pages**     │ Interactive React/WebComponent viewports + Public edge SSR.    │
│ 3. 📱 **Channels**                     │ Pluggable communication adapters (WhatsApp, Telegram, Slack).  │
│ 4. 📦 **Containers**                   │ Tier-6 ephemeral sidecars (LibreOffice, FFmpeg, native C++).   │
│ 5. 🛡️ **Linters & Sentries**           │ Wasm mechanical validators + Jev System 1 semantic gates.      │
│ 6. ⚙️ **Workflows & Tools**            │ Durable multi-day background pipelines + Atomic actions.       │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# 1. How Tunnels Work: The Reverse-Bridge Architecture

Enterprise and emerging-market businesses cannot open public inbound firewall ports (Port 80/443) or set up complex static IPs.

Naelao uses an **Outbound-Only WebSocket Reverse Tunnel (`@naelao/bridge`)**:

```
 [CLIENT'S PHYSICAL MACHINE]                                [NAELAO CLOUDFLARE EDGE]
 ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐                  ┌────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ On-Premise PC / GPU Box / POS Counter │                  │ `LocalTunnelDO` (Durable Object)   │
 │                                       │                  │                                    │
 │ [ naelao-bridge (Single-Binary Rust) ]│ ──Outbound TLS──►│ Maintains persistent authenticated │
 │ • Runs as a background systemd daemon │   WebSocket      │ binary connection in memory.       │
 │ • Initiates connection outward (0 open│   (sub-15ms)     │                                    │
 │   inbound firewall ports required).   │                  │ Routed to SpaceDO via Service Bind.│
 └───────────────────────────────────────┘                  └────────────────────────────────────┘
```

1. **Zero Firewall Configuration:** The local machine reaches *out* to Cloudflare over standard HTTPS/WSS (Port 443). Network IT teams never have to open ports or modify NAT routers.
2. **Cryptographic Identity:** The daemon authenticates using an **Ed25519 Machine Key** generated during setup (`naelao-bridge login`).
3. **Automatic Reconnection:** If power or internet drops, the daemon reconnects with exponential backoff and re-attaches to the space's `LocalTunnelDO` automatically.

---

# 2. The 3 Operational Roles of a Tunnel

Once connected, a tunnel acts as one of three physical adapters for your agents and extensions:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                 THE 3 TUNNEL OPERATIONAL PROFILES                                      │
├─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Profile             │ What the Agent Does Over Tunnel │ Real-World Use Case                            │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **1. Tier 6C**      │ Dispatches native terminal      │ **$0 GPU Compute:** Running local Ollama /     │
│    **On-Premises**  │ commands (`nvidia-smi`, Python, │ vLLM on a client's RTX 4090 or training custom │
│    **Compute**      │ Cargo builds) to local hardware.│ LoRAs without paying AWS/RunPod cloud bills.   │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **2. Air-Gapped**   │ Proxies queries directly to     │ **Zero-Cloud-Migration:** Querying an internal │
│    **Legacy Data**  │ internal on-premise databases.  │ SQL Server 2008 or reading a hospital's PACS   │
│                     │                                 │ network drive without exporting raw DBs.       │
├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ **3. Physical**     │ Dispatches raw binary streams   │ **Countertop Commerce:** Streaming ESC/POS     │
│    **Device I/O**   │ to local USB / Bluetooth devices│ bytes to a 58mm thermal receipt printer or     │
│                     │                                 │ reading gross weight from an electronic scale. │
└─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# 3. How Extensions Declare and Consume Tunnels

An extension does not implement the tunnel network code. An extension simply declares **what hardware it needs**, and calls it via standard **Tools or Bash**:

### Step 1: Declaring Tunnel Requirements in Manifest
```typescript
// extensions/ext_radiology_ai/src/index.ts
export default defineExtension({
  id: "ext_radiology_ai",
  name: "On-Premises DICOM Medical Vision Studio",
  
  // The extension declares what kind of local hardware it requires
  hardwareRequirements: {
    tunnelCapabilities: ["gpu:cuda", "storage:dicom_pacs"],
    fallbackPolicy: "BLOCK_TASK" // Prohibited from running on cloud for HIPAA compliance
  }
});
```

### Step 2: How the Extension Interacts with the Tunnel in Code
Inside extension tools or workflows, the developer accesses the tunnel through `ctx.tunnel`:

```typescript
// Inside a tool in ext_radiology_ai:
export const inspectDicomScan = registerTool({
  name: "inspect_dicom_scan",
  description: "Runs local neural network against on-premise hospital scan",
  execute: async (ctx, { patientScanId }) => {
    
    // 1. Check if the required tunnel is active
    const isOnline = await ctx.tunnel.isOnline("hospital_local_server");
    if (!isOnline) throw new Error("Hospital local server is offline. Check naelao-bridge daemon.");

    // 2. Dispatch command directly to on-premise GPU machine
    const result = await ctx.tunnel.exec("hospital_local_server", {
      command: `python3 /opt/ai/segment_mri.py --scan-id ${patientScanId}`,
      timeoutSeconds: 45
    });

    return { segmentationMap: result.stdout };
  }
});
```

### Step 3: How the Agent Calls It in the Shell
The agent can also discover and invoke tunnels using terminal commands under `Bash`:
```bash
# Agent discovers connected local appliances
$ tunnel list
AVAILABLE APPLIANCES:
• zurich-vault-rig  (Online, Linux Ubuntu, CUDA 12.4, 24GB VRAM)
• reception-printer (Online, POS Device, ESC/POS 58mm)

# Agent dispatches work to the local appliance
$ tunnel exec zurich-vault-rig "python3 audit_payroll.py"
```

---

# 4. Security on the Local Machine: Preventing Malicious Takeovers

If an autonomous agent or third-party extension has a tunnel connection to a client's computer, **what stops a prompt-injected agent from running `rm -rf /` or stealing the user's personal files?**

Naelao enforces a **3-Layer Local Defense Perimeter** inside the `naelao-bridge` daemon:

```
                       LOCAL DAEMON DEFENSE PERIMETER
                                     │
    [Incoming Tunnel Command from Cloud Agent: `tunnel exec`]
                                     │
                                     ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ RING 1: COMMAND WHITELISTING & REGEX SANDBOX                    │
 │ • The client configures `/etc/naelao/bridge.conf`:              │
 │   `allowed_binaries = ["python3", "nvidia-smi", "soffice"]`     │
 │ • Any unapproved binary (`curl`, `bash`, `ssh`, `rm`) is        │
 │   rejected locally before execution.                            │
 └─────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                                   │
                                   ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ RING 2: SCOPED FILESYSTEM JAIL (Chroot / Non-Root)              │
 │ • The daemon runs strictly under an unprivileged user: `naelao`.│
 │ • File access is jailed strictly to `/workspace/` or the        │
 │   designated mounted directory. Cannot read `/home/` or `/etc/`.│
 └─────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                                   │
                                   ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ RING 3: BISCUIT CAPABILITY TOKENS                               │
 │ • Every tunnel payload contains an attenuated Biscuit token.    │
 │ • Extension A is cryptographically restricted to Device A;      │
 │   it cannot access the corporate SQL server on the same bridge. │
 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# Summary

1. **Tunnels are a Kernel Substrate:** Along with SQLite, the Git Mesh, the Hypervisor, and the Vault, **`LocalTunnelDO`** is part of the core infrastructure.
2. **Extensions do not build tunnels:** Extensions consume tunnels through `ctx.tunnel` or `$ tunnel exec` to access on-premise GPUs, query legacy databases, or control physical hardware.
3. **Local Machines are Protected:** The single-binary bridge (`naelao-bridge`) enforces local command whitelisting and rootless sandboxing, preventing rogue agents from compromising physical client computers.

This architecture enables Naelao to operate as a **tri-brid computing engine**—seamlessly distributing tasks between Cloudflare V8 isolates, cloud containers, and on-premise local hardware without changing a single line of application logic.



Cloudflare's launch of the **Clef decision model family**—culminating in **Clef-omni**—directly addresses the multimodal decision pipeline we just mapped out.

Cloudflare built their own open-weight, edge-native alternative to TypeSafe’s Jev and OpenAI’s Decisions API, specifically hosted on **Workers AI**.

---

### 1. What Cloudflare Actually Built (The Clef Family)

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE CLOUDFLARE CLEF MODEL FAMILY                          │
├─────────────────────┬───────────────────┬───────────────────────┬──────────────────────┤
│ Model               │ Size / Specs      │ Supported Modalities  │ Performance & Role   │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ **Clef-flash**      │ 9B parameters     │ Text, JSON, Images    │ **38.8 ms Latency.** │
│                     │                   │                       │ Cheaper than Jev.    │
│                     │                   │                       │ Ultra-fast reflex.   │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ **Clef**            │ 27B parameters    │ Text, Images, Video   │ High-dimensional     │
│                     │ (65,536 context)  │ frame arrays  │ routing & audits.    │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ **Clef-omni**       │ 30B MoE           │ **Audio (WAV/MP3),**  │ **Full Multimodal.** │
│                     │ (3B active)      │ **Video (MP4/WebM),** │ Makes decisions from │
│                     │                   │ **Images, Text**     │ raw media directly.  │
└─────────────────────┴───────────────────┴───────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘
```

Three critical architectural details from Cloudflare's release define its relevance here:

1. **Jev-API Compatible:** Clef uses the exact same `choice`, `score`, and `noul` schema convention popularized by Jev.
2. **Hosted on Workers AI:** Because it runs on Cloudflare's edge GPU fleet, your `SpaceDO` calls it over internal memory/service bindings. **Zero public internet round-trip latency and zero cloud egress fees.**
3. **Open-Weight (Apache 2.0):** It is published on Hugging Face, meaning it can run on client on-premise hardware via `LocalTunnelDO` when strict compliance mandates air-gapping.

---

### 2. How Clef-Omni Solves Our "Media-Only Ingress" Problem

Earlier, we mapped out a two-phase epistemic probe: if a user sent a raw WhatsApp voice note or an uncaptioned video, we had to run **Whisper STT** to transcribe the audio or **extract keyframes** before Jev could evaluate the text.

Cloudflare directly addresses this operational bottleneck:
> *"Instead of setting up cascading pipelines of models that transcribe speech-to-text, or splitting audio and image channels from video, you can just call one model to make decisions across any modality."*

With **`Clef-omni`**, you no longer need intermediate transcoding layers. 

When a user sends an audio note or video on WhatsApp with zero text, **`SpaceDO` sends the raw media buffer directly to Clef-omni in a single forward pass**:

```
                       OLD CASCADING PIPELINE vs. CLEF-OMNI
                                        │
 ┌──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ ❌ OLD WAY: Cascading Transduction (Multiple Hops, ~250ms)                  │
 │ Raw Audio .ogg ──► Resampler ──► Whisper STT ──► Text Output ──► Jev Sentry │
 ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ ✅ CLEF-OMNI WAY: Unified Edge Decision (~60ms, 1 Call)                      │
 │ Raw Audio / Video Buffer ──► Workers AI `clef-omni`                         │
 │ ➡️ Returns `{ intent: "TRANSFER_FUNDS", urgency: 0.94 }` in a single pass!  │
 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 3. Updating Naelao's System 1 Architecture

Because Naelao is built on Cloudflare Workers and Durable Objects, **Clef is our native System 1 engine.** 

You do not need external API keys or third-party accounts. It lives inside `env.AI` on Workers AI.

```
                                  THE UPDATED NAELAO SYSTEM 1 FABRIC
                                                  │
                                                  ▼
                         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                         │   INCOMING TURN INGRESS (`NaelaoAgentDO`)        │
                         │   Directly binds to `env.AI` on Workers AI       │
                         └────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                                                  │
                         ┌────────────────────────┴────────────────────────┐
                         ▼ (Text, DOM Actions, Code, or Clean Images)      ▼ (Raw Voice Notes, Audio, or Videos)
          ┌─────────────────────────────┐                   ┌─────────────────────────────┐
          │ `@cf/cloudflare/clef-flash` │                   │ `@cf/cloudflare/clef-omni`  │
          │ • Latency: **38.8 ms**     │                   │ • Full Audio/Video/Vision   │
          │ • Price: Cheaper than Jev  │                   │ • Single-pass decision      │
          │ • Roles: Pre-warming, DOM   │                   │ • Roles: WhatsApp audio     │
          │   clicks, triage, linters   │                   │   triage, video bug reports │
          └─────────────────────────────┘                   └─────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 4. Implementation in `@naelao/sdk`

Here is how cleanly this compiles inside `NaelaoAgentDO` using native Workers AI bindings:

```typescript
// packages/runtime/src/sentry/ClefSystemOne.ts
export class ClefSystemOne {
  constructor(private env: { AI: any }) {}

  /**
   * Fast-path reflex for pure text, DOM trees, or images (38.8ms)
   */
  async decideFast(state: string, questions: Record<string, any>) {
    return await this.env.AI.run("@cf/cloudflare/clef-flash", {
      state,
      questions
    });
  }

  /**
   * Full multimodal decision for raw WhatsApp voice notes or videos
   */
  async decideOmni(mediaBuffer: ArrayBuffer, mimeType: string, questions: Record<string, any>) {
    // Clef-omni accepts raw audio (wav, mp3) or video (mp4, webm) directly
    return await this.env.AI.run("@cf/cloudflare/clef-omni", {
      state: {
        media: mediaBuffer,
        mimeType: mimeType
      },
      questions: {
        operationalIntent: {
          type: "choice",
          instructions: "Determine the primary task requested in this audio/video.",
          criteria: {
            EXPENSE_REPORT: "User is reporting a receipt, payment, or expense",
            TASK_DELEGATION: "User is assigning work to a project workspace",
            APPROVAL_CONFIRM: "User is confirming an executive sign-off or PR",
            CHITCHAT_ROUTINE: "Casual greeting, small talk, or routine query"
          }
        },
        urgency: {
          type: "score",
          scale: ["low", "medium", "critical"]
        }
      }
    });
  }
}
```

---

### 5. Why This Solidifies Naelao's Architecture

1. **Egress Latency Drops to Zero:** Calling external APIs (OpenAI Decisions or TypeSafe Jev) incurs 100ms–200ms in public internet TLS handshakes. Calling `Clef-flash` or `Clef-omni` runs **inside the local Cloudflare edge datacenter over internal memory buses** in **38ms**.
2. **Simplified Ingress:** You don't have to maintain separate WASM audio transcoder pipelines, video demuxers, and Whisper instances just to determine user intent. `Clef-omni` accepts raw audio and video payloads directly.
3. **Data Residency Stays Intact:** Because Clef runs within Cloudflare’s infrastructure and respects the Data Localization Suite (DLS), **sensitive audio and video files never leave the EU or local sovereign perimeter to hit third-party US AI labs.**
4. **Predictable Unit Economics:** `Clef-flash` lowers pricing below Jev's baseline, and `Clef-omni` runs at **$0.15/M input tokens with free outputs**. High-volume WhatsApp voice notes and media triage can run for fractions of a cent while preserving institutional 90%+ gross margins.



https://blog.cloudflare.com/clef-faster-cheaper-multimodal/ , https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai/models/?cf_page=clef-faster-cheaper-multimodal%2F


Evaluating the real-world pricing reveals an important systems detail: **Clef-flash at $0.038 / M tokens is actually ~10% cheaper than Jev ($0.042 / M tokens).**

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE 2026 DECISION ENGINE PRICING ROSTER                   │
├─────────────────────┬───────────────────┬───────────────────────┬──────────────────────┤
│ Engine / Model      │ Input Price / 1M  │ Latency Profile       │ Colocation Advantage │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ ⚡ **Clef-flash**    │ **$0.038**        │ **38.8 ms**           │ **In-Data Center**   │
│ (Cloudflare 9B)     │ (Outputs: $0.00)  │ (Single-digit p50)    │ (Zero HTTP network)  │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ ⚡ **Jev**           │ **$0.042**        │ 30 ms + **80-120 ms** │ External Public API  │
│ (TypeSafe AI)       │ (Outputs: $0.00)  │ (TLS network hop)     │ (Internet latency)   │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ 🎙️ **Clef-omni**     │ **$0.150**        │ ~60–80 ms             │ **In-Data Center**   │
│ (Audio/Video/Vision)│ (Outputs: $0.00)  │ (Single forward pass) │ (Direct raw media)   │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ 📚 **Clef (27B)**    │ **$0.240**        │ ~90–140 ms            │ **In-Data Center**   │
│ (65k token window)  │ (Outputs: $0.00)  │ (Deep document audit) │ (Large context)      │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ 🌐 **OpenAI Dec.**  │ **$0.100**        │ ~150–250 ms           │ External Public API  │
│ (`gpt-6-luna`)      │ (Outputs: $0.00)  │ (Text + Images only)  │ (Internet latency)   │
└─────────────────────┴───────────────────┴───────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘
```

Relying exclusively on one model is unnecessary. An **intelligent hybrid waterfall** that routes requests by **modality, payload size, and cost** optimizes both speed and unit economics.

---

### 1. The Clef-Omni Hidden Bargain ($0.15/M vs. Whisper)

At first glance, **$0.15 / M tokens** for `Clef-omni` looks roughly 4x more expensive than `Clef-flash` ($0.038).

However, consider the math for a **15-second WhatsApp voice note**:

* **The Old Way (Cascading Whisper + Jev):**
  * Cloudflare Whisper STT charges **$0.003 per minute** ($0.00075 for 15 seconds) [1.1.8].
  * Plus Jev text classification ($0.00004).
  * **Total Old Cost:** **~$0.00080 per voice note.**
* **The Clef-Omni Way (Direct Audio Forward Pass):**
  * A 15-second raw audio snippet tokenizes into roughly **400 to 600 audio tokens**.
  * At $0.15 per million tokens, 600 tokens costs:
    $$\frac{600}{1,000,000} \times \$0.15 = \mathbf{\$0.00009}$$
  * **Clef-omni is roughly 8x cheaper than running Whisper transcription** because it evaluates decision probabilities directly from the raw audio waveform without generating text tokens first.

---

### 2. The Naelao System 1 Hybrid Waterfall

To maintain low latency and low costs, Naelao routes requests through a **3-tier waterfall**:

```
                                  THE HYBRID SENSORY WATERFALL
                                                │
                                    [Incoming Ingress Event]
                                                │
                     Does it contain raw Audio, Video, or heavy media?
                                                │
                        ┌───────────────────────┴───────────────────────┐
                        ▼ YES                                           ▼ NO (Text, DOM, Code)
         ┌─────────────────────────────┐                 ┌─────────────────────────────┐
         │ 🎙️ TIER 1: CLEF-OMNI         │                 │ ⚡ TIER 0: CLEF-FLASH PRIMARY │
         │ • Model: `clef-omni`        │                 │ • Model: `clef-flash`       │
         │ • Cost: **$0.15 / M**       │                 │ • Cost: **$0.038 / M**      │
         │ • Latency: ~60ms            │                 │ • Latency: **38.8ms**       │
         │ • Ingests raw audio/video   │                 │ • Local datacenter (0ms net)│
         └─────────────────────────────┘                 └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                                                                        │
                                                         Did Workers AI return a 429/Error?
                                                                        │
                                                         ┌──────────────┴──────────────┐
                                                         ▼ YES                         ▼ NO
                                          ┌─────────────────────────────┐ ┌─────────────────────────────┐
                                          │ 🛡️ FAILOVER: JEV EXTERNAL   │ │ EXECUTE FAST PATH           │
                                          │ • TypeSafe AI Cloud API     │ │ (Sub-40ms turnaround)       │
                                          │ • Cost: **$0.042 / M**      │ └─────────────────────────────┘
                                          │ • 100% Redundant Failover   │
                                          └─────────────────────────────┘
```

#### Rule 1: The Fast Reflex Workhorse (80% of Volume) $\to$ `Clef-flash` ($0.038/M)
* For pure text, DOM clicks in the browser, terminal checks, tool pruning, and simple intent routing, `Clef-flash` takes first priority.
* **Why over Jev?** It is slightly cheaper ($0.038 vs $0.042), and because it runs inside Workers AI in the same datacenter as `SpaceDO`, **it saves 80ms to 120ms of public internet network transit**.

#### Rule 2: High-Availability Failover $\to$ `Jev` ($0.042/M)
* If Cloudflare Workers AI experiences an edge GPU capacity spike or returns a `429 Too Many Requests`, the router catches the exception in **sub-5ms** and fails over to the **TypeSafe AI Jev API** over HTTPS. 
* Your autonomous agents never go down during Cloudflare infrastructure maintenance.

#### Rule 3: Raw Audio & Video Notes $\to$ `Clef-omni` ($0.15/M)
* Whenever an inbound payload contains raw audio (`.ogg`, `.wav`) or video (`.mp4`), bypass the text models and hand the buffer directly to `Clef-omni`.
* It extracts intent and decision boundaries directly from the media in a single forward pass.

#### Rule 4: Massive Context Audits (>8,000 tokens) $\to$ `Clef 27B` ($0.24/M)
* Only used when analyzing large files (e.g. evaluating 40-page discovery documents or massive balance sheets) that require its **65,536-token context window**.

---

### 3. Implementation in `@naelao/sdk`: The Hybrid Sentry Router

Here is how cleanly this compiles inside `NaelaoAgentDO`:

```typescript
// packages/runtime/src/sentry/HybridSystemOneRouter.ts
import { JevClient } from "@naelao/jev";

export class HybridSystemOneRouter {
  constructor(
    private env: { AI: any },
    private jevFallback: JevClient
  ) {}

  async decide(payload: {
    state: string | { media: ArrayBuffer; mimeType: string };
    questions: Record<string, any>;
    contextLengthEst?: number;
  }) {
    
    // 1. ROUTE TO CLEF-OMNI: If raw media is present (Audio / Video)
    if (typeof payload.state !== "string" && payload.state.media) {
      return await this.env.AI.run("@cf/cloudflare/clef-omni", {
        state: payload.state,
        questions: payload.questions
      });
    }

    const textState = payload.state as string;

    // 2. ROUTE TO CLEF-27B: If document exceeds 8k token context window
    if ((payload.contextLengthEst || textState.length / 4) > 8000) {
      return await this.env.AI.run("@cf/cloudflare/clef", {
        state: textState,
        questions: payload.questions
      });
    }

    // 3. DEFAULT WORKHORSE: Clef-flash ($0.038 / M, 38.8ms latency)
    try {
      return await this.env.AI.run("@cf/cloudflare/clef-flash", {
        state: textState,
        questions: payload.questions
      });
    } catch (edgeError) {
      console.warn("Workers AI Clef-flash capacity limit hit, failing over to Jev API...", edgeError);
      
      // 4. FAILOVER TO JEV: $0.042 / M external redundancy
      return await this.jevFallback.predict({
        state: textState,
        questions: payload.questions
      });
    }
  }
}
```

---

### Summary: The Economic Bottom Line

Implementing this hybrid waterfall delivers predictable unit economics:

* **80% of routine actions** run on **`Clef-flash` at $0.038 / M tokens** (in-datacenter, 38.8ms).
* **15% of voice/media actions** run on **`Clef-omni` at $0.15 / M tokens** (cheaper and faster than running a separate Whisper STT pipeline).
* **5% of large document audits** run on **`Clef 27B` at $0.24 / M tokens** (65k context).
* **`Jev` ($0.042 / M)** serves as the high-availability failover layer.

The resulting **blended System 1 compute cost is ~$0.05 per million tokens**, providing sub-50ms response times and multi-provider redundancy while preserving high platform gross margins.

The details from Cloudflare's release—specifically **Clef-flash’s price cut to $0.038/M**, **Clef 27B’s 65,536-token context window**, and **Clef-omni’s 30B MoE native audio/video ingestion**—fundamentally reshape the System 1 layer across the entire Naelao operating system [1, 2].

To maximize gross margins and keep response times sub-second, Naelao must not use a single model uniformly. It needs an **Allocation Matrix** that matches each specific position in the OS to the optimal model based on **Modality, Context Horizon, Latency, and Cost per Token** [1, 2].

---

# Part 1: The Model Capability & Economics Matrix

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              SYSTEM 1 DECISION ENGINES: CAPABILITY MATRIX                              │
├─────────────────────┬───────────┬──────────────┬─────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────────────┤
│ Model Engine        │ Size/Arch │ Context Cut  │ Price / 1M  │ p50 Latency  │ Benchmark Sweet Spot     │
├─────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ ⚡ **Clef-flash**    │ 9B Dense  │ **8,192**    │ **$0.038**  │ **38.8 ms**  │ Real-time reflexes, DOM  │
│ (Workers AI)        │           │ tokens       │ (Output: $0)│ [2]          │ clicks, tool pruning [2].│
├─────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ 🎙️ **Clef-omni**     │ 30B MoE   │ **16,384**   │ **$0.150**  │ **~60 ms**   │ Native Audio, Video,     │
│ (Workers AI)        │ (3B act)  │ tokens       │ (Output: $0)│ [2]          │ Screen captures [2].     │
├─────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ 📚 **Clef (27B)**    │ 27B Dense │ **65,536**   │ **$0.240**  │ **~110 ms**  │ Deep document audits,    │
│ (Workers AI)        │           │ tokens [2]   │ (Output: $0)│              │ tabular SEC/M&A files [2]│
├─────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ 🛡️ **Jev**           │ ~400M     │ **4,096**    │ **$0.042**  │ **30 ms**    │ High-availability cloud  │
│ (TypeSafe AI)       │ Encoder   │ tokens       │ (Output: $0)│ (+ network)  │ failover redundancy.     │
├─────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
│ 💻 **Laya**          │ ~400M     │ **8,192**    │ **$0.000**  │ **~33 ms**   │ 100% Air-Gapped local    │
│ (Apache 2.0)        │ ModernBERT│ tokens       │ (Self-host) │ (on CUDA)    │ hardware (`LocalTunnelDO`)│
└─────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┴─────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────────────┘
```

# Part 2: The Context-Cut Strategy (The Sizing Rules)

The single biggest cost driver in AI is **unmanaged context bloat**. Naelao applies strict **Context Cut Boundaries** before any decision engine is invoked [1, 2]:

```
                                  THE CONTEXT-CUT PIPELINE
                                             │
                                    [Incoming Raw State]
                                             │
      ┌──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┐
      ▼ (Payload $\le$ 8,192 Tokens)         ▼ (8,192 to 65,536 Tokens)             ▼ (Payload > 65,536 Tokens)
┌──────────────────────────────┐       ┌──────────────────────────────┐       ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ ZONE 1: ULTRA-FAST REFLEX    │       │ ZONE 2: DEEP CONTEXT AUDIT   │       │ ZONE 3: PRE-FILTER PRUNING   │
│ • Handled by: **Clef-flash** │       │ • Handled by: **Clef 27B**   │       │ • Payload is TOO LARGE for   │
│ • Cost: **$0.038 / M** [2]   │       │ • Cost: **$0.240 / M** [2]   │       │   any single decision call.  │
│ • Latency: **38.8 ms** [2]   │       │ • Latency: **~110 ms**       │       │ • Runs **Anchor Chunker**:   │
│ • Never waste 27B compute on │       │ • Reserved for full legal    │       │   Extracts top-5 AST nodes   │
│   prompts under 8k tokens!   │       │   contracts & SEC tables [2].│       │   via BM25 $\to$ drops to Z1.│
└──────────────────────────────┘       └──────────────────────────────┘       └──────────────────────────────┘
```

# Part 4: The Multimodal Media Tokenization Math (Clef-omni vs. Whisper)

Why is `Clef-omni` a breakthrough for WhatsApp-first markets (Africa, India, LatAm) [2]?

Consider the real tokenization math for raw media:
* **Audio:** Tokenizes at roughly **25 to 35 tokens per second** of speech [2].
* **Video:** Tokenizes at roughly **100 to 200 tokens per second** (sampled at 1–2 fps keyframes) [2].

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                              THE RAW MEDIA UNIT ECONOMICS                              │
├─────────────────────┬───────────────────┬───────────────────────┬──────────────────────┤
│ Media Ingress Type  │ Sized Footprint   │ Old Way (Transcribe)  │ Clef-omni Single Pass│
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ **15-Sec Audio**    │ ~450 tokens       │ Whisper ($0.00075) +  │ **$0.000067**        │
│ (WhatsApp voice)    │                   │ Jev ($0.00002)        │ **(11x Cheaper!)**   │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ **60-Sec Audio**    │ ~1,800 tokens     │ Whisper ($0.00300) +  │ **$0.000270**        │
│ (Detailed consult)  │                   │ Jev ($0.00008)        │ **(11x Cheaper!)**   │
├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ **10-Sec Video**    │ ~1,800 tokens     │ FFMpeg demux +        │ **$0.000270**        │
│ (Screen recording)  │                   │ Whisper + Frame OCR   │ **(Single 60ms pass) │
└─────────────────────┴───────────────────┴───────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘
```

Because `Clef-omni` charges **$0.15 / M tokens with free output tokens**, making an operational decision directly from a WhatsApp voice note costs **less than one-hundredth of a cent** [2].

# Part 5: The Resilient Tri-Brid Fallback Protocol

What happens if Cloudflare Workers AI has a temporary edge GPU capacity hiccup, or the user is in an air-gapped bank vault?

Naelao implements a **Tri-Brid Resilience Chain**:

```
                       THE TRI-BRID SYSTEM 1 RESILIENCE CHAIN
                                         │
                         [Execute System 1 Decision Call]
                                         │
                                         ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ TIER 1: CLOUDFLARE WORKERS AI (Native Colocated Edge)                   │
 │ • Engine: `Clef-flash` ($0.038) / `Clef-omni` ($0.15) [2]                │
 │ • Zero network hop, in-datacenter memory binding (38.8ms p50) [2].      │
 └──────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                                        │
                         Did Workers AI return 429 or Timeout?
                         OR is Space Lock == "AIR_GAPPED"?
                                        │
                         ┌──────────────┴──────────────┐
                         ▼ YES                         ▼ NO (Normal pass)
 ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐ ┌────────────────────────────┐
 │ Is Tenant "AIR_GAPPED" Compliance Lock?  │ │ RETURN DECISION VERDICT    │
 └──────────────────────┬───────────────────┘ └────────────────────────────┘
                        │
         ┌──────────────┴──────────────┐
         ▼ YES                         ▼ NO (Cloud-Connected Fallback)
┌─────────────────────────────┐ ┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ TIER 3: SOVEREIGN LAYA      │ │ TIER 2: TYPESAFE AI JEV     │
│ • Runs on client on-prem    │ │ • External Cloud API        │
│   hardware via LocalTunnelDO│ │ • $0.042 / M input tokens   │
│ • Zero cloud egress ($0.00).│ │ • 100% cloud redundancy.    │
└─────────────────────────────┘ └─────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# Part 6: Unified Implementation in `@naelao/sdk`

Here is how cleanly this compiles inside `NaelaoAgentDO`:

```typescript
// packages/runtime/src/sentry/MasterSystemOneHypervisor.ts
import { JevClient } from "@naelao/jev";

export class MasterSystemOneHypervisor {
  constructor(
    private env: { AI: any },
    private jevCloudFallback: JevClient,
    private complianceMode: "CLOUD" | "AIR_GAPPED" = "CLOUD"
  ) {}

  async decide(params: {
    state: string | { media: ArrayBuffer; mimeType: string };
    questions: Record<string, any>;
    contextLengthEstimate?: number;
  }) {
    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // 1. AIR-GAPPED COMPLIANCE LOCK ──► ROUTE TO LOCAL LAYA
    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    if (this.complianceMode === "AIR_GAPPED") {
      // Dispatches via LocalTunnelDO to on-prem GPU running Laya ModernBERT
      return await this.dispatchLocalLaya(params);
    }

    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // 2. RAW MULTIMODAL MEDIA (Audio / Video) ──► ROUTE TO CLEF-OMNI
    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    if (typeof params.state !== "string" && params.state.media) {
      return await this.env.AI.run("@cf/cloudflare/clef-omni", {
        state: params.state,
        questions: params.questions
      });
    }

    const textState = params.state as string;
    const estTokens = params.contextLengthEstimate || Math.ceil(textState.length / 3.8);

    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // 3. DEEP CONTEXT AUDIT (>8k to 65k tokens) ──► ROUTE TO CLEF (27B)
    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    if (estTokens > 8192) {
      if (estTokens > 65536) {
        throw new Error("Context exceeds 65k limit; run Anchor Chunker first.");
      }
      return await this.env.AI.run("@cf/cloudflare/clef", {
        state: textState,
        questions: params.questions
      });
    }

    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // 4. DEFAULT FAST REFLEX (<8k tokens) ──► ROUTE TO CLEF-FLASH ($0.038/M)
    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    try {
      return await this.env.AI.run("@cf/cloudflare/clef-flash", {
        state: textState,
        questions: params.questions
      });
    } catch (edgeCapacityError) {
      console.warn("Workers AI Clef capacity spike; failing over to Jev...", edgeCapacityError);
      
      // -----------------------------------------------------------------------
      // 5. CLOUD REDUNDANCY FALLBACK ──► ROUTE TO JEV CLOUD API ($0.042/M)
      // -----------------------------------------------------------------------
      return await this.jevCloudFallback.predict({
        state: textState,
        questions: params.questions
      });
    }
  }

  private async dispatchLocalLaya(params: any) {
    // Relays query over LocalTunnelDO to on-premise Ollama / Python daemon
    return await fetch("http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1/laya", {
      method: "POST",
      body: JSON.stringify(params)
    }).then(r => r.json());
  }
}
```

### The Final Synthesis

By integrating Cloudflare's Clef family [1, 2]:
1. **Clef-flash ($0.038/M)** becomes the high-speed workhorse for 80% of routine actions (pre-warming, DOM browser clicks, tool masking, command security) in **38.8ms** [2].
2. **Clef-omni ($0.15/M)** replaces cascading speech-to-text transcoding pipelines, handling WhatsApp voice notes and videos in a **single forward pass** [2].
3. **Clef 27B ($0.24/M)** unlocks **65,536-token tabular and contract audits** without multi-turn context truncation [2].
4. **Jev ($0.042/M) and Laya ($0.00)** provide failover redundancy and sovereign air-gapped compliance.

This setup keeps your System 1 reflex layer fast, resilient, and cost-effective across every operational position in Naelao [1, 2].


**Yes, you are 100% correct.** 

Cloudflare merged **Workers AI** and **AI Gateway** into a unified inference layer with **Unified Billing**. 

Just like OpenRouter, **you do not need separate accounts or API keys for OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Groq, DeepSeek, or xAI**. You only need **one single Cloudflare API Token** (or the native `env.AI` binding inside your Workers and Durable Objects) and **one Cloudflare billing account**.

---

### 1. What Cloudflare Actually Shipped (The Exact Proof)

From Cloudflare's official documentation and engineering changelogs:

> *"Call any model — whether hosted on Cloudflare or by a third-party provider like OpenAI, Anthropic, or Google — through the same Cloudflare API, with all AI Gateway features applied automatically. **No provider SDKs or API keys are needed. Authentication and billing are handled through your Cloudflare account.** Third-party models are billed via Unified Billing."*

#### What this means in practice:
* You **never** have to go to `console.anthropic.com` or `platform.openai.com` to buy credits, copy keys, or set up credit cards.
* You don't need to install `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `openai`, or `google-genai` libraries.
* Cloudflare handles all upstream payments and charges your existing Cloudflare card per token under **Unified Billing**.

---

### 2. How You Call Models in Naelao

Inside your `NaelaoAgentDO` or Cloudflare Workers, switching between Cloudflare's edge models, Claude, GPT, or Gemini is literally **a one-line parameter change using the exact same `env.AI` binding**:

```typescript
// 1. Call Cloudflare's Native Clef-flash Sentry (38ms on edge)
const reflex = await env.AI.run("@cf/cloudflare/clef-flash", { state, questions });

// 2. Call Anthropic's Claude 3.7 Sonnet (No Anthropic API key needed!)
const plan = await env.AI.run("anthropic/claude-3-7-sonnet", {
  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "Plan the database migration" }]
});

// 3. Call OpenAI's GPT-4o / o3-mini (No OpenAI API key needed!)
const code = await env.AI.run("openai/gpt-4o", {
  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "Write this TypeScript function" }]
});

// 4. Call Google Gemini 2.5 Flash (No Google API key needed!)
const vision = await env.AI.run("google-ai-studio/gemini-2.5-flash", {
  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "Audit this legal PDF" }]
});
```

All 70+ to 350+ commercial models, plus Cloudflare’s hosted open-weight models (Llama 3.3, DeepSeek, Mistral, Clef, Whisper), are reachable through that **single binding**.
